# 🧠 DESIGN PRINCIPLES


These principles can help keep future features consistent with the central concept.


1. **Humanity remains meaningful.** The player's retained consciousness must matter in gameplay and story.
2. **Instinct creates tension.** Zombie behavior should influence decisions rather than exist only as decoration.
3. **Choices have context.** Important decisions should present understandable stakes.
4. **Systems should connect.** Crafting, exploration, social interactions, and survival should reinforce one another.
5. **Exploration rewards curiosity.** Locations should offer more than empty space.
6. **NPCs should feel responsive.** Characters should react to meaningful player actions.
7. **The interface should communicate clearly.** Visual effects must not obscure essential information.
8. **The game should remain maintainable.** Modular systems make future expansion easier.
9. **Accessibility matters.** Important information should not depend on color, sound, or a single input method.
10. **The world should tell stories.** Locations, objects, and encounters can communicate the history of the apocalypse.

- Link related documentation.
- Label proposed features as proposals.

Keep animations lightweight, provide descriptive alternative text, and avoid making essential information available only through motion.

Phoenix Blaze: The Cannabis System
"The Plant That Saved Humanity's Last Conscious Zombie"


In most games, cannabis would be a consumable.


In Phoenix Blaze, cannabis is:


Medicine
Agriculture
Crafting
Science
Economy
Therapy
Identity
Research
Politics
Survival


And most importantly:


It is the reason Ember Vale still has a conscience.


The entire game can be viewed as a giant cannabis ecosystem connected to every major pillar of gameplay.


The Origin Of Cannabis In Phoenix Blaze


Before the collapse, Ember was not a soldier.


She was not a scientist working for the military.


She was a master cultivator, geneticist, and medical cannabis researcher.


Project Phoenix originally focused on creating strains capable of supporting neural repair and emotional regulation.


After infection:


Those same strains became the first effective bloodlust suppressant.


The plant that once treated anxiety now helps prevent humanity from disappearing.


The Cannabis Gameplay Loop


The complete loop looks like this:


Discover Genetics ↓ Collect Seeds ↓ Clone Plants ↓ Build Grow Facilities ↓ Control Environment ↓ Breed Strains ↓ Harvest Plants ↓ Dry Product ↓ Cure Product ↓ Process Product ↓ Craft Items ↓ Suppress Bloodlust ↓ Trade Excess Product ↓ Fund Expansion ↓ Research New Genetics


Repeat.


Cannabis Genetics System


Every strain has hidden genetic values.


THC


Represents psychoactive potential.


Effects:


Mood changes
Bloodlust reduction
Euphoria
Pain reduction


High THC strains may provide strong suppression while creating focus penalties.


CBD


Represents calming effects.


Effects:


Bloodlust stabilization
Reduced aggression
Reduced stress
Better sleep


Extremely valuable to conscious infected.


CBG


Rare cannabinoid.


Effects:


Neural stabilization
Improved cognitive function
Reduced decay


Important in cure-path research.


CBC


Can influence:


Healing
Emotional balance
Recovery
Terpenes


Terpenes determine personality.


Examples:


Myrcene


Improves calmness.


Limonene


Improves mood.


Pinene


Improves focus.


Caryophyllene


Improves pain resistance.


Linalool


Improves sleep quality.


Seed Collection System


Seeds are found throughout the Ashlands.


Common Seeds


Simple genetics.


Reliable cultivation.


Heritage Seeds


Pre-collapse genetics.


Highly prized.


Rare Mutated Genetics


Created after the outbreak.


Found only in dangerous regions.


Legendary Genetics


Unique one-of-a-kind seeds.


May exist only once in the entire world.


Clone System


Not every crop starts from seed.


Players can clone elite genetics.


Benefits:


Consistency
Predictable quality
Commercial production


High-end settlements depend on cloning systems.


Growing Environment System


Growing quality determines final product quality.


Outdoor Farming


Advantages:


Cheap
Natural
Large yields


Disadvantages:


Weather dependent
Pest pressure
Security issues
Greenhouse Growing


Middle tier.


More consistent.


Better quality.


Requires infrastructure.


Indoor Growing


Highest control.


Player manages:


Light schedules
Ventilation
Humidity
Temperature
Air circulation


Produces elite genetics.


Consumes large amounts of power.


Soil System


Different soils produce different outcomes.


Forest Soil


Balanced growth.


River Soil


Excellent yields.


Wetland Soil


Unique mutations.


Engineered Soil


Late game cultivation.


Maximum performance.


Nutrient System


Plants require nutrients.


Primary nutrients:


Nitrogen
Phosphorus
Potassium


Secondary nutrients:


Calcium
Magnesium
Sulfur


Micronutrients affect:


Potency
Flavor
Yield

Water

Slow growth.


Too much:


Root damage.


Disease.


Lighting


Controls development stages.


Temperature

Humidity


Influences disease risk.

Protects plants.

Pest System


Plants face biological threats.


Examples:


Mites
Mold
Mildew
Fungal infections


Players must:


Prevent
Treat

Mutation System


Exposure to infected regions may alter genetics.


Possible results:


Increased potency.


New terpene profiles.


Bloodlust suppression improvements.


Enhanced medicinal effects.


Unexpected traits.


Harvest System


Harvest timing matters.


Early Harvest


Benefits:


Faster turnaround


Costs:


Lower potency


Late Harvest


Benefits:


Maximum potency


Costs:


Smaller margins for error

Fresh cannabis must be dried.


Improper drying destroys quality.


Variables:


Airflow
Humidity
Temperature
Drying time
Curing System


Very important.


Many survival games would ignore curing.


Phoenix Blaze shouldn't.


Long cure:


Better effects
Better value
Better medicinal properties


Poor cure:


Reduced effectiveness
Reduced trade value
Processing System


Harvests become ingredients.


Flower


Raw product.


Kief


Collected trichomes.


Concentrated material.


Hash


Compressed concentrate.


Higher potency.


Oils


Medical applications.


Tinctures


Precise dosing.


Ideal for bloodlust management.


Edibles


Long duration.


Slow onset.


Excellent before long expeditions.


Salves


Medical applications.


Treat pain and injuries.


Consumption Methods


Different methods create different effects.


Joint


Quick relief.


Portable.


Short duration.


Pipe


Efficient.


Reliable.


Bong


Greater bloodlust reduction.


Longer suppression window.


More resource consumption.


Vaporizer


Stealth consumption.


Minimal odor.


Excellent for infiltration missions.


Edibles


Longest duration.


Best sustained control.


Medicinal Dosing


Allows precise bloodlust stabilization.


Popular among advanced players.


Bloodlust Suppression Science


The most important gameplay function.


Every strain interacts differently with:


Bloodlust
Stress
Humanity
Sleep
Aggression
Focus


No single strain is perfect.


Players must design genetics for specific problems.

Discover:

Reduce bloodlust.

Reduce visible decay.

Improve recovery.

Enhance research.

Reduce suspicion-related symptoms.

Combinations of multiple goals.

Production

Late game transforms cannabis operations into industries.


Build:


Farms
Greenhouses
Extraction facilities
Genetics laboratories
Research centers
Seed vaults


Entire settlements may exist around cultivation.


Legendary Strains


Examples:


Phoenix Flame


Ember's original genetics.


Exceptional bloodlust control.


Humanitas


Supports humanity retention.


Ghost Veil


Improves zombie blending.


Serenity Bloom


Best stress reduction in the game.


Eden Prime


Essential for advanced cure research.


The Ultimate Cannabis Fantasy


In Phoenix Blaze the cannabis system is not a side activity.


It is a complete progression path.


A player can become:


Master Cultivator
Genetic Scientist
Medical Researcher
Settlement Provider
Industrial Producer
Hybrid Biologist
Savior Of Conscious Infected


The plant becomes the thread connecting survival, science, medicine, economy, politics, relationships, civilization rebuilding, and Ember's personal fight to remain human.


# Phoenix Blaze: A Conscious Zombie Survival Game


## Overview
**Phoenix Blaze** is an innovative HTML5-based open-world sandbox survival game where you play as a newly-turned zombie who retains human consciousness. Manage your bloodlust with cannabis-based crafting, blend in with both humans and zombies, and navigate a post-apocalyptic world of survival, moral choices, and self-discovery.


### Core Features
- 🧟 **Play as a Conscious Zombie**: Retain fragments of humanity while battling zombie instincts
- 🍃 **Cannabis Integration**: Craft marijuana-based items to suppress bloodlust and enhance abilities
- 🎭 **Stealth & Disguise**: Blend in with humans or mimic zombie behavior
- 🔨 **Deep Crafting System**: Create tools, weapons, shelters, and survival gear from gathered materials
- 🌍 **Open-World Exploration**: Semi-open world with 6-8 diverse regions
- 🤖 **AI-Driven NPCs**: Dynamic interactions with human survivors and other zombies
- 🎮 **Moral Decision-Making**: Branching storylines based on player choices
- 🔞 **Mature Content**: NSFW character customization and relationship mechanics


## Project Structure
```
zombie/
├── src/
│   ├── game/
│   │   ├── core/           # Core game engine
│   │   ├── systems/        # Game systems (crafting, combat, AI)
│   │   ├── entities/       # Player, enemies, NPCs
│   │   └── ui/             # HUD, menus, inventory
│   ├── assets/             # Sprites, audio, configs
│   ├── utils/              # Helper functions
│   └── index.html          # Main entry point
├── docs/                   # Documentation
├── tests/                  # Unit and integration tests
├── config/                 # Configuration files
├── package.json
└── README.md
```


## Technology Stack
- **Framework**: Phaser 3 (2D game engine for web)
- **Language**: JavaScript (ES6+)
- **Build Tool**: Webpack/Parcel
- **Testing**: Jest
- **Version Control**: Git


## Getting Started


### Prerequisites
- Node.js (v14+)
- npm or yarn


### Installation
```bash
# Clone the repository
git clone https://github.com/reeferguyai/zombie.git
cd zombie


# Install dependencies
npm install


# Start development server
npm run dev


# Build for production
npm run build
```

## Contact
- **Developer**: @reeferguyai
- **GitHub**: [reeferguyai/zombie](https://github.com/reeferguyai/zombie)




This is a strong high-level game concept. The most distinctive part is not the zombie survival itself, but the identity conflict mechanic: a player who is technically undead yet still conscious and trying to preserve their humanity. That gives Phoenix Blaze a unique hook that separates it from most survival and zombie titles.


What Already Works Well
Unique Core Pillar


The strongest design pillar is:


"You are a zombie trying not to become a monster."


Almost every major system supports that idea:


Bloodlust management
Human disguise
Zombie camouflage
Moral choices
Cannabis-based suppression mechanics
Relationship systems
Branching story outcomes


That creates a clear gameplay loop.


Natural Gameplay Loop


A possible core loop could be:


Explore world
Gather resources
Avoid detection
Craft suppression items
Manage bloodlust
Interact with humans/zombies
Make moral decisions
Unlock new regions
Repeat with escalating stakes


This is a solid survival-sandbox framework.


Biggest Opportunities
1. Bloodlust Should Be The Main Survival Mechanic


Instead of hunger, thirst, and seventy different survival meters, make bloodlust the central mechanic.


Example:


State        EffectsControlled        Think clearly
Irritated        Reduced social options
Unstable        Increased aggression
Frenzied        Temporary loss of control
Feral        NPCs and player behavior altered


This becomes the defining system of the game.


2. Cannabis Should Have Tradeoffs


Rather than simply reducing bloodlust:


Strains


Indica


Reduces cravings
Slower movement


Sativa


Faster movement
Increased detection


Hybrid


Balanced effects


Rare Mutant Strains


Temporary zombie control
Enhanced smell
Night vision
Human-passing effects


This creates meaningful crafting choices instead of a simple consumable.


3. Factions


The world would feel more alive with competing groups.


Survivors
Hunters
Settlers
Traders
Scientists
Zombies


Ferals


Mindless


Stalkers


Ambush predators


Conscious Zombies


Rare individuals like the player


Hive Cult


Accepts the infection as evolution
Regions


A six-region structure works well:


Safe Haven


Tutorial area


Suburban Ruins


Beginner crafting


Downtown Dead Zone


Dense loot


Industrial Sector


Advanced materials


Forest Wilderness


Natural resources


Military Quarantine Zone


Late-game technology


Research Complex


Story revelations


Underground Network


Endgame faction hub

Character Progression


Rather than traditional RPG levels:


Humanity


How human you remain

This creates multiple playstyles.

Phoenix Blaze
Complete Game Architecture, Bloodlust System, Supporting Systems & Core Gameplay Loop


The heart of Phoenix Blaze is not survival.


It is:


A conscious zombie struggling to remain human in a world where neither humans nor zombies fully accept them.


Everything in the game should reinforce this identity conflict.


CORE GAME PILLARS
Pillar 1: Humanity vs Bloodlust


The player's constant internal battle.


Pillar 2: Survival Through Adaptation


Scavenge, craft, build, and evolve.


Pillar 3: Social Camouflage


Blend with humans. Blend with zombies. Trust nobody.


Pillar 4: Freedom


Play how you want:


Survivor
Villain
Merchant

Pillar 5: Choice


Every decision affects the world.

THE BLOODLUST SYSTEM
The Most Important System In The Game


Bloodlust is constantly changing.


Scale:


0-100


0-20 Stable


Player looks mostly human.


Benefits:


Normal speech
Better stealth
Lower suspicion
21-40 Irritated


Zombie instincts begin.


Effects:


Minor aggression
Strange thoughts
Reduced patience
41-60 Unstable


Visible symptoms emerge.


Effects:


Tremors
Eye discoloration
Increased smell sensitivity


Humans notice.


61-80 Frenzied


Dangerous state.


Effects:


Random blood cravings
Hallucinations
Reduced dialogue options


Humans highly suspicious.


81-100 Feral


Loss of control.


Effects:


Partial player control loss
Forced combat urges
Visual distortion


Can trigger:


Feeding Events


Player attacks nearby targets.


BLOODLUST SUPPRESSION

Cannabis becomes essential medicine.


It slows neural degeneration and calms zombie instincts.

CORE GAME LOOP
Minute-to-Minute Loop


Explore → Scavenge → Manage Bloodlust → Gather Materials → Avoid Threats → Craft Equipment → Interact With NPCs → Return To Safehouse → Upgrade → Venture Further


Repeat


Mid-Game Loop


Explore New Region → Discover Resources → Unlock Recipes → Grow Cannabis → Suppress Bloodlust → Build Reputation → Expand Base → Complete Quests → Gain Influence


Repeat


Long-Term Loop


Survive → Choose Moral Path → Evolve Character → Join/Create Faction → Shape World Politics → Unlock Endgame Technologies → Determine Fate Of Humanity


Repeat Until Final Ending


The Real Gameplay Fantasy


Phoenix Blaze is ultimately:


Part survival game, part crafting simulator, part morality simulator, part zombie RPG.


The player is constantly asking:


"How much of my humanity am I willing to sacrifice to survive one more day?"


That question should drive every system in the game.




Phoenix Blaze
The Story of Ember Vale
Before The Fall


Long before she became known as Phoenix Blaze, she was simply Ember Vale.


Twenty-six years old.


A cannabis cultivator.


A botanist.


A dreamer.


She lived on the outskirts of a small mountain city, operating a legal craft cannabis farm known as Phoenix Grove, a place dedicated to medicinal genetics and sustainable cultivation.


Ember believed plants could heal almost anything.


Anxiety.


Trauma.


Addiction.


Pain.


She spent years crossbreeding unique strains designed to help veterans, cancer patients, and people suffering from severe PTSD.


Few people knew she had quietly become one of the top cannabis geneticists in the country.


Her greatest project was known as:


Project Phoenix


A strain unlike any before it.


Designed to increase neural repair.


Reduce aggression.


Promote emotional regulation.


Potentially slow neurodegenerative diseases.


Then the outbreak happened.


The Outbreak


Nobody knows where the infection started.


Some believe it came from:


A military lab
A pharmaceutical experiment
A fungal mutation
A biological weapon


Every region tells a different story.


Within weeks:


Governments collapsed
Cities burned
Communications failed
Humanity fractured


Most people infected lost everything.


Their memories.


Their identities.


Their souls.


But not Ember.


Day Zero


The attack happened while attempting to evacuate researchers from Phoenix Grove.


While helping survivors onto transport helicopters, a newly infected victim bit Ember on the neck.


The wound should have killed her.


Everyone thought it had.


For three days she drifted between life and death.


Then she woke up.


Hungry.


Terrified.


Changed.


But conscious.


Somehow Project Phoenix had altered her brain chemistry enough to preserve fragments of her humanity through the transformation.


She became something impossible.


A zombie who still remembered who she was.


The Birth Of Phoenix Blaze


The first weeks were horrific.


Every second she fought urges:


To hunt
To kill
To feed


Whenever bloodlust took over she would suffer flashes of memories.


Birthdays.


Family.


Laughter.


The smell of plants growing in greenhouses.


Each feeding stole another piece of herself.


But then she discovered something extraordinary.


Her own cannabis research slowed the urges.


Not permanently.


Not completely.


But enough.


Just enough.


For her to think.


To remember.


To survive.


The survivors who witnessed her refusing to attack gave her a nickname.


Phoenix Blaze


The woman who burned in death and rose again.


Neither living nor dead.


A bridge between worlds.


Character Appearance
General Look


Phoenix Blaze is beautiful in a haunting, unnatural way.


She looks human at first glance.


Yet something always feels wrong.


Like a dream that doesn't quite make sense.


Height


5'8"


Athletic but lean.


Built by years of outdoor cultivation and physical labor.


Hair


Long copper-red hair.


Often tied into a rough survival braid.


Sunlight catches fiery orange highlights.


At night it appears almost black.


Eyes


The most unsettling feature.


During stable states:


Bright green
Intelligent
Human


As bloodlust rises:


Golden
Amber
Deep crimson


Near feral states:


Pupils disappear
Eyes glow faintly red


A clear warning to observant NPCs.


Skin


Very pale.


Almost porcelain.


Small traces of decay appear under stress.


Veins darken during bloodlust episodes.


As if something underneath is trying to crawl out.


Clothing Style


Practical survivor gear.


Favorite equipment:


Cultivator jacket
Utility belt
Arm guards
Greenhouse gloves
Combat boots


Many pieces are remnants from Phoenix Grove.


Personality


Ember is not a superhero.


She is exhausted.


Scared.


Often angry.


Yet she keeps moving.


Because every day she remains herself is a victory.


She constantly asks:


If I still care about people, am I truly a monster?


That question becomes the emotional center of the game.


The Land
The Ashlands


The world of Phoenix Blaze is called:


The Ashlands


A continent forever transformed by the Collapse.


Nature and ruin have merged into something both beautiful and terrifying.


Every region tells part of the apocalypse.


Region 1
Greenwood Basin


Starting Region


Once farmland.


Now overgrown wilderness.


Features:


Abandoned farms
Greenhouses
Streams
Wildlife
Small survivor camps


Relatively safe.


A place where players learn survival.


Region 2
Hollow Pines


Massive forests.


Fog rarely lifts.


Features:


Hidden cabins
Predator wildlife
Zombie nests
Natural resources


At night strange sounds echo through the trees.


Region 3
Iron Reach


An industrial wasteland.


Collapsed factories.


Rail systems.


Refineries.


Abandoned warehouses.


Prime scavenging territory.


Highly dangerous.


Region 4
Revenant City


The largest city in the game.


Millions died here.


Skyscrapers pierce the skyline like tombstones.


Roaming hordes fill streets below.


Contains:


Hospitals
Office towers
Underground transit
Research facilities


A treasure trove of technology.


Region 5
Crimson Wetlands


Mutated swamp ecosystem.


Cannabis strains grow wild here.


Features:


Rare medicinal plants
Giant insects
Toxic pools
Strange fungal colonies


Many players believe the outbreak originated here.


Region 6
Blackstone Mountains


Cold late-game territory.


Features:


Mining operations
Military bunkers
Secret facilities


Home to some of the strongest enemies in the world.


Region 7
Solar Scar


A massive desert created when power stations exploded.


Burned earth stretches for hundreds of miles.


Scavengers fight over:


Fuel
Solar technology
Water purification systems
Region 8
The Silent Coast


End-game region.


An enormous coastline where evacuation fleets once gathered.


Thousands of abandoned ships remain offshore.


Many secrets lie beneath the water.


Including answers about the infection itself.


The Feeling Of The World


The Ashlands should feel neither hopeless nor hopeful.


It should feel like a world trying to decide what it wants to become.


Flowers grow through cracked highways.


Cannabis fields thrive where cities once stood.


Birds nest atop military checkpoints.


Forests swallow abandoned suburbs.


Humanity has fallen.


Nature has adapted.


And Phoenix Blaze exists between those two futures.


Not fully alive.


Not fully dead.


Trying to discover whether she is the cure, the bridge, or the beginning of something entirely new.




Companion Animal System


One of the themes of Phoenix Blaze is that Ember is constantly trying to hold onto her humanity. Companion animals become one of the strongest emotional systems in the game because they can sense who she truly is beneath the infection.


Some animals fear her.


Some accept her.


A rare few become loyal companions.


Bond System


Every companion has:


Trust


How comfortable they are around you.


Loyalty


Whether they stay when danger appears.


Affection


Personal relationship level.


Training


Skills learned.


Fear


Affected by bloodlust episodes.


Companion Classes
Dogs


The most common companions.


Roles:


Tracking enemies
Hunting
Guarding safehouses
Finding loot
Alerting player to danger


Traits:


German Shepherd
Husky
Labrador
Mutts
Post-apocalyptic mixed breeds


Special Effect:


Dogs react to bloodlust before humans do.


Cats


Stealth companions.


Abilities:


Find small loot
Catch pests
Sense hidden areas
Reduce stress


Cats can curl up near Ember while she sleeps.


One of the few creatures completely unbothered by her condition.


Ravens


Highly intelligent.


Abilities:


Scout locations
Reveal enemies
Deliver messages
Find corpses


Can ride on Ember's shoulder.


Useful for recon.


Horses


Transportation companions.


Abilities:


Faster travel
Carry supplies
Pull carts


Late-game mounted combat.


Goats


Base animals.


Benefits:


Milk production
Farming support
Rural companion


Strangely resistant to zombie attacks.


Exotic Companions


Rare discoveries.


Examples:


Wolf


Requires earning trust.


Excellent protector.


Fox


Stealth specialist.


Loot detection.


Crow Swarm


Can eventually gather multiple birds.


Functions almost like a reconnaissance network.


Zombie Animal Variants


The infection affects animals differently.


Examples:


Zombie dogs
Mutated wolves
Infected deer
Corrupted birds


Some can be rehabilitated.


Some become companions.


Some become bosses.

The player may choose to play as:


Ember Vale
A custom character
Future unlockable conscious zombies

Mature Character Customization


For adult players, a mature-content option could allow for additional body customization settings.


This should remain focused on character realism, body diversity, immersion, and role-playing rather than explicit sexual content. Players can adjust physical features, scars, athletic builds, aging details, and other appearance traits to create unique survivor identities.


Infection Appearance System


Perhaps the most unique customization system.


As infection evolves:


Players choose how it manifests.


Examples:


Preserved


Almost human.


Requires constant maintenance.


Hybrid


Visible undead traits.


Balanced path.


Feral


Monster-like evolution.


Maximum zombie features.


Mutated


Rare evolutionary path.


Unlocks unique abilities.


Marijuana System


The marijuana system is not merely recreational.


For Phoenix Blaze it is essentially:


Medicine
Survival Tool
Economy
Crafting Profession
Story Mechanic


It becomes one of the defining features of the entire game.


Cannabis Genetics System


Every plant contains values for:


THC


Psychoactive potency.


CBD


Calming effects.


CBG


Neural stabilization.


Terpenes


Determine special bonuses.


Yield


Harvest quantity.


Resistance


Disease protection.


Growing Pipeline


Seed ↓ Clone ↓ Vegetative Growth ↓ Flowering ↓ Harvest ↓ Drying ↓ Curing ↓ Processing ↓ Consumption/Crafting/Selling


Equipment Progression
Primitive
Homemade pots
Outdoor plots
Rain collection
Intermediate
Greenhouses
Irrigation
Nutrient systems
Advanced
Solar-powered facilities
Climate control
Research laboratories
Endgame


Phoenix Labs


A massive cultivation operation capable of producing legendary strains.


Consumption Methods


Different methods affect bloodlust control differently.


Joint


Cheap.


Quick.


Short effect.


Pipe


Efficient.


Portable.


Moderate suppression.


Bong


Powerful relief.


Large suppression effect.


Uses more product.


Water Pipe


Cleaner delivery.


Longer-lasting effects.


Vaporizer


Advanced technology.


Minimal odor.


Excellent stealth choice.


Edibles


Slow activation.


Very long duration.


Useful before dangerous missions.


Tinctures


Medical-grade extracts.


Precise dosing.


Excellent for controlled bloodlust management.


Legendary Strains
Phoenix Flame


Ember's original genetics.


The rarest strain in the game.


Provides powerful bloodlust control while preserving humanity.


Ghost Veil


Masks zombie scent.


Improves infiltration.


Serenity Bloom


Maximum mental stability.


Gravewalker


Allows easier interaction with zombie hordes.


Eden's Breath


Extremely difficult to cultivate.


One of the key strains required for certain "cure" story paths.


The Ultimate Marijuana Gameplay Loop


Discover Genetics ↓ Gather Seeds ↓ Build Grow Space ↓ Cultivate Plants ↓ Create New Strains ↓ Process Harvest ↓ Craft Consumption Tools ↓ Manage Bloodlust ↓ Trade With Settlements ↓ Expand Operations ↓ Unlock New Genetics ↓ Repeat


The result is a system where cultivation is much more than farming. It becomes a core part of survival, identity, economy, and the struggle to remain human in a world where every day the infection is trying to take control.


Crafting System
"If You Can Find It, Salvage It, Learn It, Or Imagine It... You Can Probably Build It."


Crafting in Phoenix Blaze should feel closer to Project Zomboid + State of Decay + Fallout than a simple survival game's recipe menu.


The player isn't just crafting items.


They are rebuilding civilization while simultaneously holding their mind together.


Crafting Progression
Tier 1: Primitive Survival


The first few days after transformation.


Crafting consists of:


Sticks
Stones
Scrap metal
Rope
Duct tape
Cloth


Items:


Spears
Clubs
Stone knives
Campfires
Water collectors
Basic shelters
Primitive pipes


Everything feels desperate.


Tier 2: Improvised Technology


Player starts restoring abandoned equipment.


Can build:


Workbenches
Drying racks
Rain collectors
Greenhouses
Solar chargers


Items:


Bows
Crossbows
Metal tools
Grow systems
Makeshift armor
Tier 3: Professional Equipment


Now rebuilding old-world technology.


Items:


Advanced bongs
Vaporizers
Medical equipment
Power systems
Automated irrigation


Structures:


Workshops
Labs
Cultivation facilities
Tier 4: Phoenix Technology


End-game technology.


Player combines:


Biology
Zombie physiology
Cannabis research
Salvaged science


Creates:


Bloodlust suppressors
Cognitive stabilizers
Specialized strains
Human-zombie hybrid technologies
Blueprint Discovery


Recipes aren't simply unlocked through levels.


You learn by:


Reading


Books Magazines Scientific journals


Observation


See an item. Reverse engineer it.


Experimentation


Mix resources.


Sometimes discover completely new recipes.


Combat System
Combat Feels Brutal, Desperate, And Personal


Unlike superhero games.


You are dangerous.


But so is everything else.


Three Combat Styles
Human Style


The remnants of your training and intelligence.


Focus:


Weapons
Planning
Defense


Examples:


Spears
Pistols
Rifles
Bows
Traps


Very controlled.


Survivor Style


Improvised combat.


Examples:


Baseball bats
Fire axes
Pipes
Machetes


Scrappy and practical.


Zombie Style


Uses infection abilities.


Examples:


Claw attacks
Pounces
Bites
Enhanced strength


Extremely dangerous.


Raises bloodlust.


Combat Attributes
Strength


Melee damage.

Movement.

Climbing.

Stamina.


Awareness


Detection.


Critical hits.


Composure


Resistance to bloodlust spikes.


Bloodlust Combat System


The higher bloodlust becomes:


The stronger the player grows.


But the harder they are to control.


Stable State


100% player control.


Normal combat.


Elevated State


Enhanced strength.


Faster movement.


Minor visual distortions.


Frenzied State


Much stronger.


Blood vision appears.


Target fixation occurs.


Feral State


Near-monster status.


Massive power.


Risk of temporary loss of player agency.


Extremely dangerous.


Stealth System
The Most Unique System In The Entire Game


Most survival games only ask:


"Can the enemy see you?"


Phoenix Blaze asks:


"Who are you pretending to be?"
Human Stealth


The player attempts to appear human.


Requirements:


Controlled bloodlust
Clean appearance
Human clothing
Concealed mutations


If successful:


Trade freely
Access settlements
Receive quests
Zombie Stealth


The player pretends to be another infected.


Requires:


Zombie scent
Zombie posture
Controlled movement


If performed correctly:


A zombie horde may completely ignore you.


You can walk directly through dangerous areas.


Scent System


Every creature emits scent.


Humans:


Sweat
Blood
Food


Zombies smell these scents.


Player may craft:


Scent Masks


Hide human odors.


Zombie Repellents


Reduce encounters.


Zombie Attractants


Lure hordes elsewhere.


Sound System


Everything creates noise.


Weapons.


Doors.


Glass.


Animals.


Running.


Even breathing.


Players can use:


Sound traps
Decoys
Timed distractions


To manipulate both humans and zombies.


Light System


Darkness matters.


Night survival changes entirely.


Players can:


Extinguish lights
Hide in shadows
Create blackout zones


Some zombies hunt almost entirely through hearing.


Others use sight.


Playing As A Conscious Zombie
The Core Fantasy


Most zombie games ask:


"How do you survive zombies?"


Phoenix Blaze asks:


"How do you survive being one?"


Living Between Two Worlds


Humans fear you.


Zombies don't understand you.


Neither side fully accepts you.


The player is constantly trapped in-between.


Zombie Advantages


Because of the infection:


Enhanced Smell


Can detect:


Blood
Food
Survivors
Animals


Through walls.


Regeneration


Minor wounds recover quickly.


Reduced Disease Risk


Many illnesses no longer matter.


Night Adaptation


Superior night vision.


Fear Resistance


Less panic.


More composure under pressure.


Zombie Disadvantages


Decay must be managed.


Players need:


Hygiene
Medical supplies
Preservation treatments


To maintain a human appearance.


Social Suspicion


Humans observe:


Speech patterns
Eyes
Body temperature
Behavior


One mistake may expose you.


Hunger vs Bloodlust


A normal survivor worries about starvation.


Phoenix Blaze worries about becoming a monster.


The player's greatest threat is not death.


It is losing humanity.


The Daily Life Of A Conscious Zombie


A typical day might look like:


Wake Up → Inspect Bloodlust → Consume Suppressant → Check Grow Operation → Craft Supplies → Travel To Settlement → Trade As Human → Gather Information → Leave Before Suspicion Rises → Cross Zombie Territory → Blend Into Horde → Reach Objective → Scavenge Resources → Fight Only If Necessary → Return To Base → Harvest Crops → Upgrade Equipment → Manage Humanity → Sleep


And every day asks the same question:


Can you remain human long enough to build a future, or will survival slowly turn you into the very thing everyone fears?


That tension between human intelligence and zombie instinct is what makes Phoenix Blaze fundamentally different from a traditional survival game.




NSFW / Mature Content System


For Phoenix Blaze, a mature-content mode should focus on adult storytelling, relationships, emotional intimacy, body customization, and realistic survivor life, rather than explicit sexual gameplay.


The apocalypse is lonely.


People are scared.


Human connection becomes valuable.


When enabled, mature themes could include:


Mature Themes
Romantic relationships
Emotional attachment
Co-habitation
Jealousy
Trust
Betrayal
Family building
Survivor relationship drama
Relationship Depth


Every important NPC has:


Trust


Do they trust you?


Attraction


Are they interested in you?


Fear


Are they suspicious of your condition?


Loyalty


Will they stand by you?


Discovery


How much do they know about what you are?


The Invisible Tension


For Ember especially:


Many companions may slowly fall in love with her.


The problem is:


She's hiding a terrifying secret.


Every relationship becomes a balancing act between:


Honesty
Safety
Secrecy
Survival


The moment someone discovers she's infected can completely alter the relationship.

Shelters are the backbone of the game.


Unlike many survival games, the shelter is not simply storage.


It becomes:


Home
Headquarters

Laboratory
Community

The World Structure


The Ashlands feel massive.


Think:


The Last of Us + State of Decay + Project Zomboid + Red Dead Redemption's world design


Region: Greenwood Basin


Beginning zone.


Rolling hills.


Old farms.


Abandoned greenhouses.


Fewer zombies.


Teaches survival.


Region: Hollow Pines


Dense forests.


Fog-covered trails.


Old ranger stations.


Wolves.


Hidden communities.


The region feels mysterious and isolated.


Region: Iron Reach


Industrial corridor.


Factories.


Railyards.


Refineries.


Power plants.


One of the best places to obtain building materials.


Region: Revenant City


The largest city in the game.


Towering skyscrapers.


Dark subway tunnels.


Massive hordes.


Danger around every corner.


Thousands of hidden stories.


Region: Crimson Wetlands


Swamps.


Mutated ecosystems.


Strange cannabis phenotypes growing wild.


Mysterious biological phenomena.


Many rumors surround this region.


Region: Blackstone Mountains


Military installations.


Research bunkers.


Snow-covered peaks.


High-level enemies.


Many secrets about the outbreak.


Region: Solar Scar


A desert formed after catastrophic industrial disasters.


Dust storms.


Burned landscapes.


Fierce scavenger wars.


Valuable technology.


Region: The Silent Coast


The endgame region.


Huge abandoned ports.


Ship graveyards.


Offshore mysteries.


Potential answers about the origin of the infection.


Quest System


The game contains three major quest types.


Main Story Quests


Focused on:


Project Phoenix
Ember's past
The infection
Humanity's future


Questions include:


Why did Ember survive mentally?
Can the infection be cured?
Are conscious zombies evolving?
Faction Quests


Every faction has goals.


Examples:


The Rangers


Protect settlements.


The Iron Syndicate


Industrial scavengers.


The Cult of Ascension


Believe zombies are humanity's next evolution.


Phoenix Collective


Scientists seeking coexistence.


Dynamic Quests


Generated naturally.


Examples:


Missing survivor
Supply run
Escort mission
Animal rescue
Zombie nest removal
Settlement defense


No two playthroughs feel exactly the same.


NPC System
One Of The Most Complex Systems In The Game


NPCs should feel alive.


Each has:


Personality
Schedule
Occupation
Fears
Goals
Memories
Relationships
Survivor NPC Types
Farmers


Produce food.


Operate greenhouses.


Engineers


Repair technology.


Construct facilities.


Medics


Treat injuries.


Research infection.


Traders


Run supply routes.


Create economic networks.


Hunters


Track wildlife.


Protect settlements.


Scientists


Study the outbreak.


Can unlock late-game technologies.


Conscious Zombie NPCs


Extremely rare.


Each has:


Unique appearance
Distinct personality
Different coping strategies


Examples:


The Monk


Uses meditation to suppress bloodlust.


The Butcher


Feeds regularly but follows strict rules.


The Botanist


Uses plant science like Ember.


The Watcher


Lives among zombie hordes for years.


Knows secrets few people understand.


Major Story NPC Example
Dr. Elara Kane


Former Phoenix Grove researcher.


Believes Ember is the key to saving civilization.


Wants to find a cure.


Mason Voss


Mercenary leader.


Believes infected should be exterminated.


Would kill Ember if he discovered the truth.


Rhea Thorn


Leader of the Cult of Ascension.


Believes Ember is the first step of a superior species.


Wants her to embrace the infection fully.


What Makes Phoenix Blaze Different


Most zombie games are about:


"Survive the zombies."


Phoenix Blaze is about:


"Build a life while secretly being one."


You are not merely building shelters.


You are building a home.


You are not simply completing quests.


You are determining the future relationship between humans and the infected.


And every NPC, every settlement, every relationship, and every choice asks the same question:


If the world discovers what you truly are, will it see a monster... or a miracle?


The Fall of Civilization
What Created the Chaos?


The world did not end overnight.


It ended in stages.


That's what made it so terrifying.


At first, it wasn't called an outbreak.


It was called:


A Neurological Syndrome


People reported symptoms including:


Aggression
Emotional instability
Insomnia
Violent impulses
Memory problems


Governments treated it like a new disease.


Hospitals became overwhelmed.


Researchers raced for answers.


Project Lazarus


Years before the outbreak, a coalition of governments, pharmaceutical corporations, and military research divisions secretly funded an initiative known as:


Project Lazarus


The goal was noble.


They wanted to repair damaged brains.


Potential applications:


Alzheimer's
PTSD
Parkinson's
Brain injuries


Scientists engineered a microscopic neural-repair organism capable of rebuilding damaged neural pathways.


The problem?


It worked too well.


Instead of merely repairing the brain, it began rewriting it.


The Mutation


Eventually the organism mutated.


The infected stopped valuing human needs.


Their brains prioritized:


Feeding
Instinct
Survival
Aggression


Higher functions disappeared.


Empathy collapsed.


Most victims became what survivors simply called:


Zombies


Not undead.


Not magical.


They were biologically alive.


But mentally altered beyond recognition.


The Collapse


Within weeks:


Power grids failed.


Supply chains collapsed.


Cities burned.


Governments retreated.


Military forces fragmented.


Communications disappeared.


Small survivor communities formed.


The modern world died.


The Great Lie


Years later, nobody knows the full truth.


Every faction tells a different story.


Some believe:


It was a bioweapon.
It was corporate negligence.
It was natural mutation.
It was government experimentation.


The truth remains buried beneath ruined laboratories and military bunkers throughout the Ashlands.


Discovering that truth becomes one of the central mysteries of the game.


Why Ember Is Different


The thing that makes Ember extraordinary is that she should not exist.


Every other infected person experienced:


Neural Overwrite


The parasite consumed higher reasoning.


Their humanity vanished.


Their memories fragmented.


Their conscience disappeared.


Ember's brain reacted differently.


Years before the outbreak she had been exposed to experimental compounds and custom cannabinoid genetics she developed through Project Phoenix.


When she was infected:


Something unexpected happened.


The infection rebuilt her body.


But it only partially rewrote her mind.


Instead of becoming:


Human


or


Zombie


She became:


Both


A biological impossibility.


What She Actually Is


Ember isn't a human infected with zombie symptoms.


And she isn't a traditional zombie.


She is:


A Human-Zombie Hybrid


Part human.


Part infected.


Part something entirely new.


The Human Half


The human side still possesses:


Empathy


She feels guilt.


Love


She forms attachments.


Morality


She understands right and wrong.


Memory


She remembers her childhood.


Friends.


Her farm.


Her life.


Self Awareness


She knows what she has become.


And she fears it.


The Zombie Half


The infection grants powerful adaptations.


Enhanced Strength


Far stronger than normal humans.


Regeneration


Minor wounds heal rapidly.


Heightened Smell


Can detect:


Blood
Fear
Rot
Animals
Survivors


At extraordinary ranges.


Night Vision


Can navigate darkness more effectively.


Reduced Pain


Can keep fighting after injuries that would stop most humans.


The Internal War


The true battle is not against external enemies.


It happens inside her head.


Every day.


Every hour.


Every minute.


Two personalities constantly pull against one another.


Ember


The woman she used to be.


Compassionate.


Curious.


Hopeful.


Protective.


The Hunger


A separate presence created by infection.


Not a voice.


Not hallucinations.


More like a primal instinct.


An urge.


A pressure.


A need.


Constantly whispering:


Feed.


Hunt.


Consume.


The Bloodlust Mechanic


Bloodlust represents how much influence the infection currently has over Ember.


At low levels:


Ember dominates.


At high levels:


The infection dominates.


When bloodlust rises:


Colors become richer.


Heartbeats become louder.


The smell of living creatures becomes intoxicating.


Blood becomes impossible to ignore.


The player begins seeing the world through infected senses.


Living Between Species


One of the most interesting aspects of Phoenix Blaze is that Ember belongs nowhere.


Humans see a monster.


Zombies sense something strange.


Both sides are uncomfortable around her.


To humans:


She is infected.


Dangerous.


Unnatural.


To zombies:


She smells wrong.


Acts strangely.


Thinks too much.


As a result:


She spends the entire game existing between both worlds.


Never fully accepted by either.


Why This Is The Core Fantasy


Most zombie games ask:


"Can you survive the apocalypse?"


Phoenix Blaze asks:


"Can you survive becoming the thing everyone fears?"


The player isn't fighting to stay alive.


The player is fighting to stay themselves.


Every cannabis strain grown, every shelter built, every survivor helped, every bloodlust episode resisted, and every hard moral choice ultimately serves one question:


If your body has become a monster, what truly determines whether you are still human?


That question is the philosophical heart of Phoenix Blaze and the thread that ties every major system in the game together.










Movement System
"Move Like A Human. Hunt Like A Zombie."


Movement in Phoenix Blaze is one of the most important gameplay systems because movement itself affects:


Bloodlust
Suspicion
Stealth
Stamina
Combat
Survival


How you move tells the world who you are.


Human Movement


When Ember is controlling her human side, movement feels deliberate and athletic.


Walking


Default movement.


Benefits:


Low stamina use
Lower suspicion
Better awareness


Useful for entering settlements.


Jogging


Balanced travel speed.


Good for exploration.


Sprinting


Fast travel.


Costs:


Stamina
Noise generation


Humans notice.


Zombies hear.


Crouching


Reduces:


Visibility
Noise


Useful for infiltration.


Crawling


Slow but nearly silent.


Useful through:


Tall grass
Ruins
Ventilation systems
Tight spaces
Zombie Movement


As bloodlust rises, movement begins changing.


The player gains access to new behaviors.


Predator Walk


A controlled zombie gait.


Benefits:


Better zombie camouflage
Lower detection by infected
Lunge


Short burst attack.


Quickly closes distance.


Pounce


Leap onto enemies.


Excellent ambush ability.


Wall Scramble


Advanced infection ability.


Allows climbing certain structures faster than humans.


Rooftop Traversal


Late-game zombies become surprisingly mobile.


Can move:


Across rooftops
Through broken buildings
Along collapsed highways


Creating alternative navigation paths.


Parkour System


Ember was athletic before infection.


Movement gradually evolves into a light parkour system.


Actions include:


Vaulting


Jump over:


Fences
Barricades
Cars
Climbing


Scale:


Fire escapes
Walls
Towers
Sliding


Move under obstacles.


Hanging


Traverse ledges.


Traversal Progression


At the start of the game:


Movement is grounded.


Survival-oriented.


Later:


The infection unlocks enhanced mobility.


Players may have access to:


Higher jumps
Better climbing
Longer sprints
Increased endurance


The world gradually opens up.


Vehicle System


The world is huge.


Vehicles become important.


Bicycles


Early game.


Quiet.


Efficient.


Motorcycles


Fast.


Loud.


Fuel efficient.


Cars


Balanced transportation.


Trucks


Heavy hauling.


Mobile storage.


Armored Vehicles


Rare late-game vehicles.


Used during major combat operations.


Survival Systems
Everything Working Together


Most systems interact with one another.


No mechanic exists in isolation.


Hunger System


Even though infected, Ember still requires nutrition.


Poor nutrition affects:


Stamina
Healing
Morale
Hydration System


Water affects:


Endurance
Recovery
Heat resistance
Sleep System


Sleep remains critical.


Lack of sleep increases:


Hallucinations
Bloodlust spikes
Stress
Weather System


Dynamic weather creates new challenges.


Rain


Benefits:


Masks sound
Waters crops


Problems:


Reduced visibility
Mud
Thunderstorms


Can:


Frighten animals
Disrupt electronics
Conceal movement
Snow


Slows travel.


Creates tracks.


Heat Waves


Stress crops.


Dehydrate characters.


Reputation System


Every settlement remembers you.


Actions matter.


People remember:


Help received
Crimes committed
Promises broken
Lives saved
Economy System


Civilization may be broken.


Trade survives.


Currency becomes:


Food
Medicine
Seeds
Ammunition
Rare materials


Cannabis quickly becomes one of the most valuable trade resources.


Research System


Knowledge is progression.


Players discover:


New strains
Weapon blueprints
Infection science
Advanced technologies


Learning comes from:


Books
Computers
Research facilities
NPC mentors
Settlement Management


Player communities require leadership.


Needs include:


Food


Keep settlers fed.


Water


Maintain supplies.


Housing


Improve morale.


Security


Prevent attacks.


Medicine


Treat injuries.


Trade


Generate wealth.


Zombie Evolution System


The infection itself evolves.


Multiple pathways emerge.


Preserver Path


Remain as human as possible.


Focuses on:


Diplomacy
Relationships
Community
Predator Path


Embrace infection powers.


Focuses on:


Combat
Hunting
Mutation
Hybrid Path


Balance both worlds.


Most flexible route.


Sage Path


Research-focused.


Uses science and cultivation to master the infection.


Dynamic World Events


The world never stands still.


Examples:


Migrating Hordes


Entire zombie populations relocate.


Changing regional danger levels.


Settlement Attacks


Communities request assistance.


Crop Blights


Threaten food production.


Military Convoys


Rare opportunities for loot.


Radio Signals


Lead to discoveries.


Survivor Refugees


Create unexpected stories.


Endgame Systems


By the late game the player influences entire regions.


Can establish:


Trade routes
Settlements
Research networks
Faction alliances


May ultimately:


Discover a cure
Create peaceful coexistence
Establish conscious-zombie societies
Reshape the future of the Ashlands

Hidden Endgame Systems


For a truly massive version of Phoenix Blaze, you could also add:


Conscious Zombie Society System

Genetic Engineering System

Settlement Culture System

World Recovery System
Legacy Civilization System


If I were creating the full design bible for Phoenix Blaze, I'd ultimately organize the game into 12 master pillars:


Character & Identity
Bloodlust & Humanity
Survival
Combat
Stealth
Crafting & Cultivation
Exploration
Relationships
Settlements
Factions & Politics
Evolution & Research
Dynamic World Simulation


Everything else becomes a subsystem underneath those pillars. That structure would give you a complete AAA-scale game design architecture.








When all of these systems are combined, Phoenix Blaze becomes much more than a zombie survival game. It becomes a life-simulation RPG about existing between two species, building communities, managing a dangerous inner hunger, and deciding what humanity means when your body is no longer fully human.


Character Creator
"Who Were You Before The Fall? Who Are You Becoming Now?"


The Character Creator in Phoenix Blaze is not simply cosmetic. It defines how the world initially reacts to you and helps shape your survival journey.


Origin Selection


Before appearance customization, players choose an origin.

Powerful but terrifying.

Bloodlust System
The True Enemy


Bloodlust is not a resource.


It's a living pressure constantly trying to consume Ember's identity.


Bloodlust Meter


Scale:


0 - 100


0-20
Stable


Human mind dominates.


Benefits:


Full social access
Improved stealth
Better decision making
21-40
Agitated


The infection is awakening.


Symptoms:


Irritability
Enhanced senses
Minor aggression
41-60
Unstable


Visible symptoms emerge.


Effects:


Eye color shifts
Hand tremors
Increased cravings
61-80
Frenzied


The infection pushes constantly.


Effects:


Combat bonuses
Reduced social options
Hallucinations
81-100
Feral


The line between human and monster nearly disappears.


Massive power.


Major consequences.


Humanity System
What Makes You Human?


Humanity is separate from bloodlust.


Bloodlust is biology.


Humanity is morality.


Humanity Sources


Increase Humanity:


Help survivors
Rescue animals
Build communities
Resolve conflicts peacefully


Decrease Humanity:


Murder innocent people
Betray allies
Feed on survivors
Embrace cruel choices
Humanity Levels
High Humanity


Benefits:


Strong relationships
Trust
Heroic endings
Balanced Humanity


The gray area.


Most flexible path.


Low Humanity


Powerful mutation benefits.


Severe social consequences.


Cannabis Cultivation
The Heart Of Phoenix Grove


Cannabis is not a side mechanic.


It becomes one of the largest systems in the game.


Growing Cycle


Seed → Clone → Vegetative Growth → Flowering → Harvest → Drying → Curing → Processing


Environmental Controls


Players manage:


Water


Too little: Plants suffer.


Too much: Root damage.


Nutrients


Nitrogen


Phosphorus


Potassium


Micronutrients


Temperature


Affects potency.


Humidity


Controls plant health.


Light Cycles


Crucial for flowering.


Genetic Research


Players can create custom strains.


Example Traits:


Bloodlust suppression
Increased focus
Reduced decay
Better sleep
Enhanced regeneration
Companion Animals
Loyalty In A Broken World


Animals often become the player's most trustworthy allies.

Animals gain skills over time.


A rescued stray can eventually become one of the strongest companions in the game.

Shelter Customization
Every Shelter Tells A Story


A shelter is not just a base.

Relationship System
The Most Human System In The Entire Game


Relationships constantly remind the player what they are fighting to preserve.


Relationship Metrics


Every important NPC tracks:


Trust


Do they believe you?


Respect


Do they value you?


Fear


Are they afraid of you?


Affection


How emotionally connected are they?


Suspicion


Do they think you're infected?


Dynamic Relationships


Relationships evolve through:


Conversations
Quests
Gifts
Shared experiences
Major story decisions
Secrets Matter


The central relationship tension is:


Do you reveal what you truly are?


Some NPCs may:


Accept you
Fear you
Betray you
Protect you
Attempt to cure you
Want to use you


based on the answer.


Relationship Outcomes


Relationships can become:


Friendships


Trusted allies.


Partnerships


Co-builders of settlements.


Mentorships


Skill training and guidance.


Rivals


Constant opposition.


Enemies


Actively work against you.


Family-Like Bonds


The strongest connection in the game.


People who would risk everything for you after learning the truth.


Ultimately, these systems all feed into Phoenix Blaze's core theme:


You are not trying to survive the apocalypse.


You are trying to preserve your humanity while living as something that should no longer have one.


Every crop you grow, every shelter you build, every animal you save, every skill you learn, and every relationship you form becomes evidence that there is still a person underneath the infection.




Faction System
"The World Didn't Die. It Fractured."


After civilization collapsed, humanity splintered into dozens of competing ideologies. Every faction believes they know how the future should be rebuilt.


Your actions can strengthen, weaken, unite, infiltrate, or destroy them.


The Rangers


The closest thing to heroes.


Former park rangers, hunters, emergency responders, and community leaders.


Core Beliefs
Protect survivors
Preserve nature
Rebuild civilization
Specialties
Hunting
Tracking
Exploration
Settlement defense
Relationship With Ember


Initially friendly.


May become enemies if they discover infection.


Iron Syndicate


Industrial scavengers.


Operate from factories and rail yards.


Core Beliefs
Technology equals survival
Resources belong to the strong
Specialties
Engineering
Manufacturing
Vehicles
Weapons
Cult of Ascension


The most dangerous faction.


They worship mutation.


Core Beliefs


The infected are evolution.


Humanity is obsolete.


Goals
Accelerate infection
Create new species
Recruit conscious zombies


Many consider Ember a messiah.


Phoenix Collective


Former scientists.


Researchers.


Botanists.


Doctors.


Goals
Study Project Lazarus
Research a cure
Understand conscious infected


Potentially Ember's greatest allies.


Dynamic NPC Simulation
"Everyone Has A Life."


Every major NPC exists whether the player is present or not.


Daily Schedules


NPCs wake up.


Eat.


Work.


Sleep.


Travel.


Socialize.


Fight.


Trade.


Mourn losses.


Celebrate victories.


Dynamic Memories


NPCs remember:


Arguments
Favors
Lies
Betrayals
Heroic acts


If you save someone from a horde they remember.


If you abandon them they remember.


Years later.


NPC Relationships


NPCs form relationships independently.


Friendships.


Romances.


Rivalries.


Families.


The player is merely one participant in a living world.


NPC Career Growth


A scavenger may become:


Trader
Settlement leader
Faction officer


Their life progresses naturally.


Zombie Ecosystem
"The Dead Are An Ecosystem."


Zombies aren't random wandering enemies.


They form a living ecological system.


Roamers


Most common zombies.


Constantly migrating.


Travel along:


Highways
Rivers
Urban corridors
Feeders


Gather at locations containing:


Corpses
Animals
Recent battles
Nesters


Create dens inside:


Malls
Hospitals
Tunnels
Factories


Destroying nests changes local population patterns.


Migrating Hordes


Thousands of infected travel together.


A moving disaster.


Can wipe entire settlements from the map.


Alphas


Rare evolved infected.


Smart.


Dangerous.


Territorial.


Serve as regional bosses.


Conscious Infected


The rarest infected.


Like Ember.


Retain self-awareness.


Each has a completely different philosophy.

Infected deer

Giant swamp reptiles


Not supernatural.


Biologically altered by infection.

Exploration System
The Soul Of Phoenix Blaze


Exploration is built around curiosity, danger, and discovery.


Layers Of Discovery
Surface Discovery


Obvious exploration.


Roads.


Towns.


Forests.


Hidden Discovery


Secret:


Bunkers
Caves

Finding:


New cannabis genetics
Rare plants

Personal Discovery


Perhaps the most important.


As Ember travels farther into the Ashlands, she begins uncovering the truth about:


Project Lazarus
Project Phoenix
The origin of conscious infected
Her own transformation
Whether humanity can coexist with the infected


The deeper the player explores, the more they realize the apocalypse was not simply the end of the old world.


It was the beginning of a completely new one, and Ember stands directly between humanity's extinction and its next stage of evolution.




Main Story
"The Last Human Thought"


The main story begins with Ember waking up three days after being infected.


Everyone she knew believes she is dead.


Phoenix Grove is destroyed.


Civilization is collapsing.


And yet she can still think.


That should be impossible.


Act 1: Ashes


The player learns:


Basic survival
Bloodlust management
Crafting
Cannabis cultivation
Human disguise


Questions begin forming:


Why am I conscious?
Why am I different?
What was Project Phoenix?
Act 2: Between Worlds


Ember discovers:


Conscious infected exist
Project Lazarus was real
Factions are hunting outbreak secrets


The player begins choosing ideological paths.


Act 3: Evolution


The truth emerges.


The infection was never intended to create zombies.


Something changed.


Something evolved.


The conscious infected may represent a new branch of humanity.


Act 4: The Choice


The player ultimately determines the future:


Cure humanity
Preserve humanity
Merge humanity and infection
Create a conscious infected civilization
Destroy all competing factions
Side Quest System
"Everybody Has A Story"


Side quests reveal the human cost of the apocalypse.


Many are emotionally driven.


Survivor Stories


Examples:


Lost Family


Locate missing relatives.


Discover what happened to them.


Last Letter


Deliver a final message.


May change an entire settlement's future.


Empty Greenhouse


Restore a forgotten farm.


Unlocks rare genetics.


Companion Quests


Animals also have stories.


Rescue:


Injured dogs
Lost horses
Trapped wildlife


Sometimes unlock unique companions.


Conscious Infected Quests


Rare storylines involving:


Conscious zombies
Research subjects
Hidden communities


These often reveal deeper truths about the outbreak.


Legendary Gear
World-Changing Equipment


Legendary gear should feel meaningful.


Not simply stronger.


Phoenix Mask


Created from Project Phoenix technology.


Effects:


Reduces bloodlust growth
Improves human disguise
Ember's Cultivator Jacket


A restored version of Ember's original field jacket.


Provides:


Cultivation bonuses
Reputation bonuses
Story significance
The Green Reaper


Legendary hybrid weapon.


Part farming tool.


Part combat weapon.


Upgradeable throughout the game.


Project Lazarus Scanner


High-tech research tool.


Allows analysis of:


Mutations
Strains
Biological anomalies
The Last Seed Vault


A portable genetic archive.


Contains rare plant genetics.


One of the most valuable items in the world.


Mutation Progression
The Evolution Tree


The infection is constantly evolving.


Players choose how.


Branch 1: Preserver


Remain as human as possible.


Unlocks:


Social skills
Leadership
Lower suspicion
Branch 2: Predator


Become a highly efficient hunter.


Unlocks:


Enhanced combat
Tracking
Mobility
Branch 3: Symbiote


Balance both halves.


Hybrid abilities.


Most versatile path.


Branch 4: Ascended


Radical mutation.


Powerful biological changes.


Unique ending path.

Endings


The game should have many endings.


The Cure Ending


Humanity survives.


The infection is contained.


Ember sacrifices much to save others.


The Bridge Ending


Humans and conscious infected coexist.


A fragile peace emerges.


The Ascension Ending


The conscious infected become a new civilization.


Humanity slowly fades.


The Beast Ending


Bloodlust wins.


Ember becomes a legendary monster.


Feared across the Ashlands.


The Gardener Ending


Using cultivation, science, and cooperation, Ember creates a sustainable future where both nature and civilization recover.


Often considered the most hopeful ending.

New mutation options

World Carryover

Small mysteries change between playthroughs.

Multiplayer / Co-op
Surviving Together

Ultimate Vision


By the time all systems come together, Phoenix Blaze becomes far more than a zombie game.


It becomes:


A survival simulator
A settlement builder
A cultivation simulator
A relationship RPG
A faction strategy game
A dynamic world simulator
A morality system
A conscious-zombie experience


all connected by one central question:


Can you build a future worth living in while fighting the monster inside yourself every single day?






Movement & Traversal System
"The World Is Your Obstacle Course"


In Phoenix Blaze, movement is more than transportation.


Movement determines:


Stealth
Combat effectiveness
Bloodlust management
Social suspicion
Exploration opportunities
Mutation development


As Ember evolves, the world becomes increasingly traversable.


Walking


The default movement state.


Walking is deliberately important.


Benefits
Lowest stamina consumption
Lowest sound generation
Better environmental awareness
Improved NPC interaction


Humans expect humans to walk naturally.


Walking too aggressively or mechanically may increase suspicion.


Sprinting


Fast but costly.


Human Sprint


Controlled.


Athletic.


Useful during emergencies.


Costs:


Stamina
Hydration
Increased visibility
Zombie Sprint


Different animation style.


More animalistic.


More powerful.


Bloodlust rises faster.


Nearby humans may immediately recognize something is wrong.


Climbing


The apocalypse created a vertical world.


Players can climb:


Ladders
Fire escapes
Trees
Ruins
Construction scaffolding
Mountains
Human Climbing


Uses:


Strength
Endurance


Safer.


Slower.


Zombie Climbing


Uses:


Enhanced grip
Mutation abilities


Faster.


More dangerous.


Allows access to otherwise unreachable areas.


Crawling


Used for:


Tight spaces
Vents
Tall grass
Rubble fields


Benefits:


Minimal visibility
Minimal sound


Perfect for stealth infiltrations.


Vaulting


Fluid movement over small obstacles.


Examples:


Cars
Fences
Barricades
Windows


High agility improves vault speed.


Certain mutations can allow more aggressive vault attacks.


Swimming


Water becomes a major exploration system.


Players can cross:


Rivers
Lakes
Wetlands
Coastal zones
Human Swimming


Consumes stamina.


Affected by:


Equipment weight
Current strength
Water temperature
Zombie Adaptation


Some mutation paths increase:


Water endurance
Underwater movement
Breath control


Allowing unique exploration opportunities.

Gap crossing.


Balance Traversal

Sliding

Rope Ascension


A complete vertical traversal system.


Uses
Cliff climbing

Rope quality

Stealth System
"You Are Not Hiding. You Are Pretending."


The stealth system is arguably the most unique system in Phoenix Blaze.


Traditional stealth asks:


Can they see me?


Phoenix Blaze asks:


Do they believe I belong here?


Detection Layers


Every creature tracks four major categories.


Visual Detection


What they see.


Influenced by:


Lighting
Clothing
Movement speed
Mutation visibility
Sound Detection


What they hear.


Generated by:


Footsteps
Sprinting
Doors
Combat
Crafting
Vehicles
Scent Detection


A major mechanic.


Humans and zombies use scent differently.


Behavioral Detection


The signature mechanic.


NPCs notice unusual behavior.


A zombie acting human.


A human acting zombie.


Both create suspicion.


Human Stealth


When entering settlements, Ember must appear human.


Human Appearance


Players manage:


Clothing
Grooming
Odor
Injuries
Signs of decay
Social Camouflage


Not every stealth mission involves crouching.


Sometimes the challenge is:


Making conversation
Hiding symptoms
Maintaining eye contact
Controlling bloodlust


A conversation can be more dangerous than combat.


Zombie Stealth


One of the game's signature systems.


Blending Through Behavior


Players imitate:


Walking patterns
Body posture
Head movements


Move incorrectly and zombies begin noticing.


Scent Matching


Certain items help maintain zombie camouflage.


The player balances:


Human acceptance vs Zombie acceptance


Rarely both at once.


Shadow System


Darkness becomes a tactical tool.


Light Sources
Campfires
Flashlights
Streetlights
Vehicles
Settlements


Players may:


Disable lights
Create blackout routes
Use darkness for infiltration
Environmental Stealth


The world itself becomes cover.


Use:


Tall grass
Corn fields
Forests
Rubble
Flooded zones
Dense fog
Decoy System


Players can manipulate attention.


Examples:


Sound Decoys


Throw objects.


Activate alarms.


Set timers.


Scent Decoys


Blood traps.


Animal bait.


Synthetic attractants.


Light Decoys


Flares.


Generators.


Emergency lighting.


Settlement Infiltration


One of the most advanced stealth activities.


Large settlements feature:


Guards
Patrols
Curfews
Checkpoints
Identification requirements


The player can:


Gain trust
Forge identities
Build disguises
Become a respected citizen


All while secretly being infected.


Bloodlust & Stealth Interaction


As bloodlust rises:


Stealth becomes harder.


Symptoms appear.


Examples:


Glowing eyes
Aggressive posture
Uncontrolled movements
Enhanced breathing
Visible mutations


The monster inside literally becomes harder to hide.


Endgame Stealth


Late game allows incredible scenarios.


You may:


Walk through a zombie mega-horde unnoticed.
Attend a survivor council meeting while hiding your infection.
Cross rooftops above an entire city.
Travel between factions under false identities.
Use conscious-zombie abilities without revealing yourself.


The ultimate stealth fantasy is not becoming invisible.


It's becoming believable.


To survive in Phoenix Blaze, you must convince two completely different worlds that you are one of them, while desperately trying not to lose sight of who you really are.




Advanced Stealth System
"Survival Through Deception"


Most stealth systems are about hiding.


Phoenix Blaze is about becoming someone else.


The game continuously calculates multiple layers of detection simultaneously.


Visual Detection System
What The Target Sees


Every creature has a vision profile.


Humans notice:


Clothing
Weapons
Suspicious behavior
Injuries
Mutations


Zombies notice:


Movement patterns
Body posture
Blood exposure
Behavioral abnormalities
Visibility Factors
Lighting


Bright sunlight increases visibility.


Darkness decreases it.


Movement


Fast movement attracts attention.


Slow movement blends naturally.


Silhouette


Standing on ridges.


Rooftops.


Open roads.


Makes you highly visible.


Clothing


Bright clothing increases detection.


Camouflaged clothing reduces detection.


Sound Detection System
What The Target Hears


Every movement creates sound.


Sound Sources


Walking.


Running.


Vaulting.


Doors.


Weapons.


Crafting.


Vehicles.


Animals.


Companion actions.


Surface Materials


Each material has different sound properties.


Wood:


Moderate noise


Metal:


Extremely loud


Grass:


Very quiet


Leaves:


Rustling sounds


Glass:


Catastrophic noise
NPC Hearing Profiles


Some enemies hear better than others.


Examples:


Human scout:


Excellent hearing


Regular zombie:


Average hearing


Alpha zombie:


Exceptional hearing
Smell Detection System


One of Phoenix Blaze's defining mechanics.


Human Scent


Humans emit:


Sweat
Blood
Smoke
Food odors


Infected can track these scents.


Zombie Scent


The player also emits infected odors.


This helps blend with zombies.


But humans may notice.


Wind Dynamics


Wind direction affects scent travel.


Players can use wind strategically.


Approach from downwind.


Leave scent traps.


Avoid detection.


Heat Detection System


Used by rare enemies.


Not all enemies see traditionally.


Some evolved infected sense body heat.


Heat Sources


Player.

Decay Management


Unique to Ember.


The player manages physical deterioration through:


Medical supplies
Preservation treatments
Specialized cultivation products
Hygiene


Neglecting this system increases suspicion and can impact both social interactions and gameplay effectiveness.


Together, these systems ensure that every day in Phoenix Blaze feels like a balancing act between survival, secrecy, humanity, and the constant pressure of the infection.


Survival Needs System
"You Are Not Truly Dead. You Are Not Fully Alive."


Most survival games focus on hunger and thirst.


Phoenix Blaze takes survival several layers deeper because Ember exists in a biological gray zone between human and infected.


The player isn't managing a character.


They're managing a fragile balance between two different forms of existence.

Energy

Mental stability
Improved stamina

Better stamina
Faster healing
Better mood
Hungry

Significant penalties.

The infection becomes harder to suppress.
