Season System
"Time Changes Everything"


The world constantly evolves through seasonal cycles.


Spring


Season of recovery.


Characteristics


New growth.


More wildlife.


Agricultural preparation.


Increased exploration opportunities.


Summer


Peak activity.


Benefits


Maximum crop production.


Best exploration conditions.


Abundant resources.


Risks


Heat.


Water shortages.


Wildfires.


Autumn


Harvest season.


One of the most important parts of the year.


Features


Crop gathering.


Resource stockpiling.


Settlement preparation.


Migration patterns change.


Winter


The ultimate survival challenge.


Conditions


Food becomes scarce.


Travel becomes dangerous.


Wildlife becomes aggressive.


Resources matter more than ever.


Settlement Gameplay


Winter tests every system:


Food reserves
Shelter quality
Community morale
Medical preparedness
Leadership


A poorly prepared settlement may not survive until spring.


The combination of Survival Needs, Medical Simulation, Weather, and Seasons creates the living heartbeat of Phoenix Blaze. While Bloodlust is the monster inside Ember, these systems are the relentless pressures of the world outside, ensuring that every day of survival feels earned.




Companion Relationship System
"Not All Companions Have Four Legs"


This system is completely separate from the Companion Animal System.


The animal system tracks relationships with creatures.


The Companion Relationship System tracks relationships with people who actively travel, live, fight, build, and survive alongside you.


These companions can become:


Friends
Followers
Advisors
Co-founders of settlements
Rivals
Mentors
Family-like allies


Unlike ordinary NPCs, companion characters have much deeper simulation systems.


Trust
"Can I Depend On You?"


Trust is the foundation of every companion relationship.


Trust increases when:


You keep promises
Complete companion quests
Save them
Share resources
Tell the truth


Trust decreases when:


You abandon them
Lie
Break agreements
Hoard resources
Cause unnecessary harm
Effects Of Trust


High Trust:


Unlocks personal quests
Reveals secrets
Improves combat cooperation
Opens special dialogue


Low Trust:


Arguments
Refusal to follow orders
Desertion
Betrayal
Loyalty
"Will They Stay When Things Get Bad?"


Trust and loyalty are different.


Someone can trust you but still leave.


Loyalty measures long-term commitment.


Examples


A companion may trust Ember personally.


However...


If Ember reveals she is infected:


Their loyalty determines whether they stay or leave.


High Loyalty Unlocks:


Defensive sacrifices
Heroic rescues
Leadership positions
Permanent companion status
Bonding
Emotional Connection


Bonding tracks memories.


Not statistics.


Examples include:


First mission together
Saving each other
Building a shelter together
Surviving winter together
Defending a settlement


Over time companions begin sharing personal stories.


The player becomes part of their life.

Transportation Network System
Civilization Requires Connection


Late-game gameplay moves beyond individual settlements.


Entire regions become connected.


Roads


The simplest transport network.


Benefits:


Faster travel
Better trade
More security
Bridges


Connect separated regions.


Open new trade routes.


Reduce travel times dramatically.


Trade Routes


Automated economic connections.


Resources begin moving between settlements.


Example:


Farm Settlement → supplies food → Industrial Settlement


Industrial Settlement → supplies machinery → Farm Settlement


Rail Restoration


One of the largest late-game projects.


Restoring railways creates:


Bulk transportation
Military support
Regional growth


A restored rail line may completely transform entire provinces.

Religion & Belief System


As civilization rebuilds, new belief systems emerge.


Some worship survival.


Some worship mutation.


Some see Ember as a symbol.


Some fear her.


Regional Influence System


The player eventually influences entire territories rather than individual settlements.


Politics, economics, and security begin operating on a regional scale.


World Recovery System


Perhaps the final long-term system.


Players slowly help determine whether the future becomes:


Human
Infected
Hybrid
Wild


The true endgame of Phoenix Blaze is not defeating a final boss.


It is deciding what kind of civilization rises from the ashes of the old world.


Hidden Endgame Systems
The Systems That Ultimately Decide The Fate Of The Ashlands


Most players will spend dozens of hours surviving.


The deepest systems do not fully emerge until the player has become a regional leader, scientific pioneer, settlement founder, and symbol of an entire generation.


These systems represent the true endgame of Phoenix Blaze.


Conscious Zombie Society System
"What If The Infected Are Still People?"


The single biggest question in the game.


Most survivors assume infection means death.


Ember proves otherwise.


Late-game discoveries reveal that conscious infected may be slowly appearing throughout the world.


Finding Conscious Infected


Players can locate rare individuals who:


Retained memories
Maintain self-control
Developed coping mechanisms
Built hidden communities


Each one has unique perspectives.


Building Hybrid Settlements


Players may found settlements where:


Humans live openly
Conscious infected live openly
Both cooperate


This becomes incredibly controversial.


Unique Challenges


Humans fear infected.


Infected fear rejection.


Settlements constantly struggle with:


Trust
Fear
Rights
Leadership
Unique Endings


A fully functioning conscious-zombie civilization may become:


Humanity's salvation
Humanity's replacement
The next stage of evolution
Cure Research System
The Largest Scientific Project In The Game


The "Cure" is not a single quest.


It is an enormous research program.


Research Requirements


Players must assemble:


Scientists
Medical specialists
Laboratories
Resource networks
Genetic archives
Cure Paths


Several possible outcomes exist.


Full Reversal


Restore humanity.


Stabilization


Maintain consciousness.


Suppress symptoms.


Adaptive Symbiosis


Humans and infected coexist biologically.


Failure


Some paths reveal the cure may be impossible.


Ethical Questions


The player eventually asks:


Should conscious infected be cured if they no longer want to be?


Genetic Engineering System
Rewriting Life


Emerges from Ember's botanical and scientific expertise.

Create custom:


Cannabis strains

Drought
Frost
Disease
Biological Engineering

Infection resistance
Neural stabilization
Cellular repair


Always framed as scientific survival research rather than superpowers.


Regional Agriculture


Entire ecosystems may change through player choices.


Regions become greener.


Healthier.

The Next Stage Of Existence


Different from mutations.


Mutations are individual.

Human Evolution


Human settlements adapt.

Infected Evolution


Zombie populations change.


New behaviors emerge.


New adaptations appear.


Hybrid Evolution


The player's choices can encourage coexistence.


Eventually creating an entirely new branch of civilization.

The Twelve Master Pillars Of Phoenix Blaze


Everything in the game ultimately falls into these pillars:


1. Character & Identity


Who are you?


Human?

The game's emotional core.

The world's master AI.

Whether the player is present or not.


The Ultimate Theme


Most zombie games ask:


"How do you survive the apocalypse?"


Phoenix Blaze asks:


"After the apocalypse, what kind of civilization deserves to rise from the ashes?"


Every system, from bloodlust management to city building, ultimately feeds into that question.


Phoenix Blaze
A Life-Simulation RPG Between Humanity and the Horde


At its heart, Phoenix Blaze is not a zombie game.


It is a life-simulation RPG about existing between two worlds that both believe you shouldn't exist.


You are not fully human.


You are not fully zombie.


You are something caught in the middle.


Every system in the game revolves around that reality.


The Core Fantasy


Most survival games ask:


How do I survive the apocalypse?


Phoenix Blaze asks:


How do I build a life when I no longer belong anywhere?


Human settlements fear you.


Zombie hordes don't understand you.


You wake up every morning facing the same dilemma:


Do you act more human?


Or more infected?


The answer changes who you become.


Living Two Lives


During the day you might:


Trade crops with a farming settlement
Repair generators
Tend your greenhouse
Teach children how to grow food
Attend settlement council meetings
Build relationships and friendships


To everyone around you, you're simply another survivor.


That same night you may:


Walk through a zombie migration route
Use infected senses to track prey
Listen to the whisper of bloodlust
Hide signs of decay
Hunt dangerous creatures
Explore places no human can enter


The player constantly shifts between both identities.


Daily Life


Unlike traditional action RPGs, daily life is important.


A normal day might involve:


Waking up in your shelter.


Checking crop health.


Feeding animals.


Reviewing settlement supply shortages.


Repairing damaged equipment.


Trading medicine.


Helping a neighbor.


Scouting a nearby town.


Avoiding a zombie horde.


Managing bloodlust.


Returning home before dark.


The apocalypse isn't the whole experience.


Life continues despite it.


A World Of Relationships


Relationships become one of the most valuable resources.


The player develops bonds with:


Survivors
Settlers
Traders
Scientists
Rangers
Conscious infected


People begin learning who you are.


Or who they think you are.


Some relationships are built on truth.


Others are built on lies.


A settlement leader might trust you completely.


A close friend may eventually discover you're infected.


The question becomes:


Do they accept you?


Protect you?


Expose you?


Or hunt you?


A Home Worth Protecting


The game slowly shifts from survival to belonging.


At first:


You have a campfire.


A sleeping bag.


A few supplies.


Months later:


You have:


Farms
Workshops
Laboratories
Companions
Defenses
Families
Communities


You stop surviving the world.


You start shaping it.


Living With Bloodlust


The greatest conflict isn't external.


It's internal.


The infection never leaves.


You feel it constantly.


The smell of blood.


The scent of fear.


The urge to feed.


The desire to stop fighting.


To give in.


Bloodlust isn't simply a meter.


It's a second life trying to overwrite the first.


Every day you successfully resist it feels like a personal victory.


Existing Between Species


Perhaps the most unique aspect of Phoenix Blaze is that the player slowly learns both sides are wrong.


Humans see infection as death.


Many zombies are trapped inside instincts they can no longer control.


Ember eventually discovers reality is more complicated.


Conscious infected.


Hybrid communities.


Unexpected alliances.


New forms of society.


The line between human and zombie begins to blur.


Building The Future


Most post-apocalyptic games focus on collapse.


Phoenix Blaze focuses on reconstruction.


You grow food.


Restore power.


Research medicine.


Build communities.


Preserve knowledge.


Reconnect regions.


Create transportation networks.


Reintroduce trade.


Establish governments.


Help civilization rise again.


The game slowly transforms from survival to nation-building.


The Emotional Core


The emotional heart of Phoenix Blaze isn't combat.


It isn't zombies.


It isn't even survival.


It is identity.


Every major system asks the same question:


If your body changes, your instincts change, and the entire world sees you as something else... what actually makes you human?


Is it your memories?


Your choices?


Your relationships?


Your compassion?


Your ability to rebuild rather than destroy?


What Phoenix Blaze Really Is


Phoenix Blaze is:


A survival simulator
A farming simulator
A settlement builder
A relationship RPG
A cultivation simulator
A political strategy game
A zombie survival game
A civilization recovery simulator


But above all else:


It is a life-simulation RPG about being the bridge between humanity and the infected, trying to build a future where both might survive, while struggling every day not to lose yourself in the process.




Phoenix Blaze: The Cannabis System
"The Plant That Saved Humanity's Last Conscious Zombie"


In most games, cannabis would be a consumable.


In Phoenix Blaze, cannabis is:


Medicine
Agriculture
Crafting
Science
Economy
Therapy
Identity
Research
Politics
Survival


And most importantly:


It is the reason Ember Vale still has a conscience.


The entire game can be viewed as a giant cannabis ecosystem connected to every major pillar of gameplay.


The Origin Of Cannabis In Phoenix Blaze


Before the collapse, Ember was not a soldier.


She was not a scientist working for the military.


She was a master cultivator, geneticist, and medical cannabis researcher.


Project Phoenix originally focused on creating strains capable of supporting neural repair and emotional regulation.


After infection:


Those same strains became the first effective bloodlust suppressant.


The plant that once treated anxiety now helps prevent humanity from disappearing.


The Cannabis Gameplay Loop


The complete loop looks like this:


Discover Genetics ↓ Collect Seeds ↓ Clone Plants ↓ Build Grow Facilities ↓ Control Environment ↓ Breed Strains ↓ Harvest Plants ↓ Dry Product ↓ Cure Product ↓ Process Product ↓ Craft Items ↓ Suppress Bloodlust ↓ Trade Excess Product ↓ Fund Expansion ↓ Research New Genetics


Repeat.


Cannabis Genetics System


Every strain has hidden genetic values.


THC


Represents psychoactive potential.


Effects:


Mood changes
Bloodlust reduction
Euphoria
Pain reduction


High THC strains may provide strong suppression while creating focus penalties.


CBD


Represents calming effects.


Effects:


Bloodlust stabilization
Reduced aggression
Reduced stress
Better sleep


Extremely valuable to conscious infected.


CBG


Rare cannabinoid.


Effects:


Neural stabilization
Improved cognitive function
Reduced decay


Important in cure-path research.


CBC


Can influence:


Healing
Emotional balance
Recovery
Terpenes


Terpenes determine personality.


Examples:


Myrcene


Improves calmness.


Limonene


Improves mood.


Pinene


Improves focus.


Caryophyllene


Improves pain resistance.


Linalool


Improves sleep quality.


Seed Collection System


Seeds are found throughout the Ashlands.


Common Seeds


Simple genetics.


Reliable cultivation.


Heritage Seeds


Pre-collapse genetics.


Highly prized.


Rare Mutated Genetics


Created after the outbreak.


Found only in dangerous regions.


Legendary Genetics


Unique one-of-a-kind seeds.


May exist only once in the entire world.


Clone System


Not every crop starts from seed.


Players can clone elite genetics.


Benefits:


Consistency
Predictable quality
Commercial production


High-end settlements depend on cloning systems.


Growing Environment System


Growing quality determines final product quality.


Outdoor Farming


Advantages:


Cheap
Natural
Large yields


Disadvantages:


Weather dependent
Pest pressure
Security issues
Greenhouse Growing


Middle tier.


More consistent.


Better quality.


Requires infrastructure.


Indoor Growing


Highest control.


Player manages:


Light schedules
Ventilation
Humidity
Temperature
Air circulation


Produces elite genetics.


Consumes large amounts of power.


Soil System


Different soils produce different outcomes.


Forest Soil


Balanced growth.


River Soil


Excellent yields.


Wetland Soil


Unique mutations.


Engineered Soil


Late game cultivation.


Maximum performance.


Nutrient System


Plants require nutrients.


Primary nutrients:


Nitrogen
Phosphorus
Potassium


Secondary nutrients:


Calcium
Magnesium
Sulfur


Micronutrients affect:


Potency
Flavor
Yield
Plant Care Mechanics


Players monitor:


Water


Too little:


Slow growth.


Too much:


Root damage.


Disease.


Lighting


Controls development stages.


Temperature


Affects potency.


Humidity


Influences disease risk.


Airflow


Protects plants.


Improves health.


Pest System


Plants face biological threats.


Examples:


Mites
Mold
Mildew
Fungal infections


Players must:


Prevent
Treat
Isolate outbreaks
Mutation System


Exposure to infected regions may alter genetics.


Possible results:


Increased potency.


New terpene profiles.


Bloodlust suppression improvements.


Enhanced medicinal effects.


Unexpected traits.


Harvest System


Harvest timing matters.


Early Harvest


Benefits:


Faster turnaround


Costs:


Lower potency


Late Harvest


Benefits:


Maximum potency


Costs:


Smaller margins for error
Drying System


Fresh cannabis must be dried.


Improper drying destroys quality.


Variables:


Airflow
Humidity
Temperature
Drying time
Curing System


Very important.


Many survival games would ignore curing.


Phoenix Blaze shouldn't.


Long cure:


Better effects
Better value
Better medicinal properties


Poor cure:


Reduced effectiveness
Reduced trade value
Processing System


Harvests become ingredients.


Flower


Raw product.


Kief


Collected trichomes.


Concentrated material.


Hash


Compressed concentrate.


Higher potency.


Oils


Medical applications.


Tinctures


Precise dosing.


Ideal for bloodlust management.


Edibles


Long duration.


Slow onset.


Excellent before long expeditions.


Salves


Medical applications.


Treat pain and injuries.


Consumption Methods


Different methods create different effects.


Joint


Quick relief.


Portable.


Short duration.


Pipe


Efficient.


Reliable.


Bong


Greater bloodlust reduction.


Longer suppression window.


More resource consumption.


Vaporizer


Stealth consumption.


Minimal odor.


Excellent for infiltration missions.


Edibles


Longest duration.


Best sustained control.


Medicinal Dosing


Allows precise bloodlust stabilization.


Popular among advanced players.


Bloodlust Suppression Science


The most important gameplay function.


Every strain interacts differently with:


Bloodlust
Stress
Humanity
Sleep
Aggression
Focus


No single strain is perfect.


Players must design genetics for specific problems.

Sleep Strains

Reduce suspicion-related symptoms.


Hybrid Strains


Combinations of multiple goals.

The Ultimate Cannabis Fantasy


In Phoenix Blaze the cannabis system is not a side activity.


It is a complete progression path.


A player can become:


Master Cultivator
Genetic Scientist
Medical Researcher
Settlement Provider
Industrial Producer
Hybrid Biologist
Savior Of Conscious Infected


The plant becomes the thread connecting survival, science, medicine, economy, politics, relationships, civilization rebuilding, and Ember's personal fight to remain human.




This document is already unusually strong because it has begun shifting from "feature list" thinking to design philosophy thinking. If I were elevating it toward a professional production-ready GDD, I would strengthen and clarify the foundational vision.


PHOENIX BLAZE
Core Design Pillars (Enhanced)
1. The Zombie Is The Protagonist


Phoenix is not a human survivor with zombie powers.


She is a conscious infected individual attempting to function in a world built for humans and overrun by the undead.


Her biology affects every system:


Movement
Combat
Senses
Relationships
Survival
Crafting
Exploration
Dialogue
Story outcomes


The game's central fantasy is:


"What if the monster everyone fears is still a person?"


Phoenix's undead nature should never become a cosmetic skin layered over a traditional survival game.


She sees differently.


Smells differently.


Moves differently.


Experiences the world differently.


The player should constantly feel that difference.


2. Survival Is About Identity Management


Most survival games ask:


Can you survive?


Phoenix Blaze asks:


Can you survive while hiding what you are?


The player's primary challenge is not simply resource management.


It is identity management.


Every day requires balancing:


Bloodlust
Appearance
Behavior
Reputation
Relationships
Exposure risk


Passing as human and passing as undead are separate skills with separate requirements.


Improving one often compromises the other.


A player should frequently find themselves asking:


"Which world am I trying to belong to today?"


3. Every System Expresses Identity


Systems are not isolated mechanics.


Every mechanic is a form of character expression.


Examples:


Feeding


Not merely hunger relief.


Represents:


Survival instincts
Ethics
Self-control
Cannabis Use


Not merely a consumable.


Represents:


Medical coping
Self-medication
Scientific understanding
Personal discipline
Mutations


Not simply power upgrades.


Represent:


Adaptation
Sacrifice
Acceptance
Transformation
Relationships


Not reputation farming.


Represent:


Connection
Trust
Vulnerability
Memory Recovery


Not collectibles.


Represents:


Identity reconstruction
Personal history
Truth discovery


Players should never ask:


"What gives the best stats?"


They should ask:


"Who is Phoenix becoming?"


4. Crafting Must Be Physical And Understandable


Crafting should feel grounded.


Players should generally understand:


Where materials came from
Why ingredients are needed
How objects are assembled


The Multi-Stick embodies this principle.


A branch becomes:


Walking Stick → Tool → Weapon → Fishing Spear → Leverage Tool → Survival Companion


The best crafted items feel earned through understanding rather than arbitrary recipes.


5. Story Is Archaeology


The player is not following a plot.


The player is excavating one.


Information should be discovered through:


Memories
Objects
Places
Survivors
Buildings
Research data
Environmental storytelling


The mystery unfolds because the player investigates.


Not because an NPC repeatedly explains it.


6. Consequences Create History


The world remembers.


But not through a simplistic morality meter.


Instead track:


Individual trust
Relationship histories
Faction reputation
Community stability
Research decisions
Feeding habits
Mutation choices
Important promises


Players should create a history, not earn points.


7. The World Is Authored First, Procedural Second


Procedural systems support replayability.


They do not replace storytelling.


Critical elements should always remain discoverable:


Main mysteries
Key characters
Emotional moments
Essential clues


Randomization provides variation.


Authoring provides meaning.


8. Scope Is Proven Through Play


A functioning 30-minute experience is more valuable than 100 unfinished systems.


Every proposed system must justify itself by improving:


Identity
Survival
Story
Exploration
Relationships


If a system cannot support one of those pillars, it should be reconsidered.


Refined Master Core Loop


The existing loop can become more evocative:


Observe


Read the environment. Evaluate threats. Assess bloodlust.


↓


Decide


Choose routes. Choose disguises. Choose alliances.


↓


Gather


Scavenge resources. Recover memories. Collect blood. Acquire genetics.


↓


Prepare


Craft equipment. Mix blood. Cultivate plants. Manage decay.


↓


Survive


Use stealth. Fight. Flee. Negotiate.


↓


Recover


Rest. Maintain shelter. Strengthen relationships.


↓


Discover


Unlock memories. Advance investigations. Reveal truths.


↓


Influence


Change settlements. Shape factions. Alter ecosystems.


↓


Evolve


Mutate. Adapt. Redefine identity.


↓


Repeat


The True Core Loop


Underneath every mechanic sits a deeper loop:


The Human Self


Wants:


Relationships
Community
Knowledge
Purpose


↓


The Undead Self


Wants:


Blood
Instinct
Survival
Adaptation


↓


The Player


Balances both.


↓


A choice is made.


↓


The world reacts.


↓


Phoenix changes.


↓


Repeat.


The Defining Question Of The Entire Game


Everything eventually reduces to one question:


"If you are no longer fully human, what responsibilities do you still have to humanity?"


That question should be reflected in every major system, every faction, every companion, every settlement, every memory, every mutation, and every ending.


When players finish Phoenix Blaze, they should not remember it primarily as a zombie game.


They should remember it as a game about identity, belonging, survival, and deciding what kind of future deserves to rise from the ashes.


Progression Psychology System
"The Player Should Always Want One More Day"


One of the biggest reasons survival games fail isn't combat or crafting.


It's because the player eventually asks:


"Why am I doing all this?"


Phoenix Blaze needs a progression structure that constantly feeds motivation on multiple time scales.


Short-Term Goals
Minutes To Hours


Short-term goals solve immediate problems.


They create tension.


They drive moment-to-moment play.


A player should always have 3-5 active short-term goals.


Examples:


Find food before nightfall
Lower bloodlust
Reach a safehouse
Craft a Multi-Stick
Repair a broken backpack
Avoid a nearby patrol
Find water
Harvest mature cannabis plants
Retrieve a medical supply cache
Short-Term Goal Rules


Goals should be:


Clear
Achievable
Useful


Completing them creates constant dopamine hits.


The player never feels aimless.


Mid-Term Goals
Several In-Game Days To Weeks


These drive entire play sessions.


Examples:


Upgrade shelter to Safehouse Tier 2
Grow first successful cannabis harvest
Earn Marcus's trust
Reach the Industrial Zone
Restore power to a water tower
Unlock blood mixing
Discover a conscious infected
Finish the first ChroniX investigation
Mid-Term Goal Design


Each region should introduce new mid-term goals.


Players should constantly feel like they're working toward something meaningful.


Long-Term Goals
Entire Playthrough Objectives


These define identity.


Examples:


Research Path
Find cure research
Settlement Path
Build thriving communities
Cultivator Path
Create legendary strains
Evolution Path
Master mutations
Diplomat Path
Unite factions
Conscious Infected Path
Build a hybrid civilization


These are not simply quests.


They become personal ambitions.


Goal Generation System


The game should dynamically generate new goals by combining:


Current location
Known relationships
Bloodlust state
Faction standing
Story progress
Nearby opportunities


Result:


The player almost never lacks direction.


Tutorial & Onboarding System
"Teach Through Survival"


Phoenix Blaze's systems are too complex for tutorial popups alone.


The game should teach through experiences.


Teaching Bloodlust


The opening hours should make bloodlust impossible to ignore.


Opening Event


Phoenix hides in an alley.


A wounded survivor stumbles nearby.


The screen changes.


Heartbeats grow louder.


Smells intensify.


Controls remain responsive.


The player learns:


Bloodlust changes perception.


Without reading a single tooltip.


First Cannabis Use


Shortly afterward the player discovers:


Medical stash
Cannabis product
Journal note


Using it immediately demonstrates bloodlust suppression.


The lesson becomes memorable.


Teaching Human Disguise


The player encounters survivors.


One reacts nervously.


Another notices something is wrong.


The player discovers:


Clean clothing
A mirror
A chance to wash


Returning produces noticeably different reactions.


Players naturally learn that appearance matters.


Teaching Zombie Disguise


Soon afterward the player encounters zombies.


One ignores Phoenix.


Another becomes interested.


The player experiments and realizes:


Behavior matters.


Movement matters.


Scent matters.


The system teaches itself.


Teaching Cultivation


Players encounter:


Iris's abandoned greenhouse.


Inside:


Seeds
Notes
Drying racks


The player grows a simple starter plant.


Fast growth rates in the tutorial allow immediate experimentation.


Teaching Memory Recovery


The player finds:


Old photos
Audio logs
Employee badges


Every memory recovered unlocks something useful.


Dialogue.


Recipes.


Clues.


Abilities.


This teaches:


Memories are progression.


Tutorial Design Philosophy


Never teach with:


"Press E to engage bloodlust management system."


Instead teach through:


Fear.


Curiosity.


Discovery.


Consequences.


Discovery Reward System
"Exploration Should Always Matter"


Exploration becomes addictive when every discovery belongs to a meaningful category.


Every location should contain at least one discovery reward.


Often multiple.


Mechanical Discovery
"I Learned Something"


Rewards gameplay.


Examples:


New crafting recipes
Blood mixing formula
Shelter upgrade
Mutation option
Companion training technique
New farming method
Emotional Effect


"I can do something new."


Narrative Discovery
"I Learned What Happened"


Rewards curiosity.


Examples:


Euphoria Project records
Survivor journals
Security footage
ChroniX emails
Witness statements
Emotional Effect


"I understand the world better."


Relationship Discovery
"I Learned Who Someone Really Is"


Rewards social investment.


Examples:


Marcus's past
Lena's guilt
Riven's secret
Hidden loyalties
Personal fears
Lost loved ones
Emotional Effect


"I understand this person."


World Discovery
"I Found Something"


Rewards exploration itself.


Examples:


Hidden locations
Secret tunnels
Safehouses
Greenhouses
Research labs
Underground communities
Emotional Effect


"I wonder what else is out there."


Identity Discovery
"I Learned Who Phoenix Is"


The most important discovery category.


Examples:


Childhood memories
Research involvement
Personal recordings
Lost relationships
Evidence of her transformation


These discoveries answer:


Who was Phoenix?
What happened?
Who is she becoming?
Emotional Effect


"I understand myself better."


Discovery Layering


The best discoveries reward multiple categories simultaneously.


Example:


The player discovers an abandoned ChroniX greenhouse.


Rewards:


Mechanical Discovery


New cultivation recipe.


Narrative Discovery


Research notes.


Relationship Discovery


Messages from Iris.


World Discovery


Hidden facility.


Identity Discovery


A memory of Phoenix working there.


One location.


Five rewards.


This layering makes every meaningful discovery feel significant.


Exploration Excitement Formula


The ideal player experience should be:


I found a place → I found a clue → I found a new ability → I learned something about someone → I learned something about myself → Now I have a new goal.


When exploration consistently creates new goals, reveals new information, and changes how the player sees Phoenix and the world, exploration stops being map-clearing gameplay and becomes the engine that drives the entire RPG forward.




Identity Discovery System
"Who Was Phoenix Before She Became The Monster?"


Memory recovery should not work like finding collectible audio logs.


Memories are one of the primary progression systems in Phoenix Blaze.


The player is not simply uncovering lore.


The player is rebuilding a person.


What Is An Identity Discovery?


Identity Discoveries are memories tied directly to Phoenix.


Unlike ordinary story discoveries, they answer questions such as:


Who was I?
Who did I love?
What did I believe?
What part did I play in the outbreak?
What am I becoming now?
Memory Fragment Categories
Personal Memories


Examples:


Childhood moments
Family gatherings
Friendships
Celebrations


Rewards:


New dialogue options
Social bonuses
Personality insights
Professional Memories


Examples:


Euphoria research
ChroniX meetings
Laboratory work
Experimental strains


Rewards:


New recipes
Research unlocks
Technical knowledge
Trauma Memories


Examples:


Outbreak day
Infection moment
Failed experiments
Important losses


Rewards:


Plot progression
Key story revelations
Character growth
Relationship Memories


Examples:


Former friends
Coworkers
Partners
Research teams


Rewards:


New conversation branches
Trust opportunities
Relationship context
Instinct Memories


Unique to conscious infected.


Fragments triggered by:


Blood
Smells
Locations
Certain mutations


These memories reveal how infection changed Phoenix.


Dynamic Memory Triggers


Memories should emerge naturally.


Not from opening a menu.


Triggers include:


Entering specific locations
Hearing certain music
Smelling familiar plants
Recovering important objects
Meeting specific characters
Finding old recordings


The world becomes a living web of remembered experiences.


The Identity Archive


Recovered memories populate:


Phoenix's Journal


Containing:


Timeline reconstruction
Character observations
Personal reflections
Unanswered questions


Eventually players create a complete portrait of Phoenix's life.


Migration & Population System
"Civilization Doesn't Stand Still"


Most open-world games freeze populations in place.


Phoenix Blaze should allow the world to move.


People relocate.


Animals migrate.


Communities rise and fall.


The map itself evolves.


Human Population Movement


Settlements monitor:


Food
Water
Security
Economy
Leadership


When these factors decline:


Population may leave.


Refugee Waves


Collapsed settlements create refugees.


Examples:


Settlement destroyed by horde ↓ Survivors flee ↓ Nearby towns become crowded ↓ Food shortages emerge ↓ Regional politics change


The player witnesses consequences.


Community Growth


Successfully supported settlements attract:


Farmers
Traders
Researchers
Craftspeople


Population becomes a reward.


Faction Relocation


Factions are not static.


A defeated faction may:


Retreat
Reorganize
Establish a new base


Creating evolving power maps.


Wildlife Migration


Animals respond to:


Seasons
Predators
Weather
Human activity


Examples:


Deer move to safer regions.


Wolves follow deer.


Zombies follow wildlife.


The ecosystem continually shifts.


Zombie Migration


Perhaps the most important movement system.


Hordes move because of:


Sound
Food sources
Weather
Other hordes


Players can influence migration.


Entire regions can become safer or more dangerous.


Transportation Logistics System
"Civilization Travels On Roads"


Once settlements exist, resources must move.


Late-game Phoenix Blaze becomes partially a logistics simulator.


Resource Movement


Resources include:


Food
Water
Medicine
Hemp
Cannabis
Building materials
Research equipment


Goods must physically travel.


Caravan System


NPC caravans travel between settlements.


Each caravan has:


Cargo
Guards
Destination
Risk level
Trade Route Health


Routes can become:


Stable


Reliable and profitable.


Dangerous


Bandits.


Hordes.


Storm damage.


Disabled


Bridge collapse.


Territory conflict.


Major disaster.


Player Logistics Projects


The player can improve transportation.


Examples:


Build roads
Repair bridges
Clear routes
Establish checkpoints


Infrastructure literally reshapes regional economies.


Hordes And Logistics


A migrating mega-horde may:


Block roads
Destroy commerce
Isolate communities


One event can impact multiple settlements.


Information Network System
"Knowledge Is Civilization"


Information becomes a strategic resource.


Early Game


Information travels slowly.


Sources include:


Travelers
Notes
Word of mouth


Everything feels uncertain.


Mid Game


Players begin restoring communication.


Examples:


Radios
Repeaters
Bulletin boards


Information becomes more reliable.


Late Game


Players can establish:


The Ashlands Information Network


A continent-spanning system for sharing information.


Radio Towers


Connect distant settlements.


Provide:


Warnings
News
Requests for help
Courier Routes


Dedicated information runners.


Carry:


Maps
Messages
Research
Settlement Bulletin Boards


Local information hubs.


Examples:


Requests
Rumors
Missing persons
Trade opportunities
Raven Communication


Companion ravens can transport messages.


Particularly useful between remote settlements.


Conscious Infected Communication


The conscious undead eventually create their own communication network.


Allowing cooperation across enormous distances.


Information Quality


Not all information is accurate.


Reports contain:


Accuracy ratings
Timeliness
Source credibility


Knowledge becomes something players manage.


Rumor System
"Not Everything Is True"


One of the most powerful world-building systems.


Rumors are not quests.


Rumors are possibilities.


How Rumors Appear


Sources include:


Settlement conversations
Radio chatter
Drunken survivors
Notes
Traders
Conscious infected
Rumor Quality Levels
Verified


Actually confirmed.


Reliable


Usually true.


Uncertain


Incomplete information.


False


Wrong information.


Sometimes intentionally.


Example Rumors


"The old hospital still has medicine."


Maybe true.


Maybe already looted.


Maybe a trap.


"Someone saw another conscious infected in the mountains."


Could start a major storyline.


Or be complete nonsense.


"The bridge is safe."


Maybe it collapsed yesterday.


The world changes.


Information ages.


Rumor Lifecycles


Rumors evolve.


Rumor Created ↓ Spreads Through Settlements ↓ Players Investigate ↓ Truth Emerges ↓ Rumor Becomes History


This means stories feel alive.


Rumor Consequences


False rumors still matter.


Examples:


Settlements panic
Caravans reroute
Factions mobilize
Refugees relocate


The player's world reacts to information, whether the information is true or not.


The Combined Effect


When Identity Discovery, Migration, Logistics, Information Networks, and Rumor Systems work together, the world stops feeling like a map full of quests.


Instead, it feels like a living society trying to survive the end of civilization.


Phoenix isn't just exploring ruins.


She's moving through an evolving world where memories preserve the past, migration reshapes communities, trade sustains civilization, communication spreads knowledge, and rumors create opportunities, dangers, and legends.




Fear System
"People Don't Just Trust Or Distrust You. They Fear Different Things."


Most RPGs track:


Reputation
Trust
Affinity


But fear is often more important than all three.


Fear determines what people do when stressed.


Fear determines what they become.


Fear determines what they sacrifice.


In Phoenix Blaze, fear should exist at:


Individual Level
Community Level
Regional Level
Species Level
Individual Fear Profiles


Every major NPC has unique fears.


These are hidden until discovered.


Marcus Reyes


Fears:


Losing people under his protection
Medical helplessness
Becoming infected


Because of his EMT history.


Lena Voss


Fears:


Responsibility
Failure
The truth about ChroniX
Riven


Fears:


Returning to humanity
Losing identity
Becoming controlled
Iris


Fears:


Environmental collapse
Losing biodiversity
Meaningless survival
Fear Categories
Fear Of Darkness


NPCs avoid:


Night travel
Underground locations
Unlit buildings
Fear Of Zombies


May cause:


Panic
Retreat
Mistakes
Fear Of Conscious Infected


One of the game's most interesting fears.


An ordinary zombie is understandable.


A thinking zombie is terrifying.


Many survivors react more strongly to Phoenix than to ordinary infected.


Fear Of Heights


May affect:


Exploration
Combat
Pathfinding
Fear Of Certain Factions


Some survivors may flee when specific groups arrive.


Fear Effects


Fear changes:


Dialogue
Combat
Loyalty
Relationships
Decision Making
Migration
Voting (late-game politics)
Dynamic Fear


Fear evolves.


A survivor who repeatedly survives attacks may become braver.


Someone experiencing trauma may develop new fears.


This allows character growth.


Fear Of Phoenix


Perhaps the most important fear system.


Some people fear:


Her infection.


Others fear:


What she represents.


Others fear:


Their own reaction to her.


This creates powerful emotional storytelling.


Regional Hope System
"Civilization Runs On Hope"


Perhaps one of the most important missing systems.


Most survival games focus on decline.


Phoenix Blaze focuses on rebuilding.


A world that survives is not enough.


A world must believe it has a future.


Hope Is Regional


Each region tracks:


Hope


Not morale.


Not happiness.


Hope.


What Creates Hope?
Food Security


When people know they can eat tomorrow.


Working Lights


A psychological game changer.


Seeing lights at night means civilization survives.


Safe Roads


Travel becomes possible.


Trade resumes.


People reconnect.


Schools


Knowledge survives.


Medicine


Illness becomes less frightening.


Communication


People stop feeling alone.


Leadership


Strong communities inspire confidence.

Immigration
New businesses

Exploration
Family formation


Low Hope Causes:


Desertion

Isolation
Extremism
Population decline
Regional Identity


High Hope regions feel different.


Children play.


Gardens grow.


Music returns.


People laugh.


These become visible signs of recovery.


The Hope Map


The world map evolves visually.


Regions gradually transform from:


Fear → Survival → Recovery → Prosperity


The player literally watches hope spread.


Legacy Of The Dead System
"Nobody Truly Dies While They Are Remembered"


One of the most emotional systems in the game.


Most games erase dead NPCs.


Phoenix Blaze should remember them.

Without the player's involvement.

Years later people still remember.


Family Legacy


Children.


Friends.


Partners.

History develops naturally.

The Ultimate Goal


Many post-apocalyptic games ask:


Can humanity survive?


Phoenix Blaze asks:


What is humanity worth preserving?


By the late game, the player isn't simply fighting zombies or managing bloodlust.


They're rebuilding:


Memory
Culture
Knowledge
Tradition
Community
Hope


The greatest victory is not defeating a final enemy.


It's creating a world where people stop merely surviving and begin living again.




## 🌍 Twenty Years Later: The Post-Phoenix Era


When the first wave of the ChroniX outbreak hit, humanity expected total extinction. Twenty years later, the world didn't end—it mutated. The survivors who remain are no longer fighting day-to-day for mere survival; they are building enduring institutions, establishing trade routes, and defining the political, cultural, and biological future of a shattered planet.


In *Phoenix Blaze*, civilization twenty years post-outbreak is defined by a fragile, brutal equilibrium between three emergent societal pillars: **Conscious Undead Enclaves**, **Corporate Remnants**, and **Neo-Agrarian Communes**.


---


### 🏛️ The Three Pillars of Post-Phoenix Civilization


| Civilization Type | Governing Philosophy | Economic Base | Stance on Conscious Undead |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **The Synthesis Pacts** | *“Memory is a bridge, not a barrier.”* Hybrid societies where humans and conscious undead coexist under strict quarantine and chemical stability laws. | Botanical monoculture, synthetic blood-filtering, and recovered ChroniX tech salvage. | **Integrated:** Conscious undead hold citizenship rights provided their bloodlust is legally suppressed. |
| **The Vanguard Bastions** | *“Purity through fire and protocol.”* Militarized remnants of state and corporate defense forces operating behind reinforced concrete walls. | Industrial manufacturing, slave labor camps, and heavily guarded agricultural zones. | **Hostile:** Zero tolerance. All undead—conscious or feral—are viewed as biological targets for eradication. |
| **The Feral Syndicates** | *“Strength to the apex predator.”* Tribal confederacies that reject both human laws and undead hiding, embracing the chaotic evolution of the wasteland. | Black-market trade, raiding, mutant fauna domestication, and high-potency strain distillation. | **Opportunistic:** Conscious undead are treated as warlords, mercenaries, or prey depending on their power. |


---


### 🌾 Economy, Agriculture & Botanical Trade


Twenty years of adaptation transformed the illegal cannabis labs of the early outbreak into the cornerstone of global civilization:


* **The Terpene Standard:** Traditional fiat currency collapsed decades ago. In the post-Phoenix era, currency is backed by **Stabilization Grade Tinctures**—purified, high-potency botanical extracts required to keep conscious undead from losing their minds to bloodlust.
* **Bioreactor Cities:** Former corporate skyscrapers have been gutted and converted into massive vertical hydroponic towers. Wheat, corn, and specialized medicinal strains grow under flickering grow-lights, guarded by automated defense turrets and human-undead labor cartels.
* **The Silt-Road Networks:** Caravan routes snake across the ruined highways. These trade routes are patrolled by heavily armed scouts who trade high-grade seeds, clean water, and mechanical parts in exchange for forged steel and rare pre-outbreak artifacts.


---


### 🎭 Culture, Religion & Philosophy


Time heals nothing; it only mythologizes. Two decades after the collapse, a rich cultural landscape has emerged from the ashes:


* **The Cult of the Second Birth:** A widespread religious movement teaching that zombification was a divine evolutionary crucible designed to strip away human vanity and leave only pure, conscious survival instinct.
* **Memory Preservationists:** Nomadic archivists who scour ruined pre-outbreak libraries, hard drives, and museums to record lost human history, art, and music before it rots away entirely.
* **The Grey Code:** An unwritten legal system governing disputes between human settlements and undead enclaves, establishing rules for territory rights, bloodlust incidents, and trade disputes.


---


The videos above showcase atmospheric post-apocalyptic world-building, settlement design, and environmental storytelling that capture the tone of a world two decades into reconstruction.


---


> 🧠 **DESIGN INTENT**
> Shifting the lens from a personal survival struggle to a twenty-year-old civilization simulation transforms *Phoenix Blaze* from a standard zombie game into a societal sandbox. Your conscious undead protagonist isn't just hiding in a basement anymore—they are a living historical anomaly capable of shifting the balance of power between rising empires.




# Civilization Simulation: The World Twenty Years After Phoenix


The biggest evolution of High and Undead / Phoenix Blaze is moving beyond a conventional zombie survival game into a living, breathing civilization simulation.


The central question is no longer simply:


"Can Phoenix survive the apocalypse?"


It becomes:


"What kind of civilization emerges twenty years after the world ends, and what happens when the creatures who destroyed humanity begin building a world of their own?"


Twenty years have passed since the outbreak. The old world is gone, but the world itself has not stopped evolving. Humanity has adapted, zombies have changed, new societies have formed, and the ruins of civilization have become the foundations of something entirely different.


Phoenix isn't just surviving in this world. Phoenix is living inside a civilization that continues to develop, whether the player participates or not.


## 1. A world that continues without the player


[Ученые посчитали, за какое время на Земле пропадут следы цивилизации, если люди исчезнут — Образ жизни / События | Trendymen](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/IVIbEaluMojNBZd5ptB2Yo01jVukpknZiR02GW4aqjhvKu4OfxGO4pcCJudqmHyDdTO3X_9cDyeeWDIwXQeZUwv8sfrtAwhqrBR58xiXIVC6BthSLe9b7oazNvBVUQpRsgj3Hpm-Nw7R9MuGrYb2h3uk_GZk3V00FKJTq2aL93M?purpose=inline)


[The Societal Rebirth: Life After the Apocalypse](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/ArLg8cSX0fTbfvfkZ1oZQRjwLha91pbQCSD4dahgTGTmKDT1sDm6Q7hszxdmJLc31c2Q2GCkLkIqB8nK6CXTIlw9kqs9KRB2FHmeZrWIh6tjaZE3kAEZCNPAbXjWkd6eYnqrS-odVdA_slU4wvyYBMAIc5U2KHYGzRLLojWNVDA?purpose=inline)


[The Last of Us | Apocalypse landscape, Apocalypse world, The last of us](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/RzsdofazJnO7ThuLR0bsJHhqaAZi-Ur6vRaZrphOsV7-wE-_joXXc3cfZayl9t-IxO_PTPfr_84T4LdRMpCn9XRlcUKuCbl_8JHUnHu9h6cn3IvoQB45KAOXj-9d8vvg7YzaOAhgFFP4-1BSDY1QD7mlqpojj7JDW7dH0Pk69Vk?purpose=inline)


14


The world must operate independently of the player.


- Settlements grow, decline, relocate, and collapse.
- Communities establish laws, customs, traditions, and governments.
- Factions expand territory and compete for resources.
- Trade routes emerge between distant settlements.
- Roads become safer or more dangerous over time.
- Infrastructure is repaired, repurposed, or abandoned.
- Crops are planted, harvested, traded, and stolen.
- Populations grow, migrate, become infected, or die.
- New generations are born who have never experienced the old world.
- Knowledge is preserved, lost, rediscovered, or deliberately suppressed.
- Wars begin and end without requiring player involvement.
- Entire regions can become uninhabitable or develop into thriving societies.


The player should be able to leave a settlement for several in-game months and return to discover that its leadership, population, economy, territory, and relationships have changed.


## 2. The emergence of new civilizations


There is no single correct way to rebuild society.


Different communities develop different philosophies about survival, infection, technology, freedom, and coexistence.


[Generador de nombres de asentamientos (The Last of Us)](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/1co3TSNpNZQIbElpAGvXvtdvu-XpjxadaRetlWUdbqnHIuyX7f1gSLnjI2zllAI6T545R6_JZWu8tvDbmL56qKXhlh5jWQAbbtAFR8UAmac0Usv4oHCIcQ0irBTpb1Yob-qeFJBgLQN-ri0lTSqJRwjrevGTm0HMMGbwZXigRa0?purpose=inline)


The Last Human Territories


Human-controlled settlements that believe infection must be eliminated. They establish strict laws, quarantine zones, military forces, and controlled borders.


[Futuristic cyborg city](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/AZSsFVSXcNOeHnWwFFPIRCYPTGIUShjNERNdf8kcZ4ixEGZ93JJj_6p_UI7Gw8lwR6ZhgsVvTmR0M_HLQZaTiF1Ier8wX99b2cYUW-pE_hCGzDgDk41l-nWfn9faXET_eiyxjKZXHlYDsmQBGiL81QMPQmpynD5erUM758qYihKZ_hIUurKcVGb9MIm0BpBN?purpose=inline)


The Undead Republics


Intelligent undead communities that have developed their own governments, economies, cultures, and systems of law. They reject the idea that being undead means being mindless.


[The Walking Dead Onslaught Doubles Down On Story, Ditches Co-Op](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/D-140Md2jPkmiR7cAwyM0xkKN-grGhnTjl7Dw2gReLSbUNGG-SYaQGillCPqzlhz5V5BSFS3svgwdB0-NaeQe1TSyT6zX_AR9BFaFDUmqAp34Q_usMQaYt6k5yb9z9m38O6yuJpiUymgeNQBK0nZjKR-pgo_VTOVbEoYCUvAqAQ?purpose=inline)


The Hybrid Enclaves


Communities where humans, infected survivors, and conscious undead attempt to coexist. Their societies are innovative but politically unstable.


[The Societal Rebirth: Life After the Apocalypse](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/ArLg8cSX0fTbfvfkZ1oZQRjwLha91pbQCSD4dahgTGTmKDT1sDm6Q7hszxdmJLc31c2Q2GCkLkIqB8nK6CXTIlw9kqs9KRB2FHmeZrWIh6tjaZE3kAEZCNPAbXjWkd6eYnqrS-odVdA_slU4wvyYBMAIc5U2KHYGzRLLojWNVDA?purpose=inline)


The Iron Dominion


Militarized territories governed by powerful leaders who control weapons, industry, food supplies, and protection. Security comes at the price of freedom.


[Offgrid Ecovillage Boreal Forest 2 | Eco city, Fantasy landscape, Eco village community](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/fuKK_hB6G4mGHDefRga2fe8YbfHLpB9QHwjMw4vRbZ2ONlUg2XWMhbXeRg9j1HCXhPqKXkKb8LTU110I3QmOC_FMdljYEnNkGXB4mREvMpaN333Im2hHnfbpV48BweBZK0fpCtzlZgr9Es_DyCF5B3gPJqXfMUYzqMHjqJ8ThDI?purpose=inline)


The Green Communion


Ecological communities that have abandoned much of the old world's industrial model. They cultivate land, preserve biodiversity, and develop new relationships with the changed ecosystem.


[Decrepit Underground Bunker & Eerie Green Area | Mysteries Unveiled | AI Art Generator | Easy-Peasy.AI](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/ZeVl0K5YK4TaKjkGfjp7ZzIqGMeNE_TEou_YkwHRrpwcfxrmhotEE__CS7Ki_odlVXTC80EViMACz1OrgbZX87p5dIsGjL8eivqUU26Q_CeWNLAWpG5PPLLxqVYjfy6vTHiEt0VwcMJfInTJvZsOSgsmBfFgiYhCYFtS8pYkTDc?purpose=inline)


The Remnant Directorate


Scientists, engineers, archivists, and former government personnel attempting to recover lost technology and understand the outbreak.


These are not merely quest-giving factions. They are functioning societies with competing interests, internal disagreements, political movements, economic systems, and evolving populations.


## 3. Civilization has an economy


[PIONER Economy & Trading: Make Money Faster](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/QJBTAD0yFXK-UB5XtUdtqNKqIsix915BMZxJgSjjMTKnn7c8nwq_Zx6iyWBX8K0Thfksjk6tP5F5WKqDzfS0MnicfV4PHU-0hHZsrElA-Gwstwe5rM4VykH5hWW1ARcvtaGKIzNkVuhARziXUrOqPnf-efIxgZ287Nr8ob7nsPw?purpose=inline)


[Forge Shed, Mac Smith](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/NbfJedyU2C0z8911_nwzX0iTmwPmFtx_WHSBr8H-5SYDfpffF_xHDvhdy-oN7kcYm_OrSuJaYWKELNbunzwGdq9s_hWwju3eafh7y_jvJq7wu0cFyKomfDWhTFHr0Hq6ex7WwMEWC4RBWjojmHeAawlSHD54BQmKS9rmzqXVTDM?purpose=inline)


[Techland releases Dying Light 2 trailer showcasing Xbox One, PS4 gameplay | Windows Central](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/sov5T4_j2JXUJuGTmKz9096u5vNQQP0Na5U_lmrg4DIjvyl4hOotVAjK6WE6i8J2B3UC3fPLpeliSdiY4krrzXHJPZF9Wk2hUb-foUEiLg5kHhu4losUMgB0F3t8j4Gjb7fUgFQStOm39Bc26-4FOQEPA7hWaWUIHb3ODA2ny6A?purpose=inline)


14


A civilization needs more than loot containers and crafting benches.


It needs production, distribution, labor, scarcity, trade, and demand.


Economic systems include:


- Food production and distribution.
- Clean water collection and treatment.
- Medicine manufacturing.
- Weapon and ammunition production.
- Clothing and armor production.
- Construction materials.
- Energy generation.
- Fuel production and distribution.
- Transportation and logistics.
- Skilled labor and apprenticeships.
- Trade agreements and commercial networks.
- Currency, barter, credit, and debt.
- Taxes, tariffs, protection payments, and black markets.
- Supply shortages, inflation, and economic collapse.
- Farming, cultivation, processing, and fictional cannabis-based industries.


A settlement that produces food but lacks medicine becomes dependent on neighboring communities. A settlement with electricity but no fuel reserves eventually loses power. A settlement that monopolizes water can become politically powerful.


The economy should create stories without needing scripted missions.


## 4. Politics, laws, and government


Who makes the rules when the old governments disappear?


Different societies should experiment with different political structures:


- Democracies.
- Councils.
- Tribal leadership.
- Military governments.
- Monarchies.
- Corporate-controlled territories.
- Religious or ideological movements.
- Independent communes.
- Anarchist settlements.
- AI-assisted governance.
- Undead-led governments.
- Human-undead coalitions.


Every government has policies, leaders, supporters, opponents, and consequences.


For example:


A settlement introduces mandatory infection registration. Some humans support it because they fear another outbreak. Conscious undead consider it discrimination. Hybrid citizens become politically divided. The policy triggers protests, black-market identity documents, migration, and eventually a possible uprising.


The player can influence these developments, but the world should not wait for the player to make every decision.


## 5. Population and generational simulation


This is one of the most important pieces.


Twenty years after the outbreak, not everyone remembers the old world.


There are different generations:


| Generation         | Experience                                              |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| The Old Worlders   | Remember civilization before the outbreak               |
| The Survivors      | Grew up during the collapse                             |
| The Rebuilders     | Were children during the outbreak and helped rebuild    |
| The Born After     | Have never known the world before Phoenix               |
| The Changed        | Infected or undead individuals with evolving identities |
| The New Generation | Born into the emerging civilizations                    |


Each generation develops different values, beliefs, skills, prejudices, and expectations.


Children born in fortified settlements may consider walls and armed patrols normal. Children raised among conscious undead may not understand why humans fear them. Some young people may romanticize the old world without understanding what destroyed it.


The world develops a history that the player did not personally experience.


## 6. Culture, religion, education, and identity


Civilization is not just buildings and armies. It is the creation of meaning.


The game world should develop:


- New religions and belief systems.
- Anti-undead and pro-undead movements.
- Hybrid identity movements.
- Festivals and public celebrations.
- Music, art, fashion, and entertainment.
- New languages, slang, and regional dialects.
- Schools and apprenticeship programs.
- Libraries and historical archives.
- Scientific institutions.
- Myths about the outbreak.
- Memorials to lost communities.
- Laws surrounding infection and transformation.
- New family structures and social customs.
- Cultural differences between regions.


Even the meaning of the word human can change.


Some societies may define humanity through biology. Others may define it through consciousness, empathy, memory, or the ability to make moral decisions.


That philosophical conflict should run through the entire game.


## 7. Technology and infrastructure


[Application of Photovoltaic Solar Energy Combined with Air-to-Water Generators in Post-Apocalyptic Safe Houses-www.atoh2o.com](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/B-NGakZT0zatKAtocSB1eg0SNst-dnuATSi2UdKipRRqi8MY6e6V_Pw7p3AHNUnUZVbq9hQkFz_abNpIgjNxNJINCLMR2zcw4sgNUaIagzrPE9gyMNXiLomTtfxNBUPhQRMGz2UYyQ0BzGte9feYGiJ--GVRav8IgJelqrU3HBw?purpose=inline)


[Metro Exodus 4K Wallpapers | Wallpapers.com](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/PeNHMpGR61wg-7SfTcRJ0uzh2r2gRtZDOdp6iweeIy2I_EES1CHzVUWv0mrwGfLYT2f2u8WP7T8JnxwT6fR5oXFBp_qyFz5Cmn-hTphxOjhVJ7hCYADB1xUk6vaRhMZ3mgqnR37UFzTmnj-H5DOHqyPGRCecUqlmkQn_mYZtyV8?purpose=inline)


[Works — The Farm - Run by artists, driven by art.](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/VHiQVRfsn_sEh7PjD_5puRQiCtrkOiGHXqCcVYnjyNitIvVKq2LaG8f5KlSIVXN8sv_UrpF7HrhNMZUCw6oPFr9KKbYAnAT76QxkQ2LsSM0Sk6GiBPS66wwRPuuG8NFlaHa-dTH5bMhrMUxrOskZUI_5g4VHm46efhReeus1GpM?purpose=inline)


15


The old world left behind technology, but technology requires maintenance, knowledge, energy, and materials.


Civilizations can develop:


- Solar and wind power networks.
- Microgrids and battery storage.
- Water purification systems.
- Agricultural automation.
- Radio communication networks.
- Local computer networks.
- Medical laboratories.
- Robotics and automated defenses.
- Manufacturing facilities.
- Transportation networks.
- Research and development centers.
- AI-assisted settlement management.
- Advanced prosthetics and biological research.
- Infection monitoring and treatment systems.


Technology should not automatically progress everywhere.


One region might operate advanced laboratories while another uses hand tools, windmills, and salvaged machinery.


Technology creates inequality, opportunity, conflict, and political power.


## 8. Ecology and the evolution of the world


The outbreak does not only change people. It changes the environment.


- Wildlife populations adapt.
- New predator-prey relationships emerge.
- Infected animals develop unusual behaviors.
- Vegetation reclaims abandoned cities.
- Ecosystems recover in some regions and collapse in others.
- Contaminated areas develop unique biological conditions.
- New diseases and parasites emerge.
- Weather and climate affect migration and settlement.
- Natural resources become scarce or newly available.
- Some regions become dangerous biological frontiers.


The environment should be a participant in civilization, not just scenery.


## 9. War, diplomacy, and international relationships


Civilizations do not always cooperate.


They compete over:


- Territory.
- Food.
- Water.
- Energy.
- Medicine.
- Technology.
- Transportation routes.
- Strategic infrastructure.
- Population and labor.
- Scientific discoveries.
- Control over infection research.


But war is not inevitable.


Factions can form alliances, negotiate treaties, exchange resources, establish borders, arrange marriages or political partnerships, share research, and create federations.


The player might prevent a war, start one, profit from one, or discover that a war has already ended before arriving.


A faction might even become powerful enough to unify several regions into a new nation.


## 10. The player as a civilization-shaping force


The player should have several possible identities within this simulation.


- Independent survivor.
- Merchant.
- Scientist.
- Engineer.
- Farmer.
- Hunter.
- Medic.
- Military commander.
- Diplomat.
- Political leader.
- Settlement founder.
- Faction leader.
- Industrialist.
- Revolutionary.
- Undead community representative.
- Hybrid civilization architect.


The player should be able to establish settlements, recruit populations, define laws, create trade networks, manage infrastructure, negotiate alliances, and shape the future.


But power should come with responsibility.


A settlement can fail because of poor leadership, corruption, disease, famine, economic mismanagement, civil unrest, or external threats.


## 11. The twenty-year timeline


## Twenty years after Phoenix


1. Year 0: The Outbreak


   Governments collapse, populations flee, infrastructure fails, and the infection spreads.
2. Years 1–3: The Collapse


   Survivors establish temporary shelters. Resources become scarce. The first organized groups emerge.
3. Years 4–7: The Fragmentation


   Settlements become permanent. Factions establish territory. Trade routes and local governments begin to form.
4. Years 8–12: The Reconstruction


   Agriculture, manufacturing, medicine, education, and infrastructure begin returning in selected regions.
5. Years 13–16: The New Order


   Civilizations develop distinct identities. Political systems mature. Human and undead societies compete, negotiate, and evolve.
6. Years 17–20: The Age of Evolution


   New generations challenge old beliefs. Hybrid societies gain influence. Advanced technologies and new biological discoveries reshape the balance of power.


The timeline is not a fixed sequence of scripted events. It is the historical foundation of the simulated world. Different regions may experience these developments at different speeds, and the player's actions can change what actually happens.


## 12. The civilization simulation engine


This is the technical heart of the concept.


The game needs a persistent world simulation that connects population, resources, politics, economics, ecology, and history.


## Civilization simulation architecture


WORLD SIMULATION ENGINE


Persistent world state and time progression


Population


Births, deaths, migration


Economy


Production, trade, scarcity


Politics


Laws, leaders, power


Factions


Diplomacy, conflict, alliances


Ecology


Wildlife, disease, climate


Technology


Research, industry, infrastructure


Culture


Education, beliefs, traditions


History


Events, consequences, legacy


WORLD EVENT GENERATOR


Emergent events, crises, opportunities, and historical changes


PLAYER INTERACTION


Influence, participate, observe, build, negotiate, or intervene


A practical implementation would use:


1. A persistent world-state database.
2. Individual settlement and faction records.
3. Population simulation with demographic summaries.
4. Resource production and consumption calculations.
5. Economic and trade calculations.
6. Political and diplomatic relationship models.
7. Environmental and infection simulation.
8. Time-step processing for world events.
9. Event history and consequence tracking.
10. Save/load and world-version management.


Not every NPC needs to be simulated every second. The engine can use different levels of simulation:


- Active simulation: Detailed updates around the player.
- Regional simulation: Periodic updates for nearby settlements.
- Background simulation: Aggregated calculations for distant civilizations.
- Historical simulation: Recorded events and long-term consequences.


This keeps a massive world manageable without making it feel static.


## 13. The real endgame: what becomes of civilization?


[Climate 🌳](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/ZaHWcPGSg7MdR70dSNYw4GN9xrhfb1prwC6ted6dGmivWmn4Ob4zdsBbH_Z4hs1sKbD-rN9CqDMux3JKRSBBQ59ZLNqVLcHoSbT_-bNFOS3md-FAi8SoIAK7r43oTjPGb9ueF8h7YwNDTG0did5byKoMATtT_d_M6fMJBm0PUlw?purpose=inline)


[Dystopia RPG Review: Social Class Systems](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/p9LScHaR88xJx9-YQbow9-Kf8msFwNuj5-jjKbbXNH3F3-2P9nY_lwv72MU9HAzoRuhNnlHXp78tDIVNrsYQxEWAF51IanQgLzsuG4nN7-UnrF7ksB0UnzDYi4oPGZtwPPXfCRN_9WUhHD_Sms_nUieYfhME2Nkn0zUhejYvSM8?purpose=inline)


[Explore Stories by Category on TxtTale](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/K_T5l4ff7EtDhtOuB6COsqR-G13ALqnQ2i8FYUB0FdwjWnZ2c04a8qMP2mjCU04OPtiQTfZgM4vPJ4qOSUvL_MkSk6NJdztlA5o6jAEFwyqCOfct8a8vqJ6PAIr74gAR74IhmQ9ppP6VgAUs8AgtU-G50SsvkUBLO1bvEKSdcxE?purpose=inline)


12


There should be no single predetermined ending.


The world could develop into:


- A restored human civilization.
- A network of independent human settlements.
- An undead-led civilization.
- A unified human-undead society.
- A hybrid civilization that transcends the original species divide.
- A technologically advanced authoritarian regime.
- A collection of isolated tribal societies.
- A world where nature dominates the ruins.
- A new civilization that has forgotten humanity's origins.
- A world that collapses into another dark age.


And the most interesting possibility?


The player might spend twenty years building a peaceful civilization, only to discover that the next generation wants something entirely different.


That is what makes the simulation compelling. The world has a life of its own.


## The defining vision


Phoenix Blaze becomes a post-apocalyptic civilization simulator wrapped inside an open-world survival, crafting, exploration, and conscious-zombie evolution RPG.


The player begins by trying to survive the end of the old world.


Eventually, the player discovers that survival was only the beginning.


The real game is discovering what comes next.


You aren't just surviving the apocalypse. You're participating in the birth of the next civilization. 🌍


# High and Undead / Phoenix Blaze


## Complete Gameplay Statistics, Balancing & Simulation Specification


This is the numerical foundation of the game. Every weapon, enemy, survival meter, mutation, skill, faction, settlement, vendor, and economic system needs defined values and predictable rules.


The goal is to create a game where numbers interact with one another rather than operating as isolated mechanics. Weapon damage affects survival. Infection affects combat. Bloodlust affects decision-making. Reputation affects trade. Trade affects settlement growth. Settlement growth affects faction power. Faction power changes the world.


All values below are proposed starting balance values for the game, not measurements of an existing implementation. They establish a consistent baseline that can later be tuned through testing.


# 1. Combat system


## 1.1 Core combat statistics


Use a standardized player and enemy combat model.


| Statistic                   | Starting value | Description              |
| --------------------------- | -------------- | ------------------------ |
| Player health               | 100 HP         | Maximum base health      |
| Player stamina              | 100 SP         | Physical action resource |
| Base melee damage           | 12             | Unarmed attack           |
| Base movement speed         | 100%           | Normal movement          |
| Base attack speed           | 100%           | Standard attack timing   |
| Base critical chance        | 5%             | Chance of critical hit   |
| Base critical multiplier    | 1.50x          | Critical damage          |
| Base armor                  | 0              | No protection            |
| Base damage resistance      | 0%             | No damage reduction      |
| Base stamina regeneration   | 8 SP/sec       | Out of combat            |
| Combat stamina regeneration | 4 SP/sec       | During combat            |
| Base dodge cost             | 18 SP          | Stamina consumed         |
| Base heavy attack cost      | 25 SP          | Stamina consumed         |


### 1.2 Weapon damage


| Weapon class     | Damage | Attack speed | Stamina cost | Range |
| ---------------- | ------ | ------------ | ------------ | ----- |
| Fists            | 12     | 1.00/s       | 8            | 1.2 m |
| Improvised knife | 18     | 1.25/s       | 10           | 1.5 m |
| Hunting knife    | 24     | 1.40/s       | 10           | 1.5 m |
| Machete          | 32     | 0.95/s       | 16           | 2.0 m |
| Baseball bat     | 28     | 0.90/s       | 15           | 2.0 m |
| Fire axe         | 45     | 0.65/s       | 22           | 2.2 m |
| Crowbar          | 30     | 0.85/s       | 16           | 1.8 m |
| Spear            | 35     | 0.80/s       | 18           | 3.0 m |
| Improvised bow   | 30     | 0.65/s       | 12           | 20 m  |
| Crossbow         | 55     | 0.40/s       | 15           | 30 m  |
| Handgun          | 35     | 2.50/s       | 0            | 25 m  |
| Revolver         | 48     | 1.50/s       | 0            | 30 m  |
| Shotgun          | 90     | 0.80/s       | 0            | 12 m  |
| Assault rifle    | 28     | 8.00/s       | 0            | 60 m  |
| Hunting rifle    | 85     | 0.70/s       | 0            | 100 m |


Firearm ammunition, reload time, weapon noise, recoil, accuracy, penetration, and durability are separate weapon attributes.


### 1.3 Damage calculation


The basic damage calculation:


\\[ D = W \times M \times C \times L \\]


Where:


- \\(D\\) = final damage.
- \\(W\\) = weapon base damage.
- \\(M\\) = attack or skill multiplier.
- \\(C\\) = critical multiplier, normally 1.0 or 1.5.
- \\(L\\) = location multiplier.


Example hit-location multipliers:


| Hit location       | Multiplier |
| ------------------ | ---------- |
| Body               | 1.00x      |
| Arm                | 0.75x      |
| Leg                | 0.70x      |
| Head               | 1.75x      |
| Weak point         | 2.00x      |
| Armored weak point | 0.75x      |


Armor is applied after the raw damage calculation:


\\[ D\_{final} = \max(1,\ D - A \times P) \\]


Where \\(A\\) is armor value and \\(P\\) is the weapon's armor penetration factor.


For damage types using percentage resistance:


\\[ D\_{final}=D\_{raw}\times(1-R) \\]


Resistance is capped at 80% to prevent ordinary builds from becoming invulnerable.


## 1.4 Critical hit rules


| Rule                          | Starting value |
| ----------------------------- | -------------- |
| Base critical chance          | 5%             |
| Maximum critical chance       | 60%            |
| Base critical multiplier      | 1.50x          |
| Precision critical multiplier | 2.00x          |
| Headshot multiplier           | 1.75x          |
| Stealth attack bonus          | +50% damage    |
| Critical resistance cap       | 75%            |


Critical chance formula:


\\[ C = \min(60,\ C_b + C_s + C_e + C_m) \\]


Where:


- \\(C_b\\) = base critical chance.
- \\(C_s\\) = skill bonus.
- \\(C_e\\) = equipment bonus.
- \\(C_m\\) = temporary mutation or effect bonus.


Critical hits should be calculated separately from headshots so that the two effects can interact through a controlled multiplier cap.


## 1.5 Armor values


| Armor type            | Armor value | Movement penalty | Noise  |
| --------------------- | ----------- | ---------------- | ------ |
| Clothing              | 2           | 0%               | Low    |
| Leather               | 6           | 2%               | Low    |
| Reinforced clothing   | 10          | 3%               | Low    |
| Scrap armor           | 15          | 7%               | Medium |
| Tactical armor        | 22          | 5%               | Medium |
| Military armor        | 30          | 10%              | High   |
| Heavy plated armor    | 40          | 18%              | High   |
| Specialized bio-armor | 35          | 5%               | Low    |


Armor should have separate protection values for:


- Blunt damage.
- Piercing damage.
- Slashing damage.
- Ballistic damage.
- Explosive damage.
- Biological damage.
- Environmental damage.


## 1.6 Enemy health values


| Enemy type       | Health | Damage | Threat     |
| ---------------- | ------ | ------ | ---------- |
| Infected crawler | 45     | 8      | Low        |
| Shambler         | 70     | 12     | Low        |
| Runner           | 90     | 18     | Medium     |
| Stalker          | 110    | 22     | Medium     |
| Brute            | 250    | 35     | High       |
| Spitter          | 130    | 20     | High       |
| Screamer         | 160    | 15     | High       |
| Armored undead   | 350    | 30     | High       |
| Mutated hunter   | 400    | 45     | Very high  |
| Alpha infected   | 750    | 55     | Boss       |
| Horde champion   | 1,200  | 65     | Boss       |
| Apex mutation    | 2,500  | 85     | World boss |


Enemy statistics should scale by difficulty, region danger, mutation stage, and world progression.


## 1.7 Stamina costs and cooldowns


| Action           | Stamina cost | Cooldown |
| ---------------- | ------------ | -------- |
| Light attack     | 8            | 0.30 sec |
| Heavy attack     | 25           | 1.50 sec |
| Dodge            | 18           | 1.00 sec |
| Sprint           | 10/sec       | None     |
| Block            | 5/sec        | None     |
| Parry            | 15           | 2.00 sec |
| Stealth takedown | 20           | 3.00 sec |
| Climb            | 8            | 0.50 sec |
| Jump             | 10           | 0.50 sec |
| Grapple escape   | 25           | 4.00 sec |


Actions should be disabled when the player lacks sufficient stamina, except for emergency actions explicitly designed to work at a cost.


# 2. Bloodlust and undead instincts


Bloodlust is one of the defining mechanics of High and Undead. It represents the player's growing undead instincts and the conflict between biological urges and conscious control.
