It must be more than another hunger bar. It should influence perception, behavior, combat, social interactions, and the player's ability to retain humanity.


## 2.1 Bloodlust statistics


## Bloodlust meter


Normal control


0–24


Elevated instincts


25–49


Predatory state


50–74


Near loss of control


75–99


Instinct override


100


Conceptual thresholds. The actual meter should be displayed with numerical and accessible status information.


| Bloodlust | State         | Gameplay effect                      |
| --------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------ |
| 0–9       | Calm          | Normal behavior                      |
| 10–24     | Stirring      | Enhanced smell sensitivity           |
| 25–39     | Agitated      | Increased aggression sensitivity     |
| 40–49     | Hungry        | Food and blood detection increases   |
| 50–64     | Predatory     | Combat damage +5%                    |
| 65–74     | Frenzied      | Combat damage +10%, social penalties |
| 75–84     | Unstable      | Reduced control and dialogue options |
| 85–94     | Near override | Automatic instinct checks            |
| 95–99     | Critical      | Severe control instability           |
| 100       | Override      | Temporary loss of voluntary control  |


## 2.2 Bloodlust increase rates


| Trigger                             | Bloodlust increase |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------------ |
| Every 10 seconds without feeding    | +1                 |
| Smelling fresh blood                | +3                 |
| Seeing injured humans               | +2                 |
| Being attacked                      | +1                 |
| Taking severe damage                | +4                 |
| Killing a human                     | +12                |
| Killing an infected enemy           | +3                 |
| Consuming blood                     | +5                 |
| Entering a crowded human settlement | +2                 |
| Prolonged starvation                | +2/min             |
| Certain mutations                   | Variable           |
| Special story events                | Variable           |


These are initial tuning values. Multiple triggers occurring simultaneously should be capped to avoid instant, unavoidable escalation.


## 2.3 Bloodlust decay rates


| Condition                           | Reduction |
| ----------------------------------- | --------- |
| Resting in a safe location          | -2/min    |
| Sleeping                            | -4/min    |
| Consuming suitable food             | -5        |
| Using a fictional stabilizer        | -15       |
| Meditation or conditioning skill    | -3/min    |
| Remaining away from blood stimuli   | -1/min    |
| Completing a self-control challenge | -10       |
| Certain mutations                   | Variable  |


Bloodlust cannot fall below zero.


## 2.4 Control-loss formula


Calculate a control-risk score:


\\[ R = \max(0,B-70) \\]


\\[ P = \min(95,\ R \times 2 + S + T - W) \\]


Where:


- \\(B\\) = bloodlust from 0 to 100.
- \\(S\\) = stress contribution.
- \\(T\\) = triggering environmental stimuli.
- \\(W\\) = self-control and willpower reduction.
- \\(P\\) = control-loss probability, expressed as a percentage.


A control check occurs every 10 seconds while bloodlust is 75 or higher.


Control-loss severity:


| Bloodlust | Possible behavior                      |
| --------- | -------------------------------------- |
| 75–84     | Delayed commands, aggressive responses |
| 85–94     | Impulsive movement, targeting fixation |
| 95–99     | Uncontrolled attacks become possible   |
| 100       | Full temporary instinct override       |


The player should retain opportunities to resist through skills, equipment, preparation, and decisions.


## 2.5 Bloodlust penalties


| Bloodlust | Penalty                                   |
| --------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| 25+       | Human dialogue checks -2                  |
| 40+       | Social suspicion +5                       |
| 50+       | Negotiation effectiveness -5%             |
| 65+       | Reputation loss risk increases            |
| 75+       | Voluntary actions may be interrupted      |
| 85+       | Companion trust decreases during episodes |
| 95+       | Significant risk of hostile actions       |
| 100       | Temporary player-control override         |


## 2.6 Bloodlust benefits


Undead instincts should also offer advantages.


| Bloodlust | Benefit                          |
| --------- | -------------------------------- |
| 25+       | Scent detection +5%              |
| 40+       | Enemy tracking +10%              |
| 50+       | Melee damage +5%                 |
| 65+       | Melee damage +10%                |
| 75+       | Threat detection +15%            |
| 85+       | Pain resistance +15%             |
| 95+       | Temporary damage resistance +20% |


These benefits should be balanced against control risks, social consequences, and resource consumption.


# 3. Infection, transformation, and mutation


Infection is a persistent biological progression system.


The player begins human, becomes infected, and gradually evolves into a conscious human-undead hybrid. Transformation should affect appearance, physical attributes, abilities, social interactions, and survival requirements.


## 3.1 Transformation stages


| Stage   | Infection | Description            |
| ------- | --------- | ---------------------- |
| Stage 0 | 0–9%      | Uninfected             |
| Stage 1 | 10–24%    | Initial infection      |
| Stage 2 | 25–39%    | Awakening              |
| Stage 3 | 40–59%    | Visible transformation |
| Stage 4 | 60–74%    | Advanced hybrid        |
| Stage 5 | 75–89%    | Evolved hybrid         |
| Stage 6 | 90–100%   | Transcendent undead    |


## 3.2 Growth rates


| Trigger                              | Infection increase |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------------ |
| Initial infection event              | +10%               |
| Infected wound                       | +2–5%              |
| Exposure to contaminated environment | +1%                |
| Severe infection exposure            | +5–10%             |
| Mutation event                       | Variable           |
| Special biological event             | Variable           |


Infection should not automatically increase every second. Its growth should depend on exposure, progression events, biological traits, and player choices.


## 3.3 Appearance thresholds


| Infection | Visual changes                |
| --------- | ----------------------------- |
| 10%       | Subtle discoloration          |
| 20%       | Eye changes                   |
| 30%       | Vein visibility               |
| 40%       | Skin texture changes          |
| 50%       | Noticeable facial changes     |
| 60%       | Advanced skin and eye changes |
| 70%       | Distinct hybrid appearance    |
| 80%       | Major biological changes      |
| 90%       | Advanced undead features      |
| 100%      | Fully evolved appearance      |


Appearance changes should support character customization and different mutation paths.


## 3.4 Transformation penalties and bonuses


| Stage | Bonus                       | Penalty                         |
| ----- | --------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| 0     | Normal human social access  | Normal human limitations        |
| 1     | Increased pain resistance   | Mild fatigue                    |
| 2     | Improved sensory perception | Increased bloodlust             |
| 3     | Improved physical strength  | Human suspicion                 |
| 4     | Improved recovery           | Increased biological needs      |
| 5     | Advanced undead abilities   | Severe social restrictions      |
| 6     | Specialized evolved powers  | Significant humanity challenges |


## 3.5 Humanity score


Use a separate humanity score from 0 to 100.


| Humanity | Identity                     |
| -------- | ---------------------------- |
| 90–100   | Strongly human               |
| 75–89    | Mostly human                 |
| 60–74    | Balanced identity            |
| 40–59    | Divided identity             |
| 20–39    | Predominantly undead         |
| 1–19     | Severely diminished humanity |
| 0        | Humanity collapse            |


Humanity changes through decisions, relationships, behavior, self-control, and significant narrative events.


Suggested adjustments:


| Action                           | Humanity change |
| -------------------------------- | --------------- |
| Protect an innocent survivor     | +5              |
| Keep a difficult promise         | +3              |
| Rescue a companion               | +4              |
| Show mercy to an enemy           | +2              |
| Complete a humanitarian mission  | +5              |
| Deliberately kill an innocent    | -15             |
| Abandon a companion              | -8              |
| Lose control and attack a human  | -10             |
| Embrace a major undead instinct  | -5              |
| Resist a major bloodlust episode | +4              |


Humanity should not simply be a morality score. A character can be compassionate, ruthless, selfish, or loyal regardless of biological transformation.


# 4. Skills and progression


Skills should reward actual gameplay activity while allowing players to specialize.


## 4.1 Skill categories


| Category     | Skills                                            |
| ------------ | ------------------------------------------------- |
| Combat       | Melee, firearms, archery, defense                 |
| Survival     | Scavenging, hunting, tracking, endurance          |
| Crafting     | Weapons, armor, medicine, engineering             |
| Construction | Building, fortification, electrical systems       |
| Stealth      | Concealment, silent movement, infiltration        |
| Social       | Negotiation, leadership, persuasion, intimidation |
| Science      | Biology, infection research, medicine, chemistry  |
| Undead       | Bloodlust control, senses, regeneration, mutation |
| Agriculture  | Farming, cultivation, harvesting, processing      |
| Commerce     | Trading, logistics, procurement, bargaining       |


## 4.2 XP requirements


Use a progressively increasing experience curve:


\\[ XP\_{next}=100+25L+10L^2 \\]


Where \\(L\\) is the current skill level.


| Level | XP required for next level |
| ----- | -------------------------- |
| 1     | 135                        |
| 2     | 190                        |
| 3     | 265                        |
| 4     | 360                        |
| 5     | 475                        |
| 10    | 1,350                      |
| 15    | 2,725                      |
| 20    | 5,100                      |
| 25    | 7,625                      |
| 30    | 9,850                      |


XP requirements are per-level costs, not total cumulative XP.


## 4.3 Skill tiers


| Tier | Levels | Classification |
| ---- | ------ | -------------- |
| 1    | 1–5    | Novice         |
| 2    | 6–10   | Apprentice     |
| 3    | 11–15  | Skilled        |
| 4    | 16–20  | Expert         |
| 5    | 21–25  | Master         |
| 6    | 26–30  | Legendary      |


## 4.4 Numerical perk values


| Skill    | Tier | Bonus               |
| -------- | ---- | ------------------- |
| Melee    | 1    | +3% damage          |
| Melee    | 2    | +6% damage          |
| Melee    | 3    | +10% damage         |
| Melee    | 4    | +15% damage         |
| Melee    | 5    | +20% damage         |
| Melee    | 6    | +25% damage         |
| Firearms | 1    | +2% accuracy        |
| Firearms | 2    | +5% accuracy        |
| Firearms | 3    | +8% accuracy        |
| Firearms | 4    | +12% accuracy       |
| Firearms | 5    | +16% accuracy       |
| Firearms | 6    | +20% accuracy       |
| Stealth  | 1    | -3% detection       |
| Stealth  | 2    | -6% detection       |
| Stealth  | 3    | -10% detection      |
| Stealth  | 4    | -15% detection      |
| Stealth  | 5    | -20% detection      |
| Stealth  | 6    | -25% detection      |
| Crafting | 1    | -3% material cost   |
| Crafting | 2    | -5% material cost   |
| Crafting | 3    | -8% material cost   |
| Crafting | 4    | -12% material cost  |
| Crafting | 5    | -16% material cost  |
| Crafting | 6    | -20% material cost  |
| Survival | 1    | +3% resource yield  |
| Survival | 2    | +5% resource yield  |
| Survival | 3    | +8% resource yield  |
| Survival | 4    | +12% resource yield |
| Survival | 5    | +16% resource yield |
| Survival | 6    | +20% resource yield |


These bonuses should have category-specific caps to prevent stacking from breaking the game.


## 4.5 Example specialization builds


- The Reaper: Melee, endurance, bloodlust mastery.
- The Ghost: Stealth, tracking, silent weapons.
- The Engineer: Crafting, construction, electrical systems.
- The Alchemist: Biology, medicine, chemistry, mutation research.
- The Warlord: Leadership, firearms, intimidation.
- The Merchant: Bargaining, logistics, reputation.
- The Hybrid: Transformation, regeneration, sensory abilities.


# 5. Reputation, trust, and faction relationships


Reputation and trust must be separate systems.


Reputation represents what a community believes about the player's identity and actions.


Trust represents how much a specific person or organization is willing to rely on the player.


A player can be feared by a faction but trusted by an individual within it.


## 5.1 Reputation values


Use a reputation score from -1,000 to +1,000 for each faction.


| Score          | Classification       |
| -------------- | -------------------- |
| -1,000 to -800 | Extermination target |
| -799 to -600   | Hated                |
| -599 to -300   | Hostile              |
| -299 to -100   | Distrusted           |
| -99 to +99     | Neutral              |
| +100 to +299   | Recognized           |
| +300 to +499   | Respected            |
| +500 to +699   | Honored              |
| +700 to +899   | Allied               |
| +900 to +1,000 | Legendary            |


## 5.2 Reputation changes


| Action                         | Reputation change |
| ------------------------------ | ----------------- |
| Complete minor faction task    | +10               |
| Complete major mission         | +35               |
| Donate essential supplies      | +15               |
| Defend settlement              | +25               |
| Establish trade agreement      | +20               |
| Rescue faction member          | +20               |
| Betray faction member          | -50               |
| Steal faction supplies         | -20               |
| Attack faction guard           | -40               |
| Kill faction leader            | -150              |
| Destroy faction infrastructure | -100              |
| Break treaty                   | -75               |
| Commit mass violence           | -200              |


Reputation changes should vary by witness, evidence, faction ideology, and the availability of information.


An action that nobody witnesses should not automatically become public knowledge.


## 5.3 Trust values


Individual trust uses a 0–100 score.


| Trust | Relationship             |
| ----- | ------------------------ |
| 0–9   | Enemy                    |
| 10–19 | Deep distrust            |
| 20–39 | Suspicious               |
| 40–59 | Neutral                  |
| 60–74 | Friendly                 |
| 75–89 | Trusted                  |
| 90–99 | Loyal                    |
| 100   | Unconditional confidence |


Suggested trust changes:


| Action                      | Trust change         |
| --------------------------- | -------------------- |
| Keep a promise              | +5                   |
| Complete a personal request | +10                  |
| Rescue the individual       | +15                  |
| Share resources             | +3                   |
| Protect the individual      | +8                   |
| Lie successfully            | +1 to +3 temporarily |
| Get caught lying            | -15                  |
| Steal from the individual   | -20                  |
| Abandon the individual      | -25                  |
| Attack the individual       | -50                  |
| Betray the individual       | -40                  |


## 5.4 Hostility thresholds


| Faction reputation | Default behavior          |
| ------------------ | ------------------------- |
| -1,000 to -800     | Kill-on-sight orders      |
| -799 to -600       | Aggressive patrols        |
| -599 to -300       | Hostile interactions      |
| -299 to -100       | Suspicious interactions   |
| -99 to +99         | Neutral interactions      |
| +100 to +299       | Basic cooperation         |
| +300 to +499       | Improved trade            |
| +500 to +699       | Special missions          |
| +700 to +899       | Alliance opportunities    |
| +900 to +1,000     | Major political influence |


Faction behavior should also depend on ideology, leadership, current conflicts, local laws, and the player's biological identity.


## 5.5 Human, undead, and hybrid reputation


Maintain three identity-related relationship modifiers:


- Human acceptance.
- Undead acceptance.
- Hybrid acceptance.


These should influence access to communities, dialogue, trade, recruitment, political roles, and social opportunities.


They must not replace the main faction reputation system.


# 6. Economy and trade simulation


The economy should make the world feel inhabited and interconnected.


Every settlement needs production, consumption, storage, population demand, trade relationships, and resource availability.


## 6.1 Core economic resources


| Resource     | Economic purpose                    |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------- |
| Food         | Population survival                 |
| Water        | Population survival and agriculture |
| Medicine     | Healthcare                          |
| Fuel         | Transportation and machinery        |
| Electricity  | Infrastructure and production       |
| Metal        | Construction and manufacturing      |
| Wood         | Construction and fuel               |
| Cloth        | Clothing and equipment              |
| Chemicals    | Medicine and production             |
| Ammunition   | Defense and combat                  |
| Components   | Machinery and electronics           |
| Seeds        | Agriculture                         |
| Tools        | Production and maintenance          |
| Knowledge    | Research and technology             |
| Luxury goods | Morale and trade                    |


## 6.2 Trade values


Use a common base currency called Scrap Credits (SC).


Proposed starting reference values, not fixed market prices.


| Item                     | Base value |
| ------------------------ | ---------- |
| Scrap metal              | 2 SC       |
| Wood bundle              | 3 SC       |
| Cloth bundle             | 4 SC       |
| Clean water              | 5 SC       |
| Basic food ration        | 8 SC       |
| Seeds                    | 10 SC      |
| Basic medicine           | 20 SC      |
| Mechanical components    | 25 SC      |
| Ammunition bundle        | 30 SC      |
| Weapon parts             | 35 SC      |
| Fuel container           | 40 SC      |
| Medical kit              | 60 SC      |
| Advanced electronics     | 100 SC     |
| Rare research material   | 150 SC     |
| Specialized bio-material | 200 SC     |


## 6.3 Dynamic pricing formula


\\[ P = B \times S \times D \times R \times F \\]


Where:


- \\(P\\) = final item price.
- \\(B\\) = base item value.
- \\(S\\) = scarcity multiplier.
- \\(D\\) = demand multiplier.
- \\(R\\) = regional modifier.
- \\(F\\) = faction or vendor modifier.


Suggested multipliers:


| Condition         | Multiplier |
| ----------------- | ---------- |
| Abundant supply   | 0.70x      |
| Normal supply     | 1.00x      |
| Limited supply    | 1.25x      |
| Scarce supply     | 1.75x      |
| Critical shortage | 2.50x      |
| Surplus demand    | 1.20x      |
| Extreme demand    | 1.60x      |
| Faction discount  | 0.90x      |
| Faction penalty   | 1.25x      |


Clamp the combined multiplier between 0.40x and 4.00x under normal trading conditions. Exceptional events can temporarily exceed this range.


## 6.4 Vendor inventories


Vendors should have distinct personalities and commercial specialties.


| Vendor type              | Inventory                                         |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------- |
| General trader           | Food, water, basic tools                          |
| Weapons dealer           | Firearms, melee weapons, ammunition               |
| Medical supplier         | Medicine, bandages, medical equipment             |
| Mechanic                 | Parts, fuel, tools, machinery                     |
| Farmer                   | Food, seeds, agricultural supplies                |
| Black-market dealer      | Rare, restricted, and illicit fictional goods     |
| Research merchant        | Scientific materials, samples, equipment          |
| Traveling merchant       | Random regional goods                             |
| Settlement quartermaster | Bulk resources and supplies                       |
| Undead trader            | Specialized biological and mutation-related goods |


## 6.5 Vendor inventory generation


A vendor's available quantity can be generated from:


\\[ Q = B_q \times S_m \times R_m \times T_m \\]


Where:


- \\(Q\\) = available quantity.
- \\(B_q\\) = vendor's base stock.
- \\(S_m\\) = settlement production multiplier.
- \\(R_m\\) = regional resource multiplier.
- \\(T_m\\) = time-based restock multiplier.


Example:


| Vendor            | Item           | Base quantity | Restock  |
| ----------------- | -------------- | ------------- | -------- |
| General trader    | Food           | 30            | 24 hours |
| General trader    | Water          | 40            | 12 hours |
| Weapons dealer    | Ammunition     | 100           | 48 hours |
| Medical supplier  | Medicine       | 15            | 72 hours |
| Mechanic          | Components     | 25            | 48 hours |
| Farmer            | Seeds          | 50            | 7 days   |
| Research merchant | Rare materials | 5             | 7 days   |


Stock should be influenced by actual supply chains, not only by a timer.


## 6.6 Supply generation


Each settlement has a production and consumption rate.


\\[ S\_{next}=S\_{current}+G-C-T-L \\]


Where:


- \\(S\_{current}\\) = current stored supply.
- \\(G\\) = generated supply.
- \\(C\\) = consumed supply.
- \\(T\\) = supply traded or transferred.
- \\(L\\) = losses from spoilage, damage, theft, or disasters.


Example settlement production:


| Production facility      | Output per day  |
| ------------------------ | --------------- |
| Small farm               | 20 food         |
| Medium farm              | 60 food         |
| Large farm               | 150 food        |
| Water collector          | 30 water        |
| Water purification plant | 100 water       |
| Small workshop           | 10 components   |
| Industrial workshop      | 40 components   |
| Medical laboratory       | 5 medicine      |
| Solar array              | 50 energy units |
| Wind generator           | 35 energy units |


## 6.7 Population consumption


| Population size | Food per day | Water per day |
| --------------- | ------------ | ------------- |
| 5               | 5            | 10            |
| 10              | 10           | 20            |
| 25              | 25           | 50            |
| 50              | 50           | 100           |
| 100             | 100          | 200           |
| 250             | 250          | 500           |
| 500             | 500          | 1,000         |


These baseline rates assume one food unit and two water units per person per day. Special biological needs, disease, climate, and population types modify consumption.


## 6.8 Settlement economic status


| Economic score | Condition  |
| -------------- | ---------- |
| 0–19           | Collapsing |
| 20–39          | Critical   |
| 40–59          | Struggling |
| 60–74          | Stable     |
| 75–89          | Prosperous |
| 90–100         | Thriving   |


Economic score can be calculated from:


- Food security: 25%.
- Water security: 20%.
- Production capacity: 15%.
- Trade access: 15%.
- Energy availability: 10%.
- Infrastructure condition: 10%.
- Financial stability: 5%.


# 7. Civilization-wide simulation values


These statistics connect individual gameplay with the twenty-year civilization simulation.


## 7.1 Settlement population tiers


| Population   | Settlement type     |
| ------------ | ------------------- |
| 1–10         | Hideout             |
| 11–30        | Camp                |
| 31–75        | Outpost             |
| 76–150       | Village             |
| 151–400      | Town                |
| 401–1,000    | Large town          |
| 1,001–3,000  | City                |
| 3,001–10,000 | Major city          |
| 10,001+      | Regional metropolis |


## 7.2 Settlement stability


| Stability | State             |
| --------- | ----------------- |
| 0–9       | Collapse imminent |
| 10–24     | Severe unrest     |
| 25–39     | Unstable          |
| 40–59     | Fragile           |
| 60–74     | Functional        |
| 75–89     | Stable            |
| 90–100    | Highly resilient  |


## 7.3 Civilization event probabilities


Example baseline probabilities per simulated 30-day period:


| Event                     | Base probability |
| ------------------------- | ---------------- |
| Resource shortage         | 15%              |
| Trade dispute             | 10%              |
| Leadership dispute        | 5%               |
| Disease outbreak          | 3%               |
| Faction recruitment drive | 10%              |
| Settlement expansion      | 8%               |
| Migration event           | 12%              |
| Infrastructure failure    | 6%               |
| Political uprising        | 2%               |
| Major faction conflict    | 2%               |


These are starting event probabilities, not independent guarantees. The simulation should modify them based on local conditions, history, and dependencies.


# 8. Numerical rules that must remain consistent


The game needs centralized balancing rules so that different systems do not contradict one another.


| System               | Rule                                                    |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| Player health        | Base 100 HP                                             |
| Player stamina       | Base 100 SP                                             |
| Critical chance      | Maximum 60%                                             |
| Damage resistance    | Maximum 80%                                             |
| Bloodlust            | 0–100                                                   |
| Infection            | 0–100%                                                  |
| Humanity             | 0–100                                                   |
| Individual trust     | 0–100                                                   |
| Faction reputation   | -1,000 to +1,000                                        |
| Settlement economy   | 0–100                                                   |
| Settlement stability | 0–100                                                   |
| Skill level          | 1–30                                                    |
| Skill tiers          | 6                                                       |
| Currency             | Scrap Credits                                           |
| Time simulation      | Real-time local simulation with accelerated world ticks |
| World history        | Persistent and event-driven                             |


## 9. Central balancing configuration


All numerical values should eventually live in centralized configuration files rather than being scattered across individual JavaScript scripts.


A suggested structure:


```
config/
├── balance/
│   ├── combat.json
│   ├── weapons.json
│   ├── armor.json
│   ├── enemies.json
│   ├── stamina.json
│   ├── bloodlust.json
│   ├── infection.json
│   ├── humanity.json
│   ├── mutations.json
│   ├── skills.json
│   ├── experience.json
│   ├── perks.json
│   ├── reputation.json
│   ├── trust.json
│   ├── factions.json
│   ├── economy.json
│   ├── trade.json
│   ├── vendors.json
│   ├── resources.json
│   ├── settlements.json
│   └── civilization.json
└── schemas/
    ├── combat.schema.json
    ├── character.schema.json
    ├── economy.schema.json
    └── civilization.schema.json
```


# High and Undead / Phoenix Blaze


## The Complete Content & World-Building Data Bible


This is the next major layer of the game: turning the concepts into actual game-ready content.


The difference is important. Saying the game has blood crafting, cannabis genetics, animals, enemies, regions, and civilization simulation describes the systems. Building the data bible means defining what exists, what it does, where it appears, how it behaves, what it produces, and how it affects everything else.


I would organize this into five connected content databases:


1. Blood & Biological Systems
2. Cannabis Genetics, Cultivation & Processing
3. Complete Crafting and Item Catalog
4. Creatures, Enemies & Wildlife
5. World Geography, Population & Civilization Simulation


The values below are proposed original game-design data. Fictional recipes and biological effects are designed as gameplay mechanics, not real-world medical or cultivation instructions.


# 1. Blood System


The Blood System should be a complete survival, crafting, research, and transformation mechanic.


Blood is not just a consumable. It is a biological resource with different characteristics, qualities, compatibility ratings, contamination risks, and mutation interactions.


## 1.1 Blood source catalog


Create at least 10 blood-source classifications, each with unique gameplay properties.


| ID      | Blood source             | Rarity    | Quality | Primary gameplay property      |
| ------- | ------------------------ | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------ |
| BLO-001 | Human survivor           | Common    | 60      | Balanced biological resource   |
| BLO-002 | Infected human           | Common    | 45      | Infection progression          |
| BLO-003 | Fresh infected           | Uncommon  | 65      | Strong undead compatibility    |
| BLO-004 | Mutated infected         | Rare      | 75      | Mutation research              |
| BLO-005 | Alpha infected           | Very rare | 90      | Advanced mutation catalyst     |
| BLO-006 | Hybrid blood             | Rare      | 85      | Hybrid compatibility           |
| BLO-007 | Animal blood             | Common    | 40      | Basic biological resource      |
| BLO-008 | Predatory animal         | Uncommon  | 60      | Sensory mutation research      |
| BLO-009 | Infected animal          | Rare      | 70      | Unstable biological properties |
| BLO-010 | Apex creature            | Legendary | 100     | Rare mutation research         |
| BLO-011 | Preserved blood sample   | Uncommon  | 80      | Research stability             |
| BLO-012 | Synthetic blood          | Rare      | 70      | Controlled compatibility       |
| BLO-013 | Ancient strain sample    | Legendary | 95      | Historical infection research  |
| BLO-014 | Contaminated blood       | Common    | 15      | High contamination risk        |
| BLO-015 | Stabilized hybrid sample | Legendary | 95      | Advanced hybrid research       |


### Blood quality attributes


Each blood sample should store:


- Source species.
- Infection classification.
- Sample quality.
- Freshness.
- Contamination.
- Compatibility.
- Mutation signature.
- Storage condition.
- Collection date.
- Expiration time.
- Research value.
- Traceability information.


Suggested numerical ranges:


| Attribute          | Range |
| ------------------ | ----- |
| Quality            | 0–100 |
| Freshness          | 0–100 |
| Contamination      | 0–100 |
| Compatibility      | 0–100 |
| Mutation intensity | 0–100 |
| Research value     | 0–100 |


## 1.2 Blood crafting recipes


These are fictional in-game laboratory recipes. They describe resource requirements and gameplay outcomes, not real biological preparation procedures.


| ID     | Recipe                    | Required materials                         | Output              | Gameplay effect                         |
| ------ | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| BR-001 | Basic blood ration        | Blood sample, sealed container             | Blood ration        | Reduces blood hunger                    |
| BR-002 | Stabilized blood ration   | Blood ration, stabilizer                   | Stable ration       | Reduced contamination risk              |
| BR-003 | Synthetic blood pack      | Synthetic base, container                  | Synthetic pack      | Alternative feeding resource            |
| BR-004 | Hybrid serum              | Hybrid sample, research component          | Hybrid serum        | Temporary hybrid enhancement            |
| BR-005 | Sensory catalyst          | Animal sample, catalyst                    | Sensory extract     | Temporary perception bonus              |
| BR-006 | Regeneration serum        | Mutant sample, medical component           | Recovery serum      | Temporary regeneration                  |
| BR-007 | Bloodlust suppressant     | Stabilizer, medical component              | Suppressant         | Reduces bloodlust                       |
| BR-008 | Mutation catalyst         | Alpha sample, rare component               | Mutation catalyst   | Unlocks mutation research               |
| BR-009 | Compatibility serum       | Stabilized sample, research component      | Compatibility serum | Improves mutation compatibility         |
| BR-010 | Infection inhibitor       | Medical component, stabilizer              | Inhibitor           | Temporarily slows infection progression |
| BR-011 | Research specimen         | Preserved sample, sample container         | Research specimen   | Laboratory research resource            |
| BR-012 | Advanced hybrid serum     | Hybrid sample, rare catalyst               | Advanced serum      | Unlocks advanced hybrid ability         |
| BR-013 | Biological scanner charge | Synthetic base, electronic component       | Scanner charge      | Powers biological detection             |
| BR-014 | Evolution catalyst        | Apex sample, legendary component           | Evolution catalyst  | Enables high-tier evolution event       |
| BR-015 | Purified sample           | Contaminated sample, purification resource | Purified specimen   | Reduces research contamination          |


## 1.3 Blood effects


| Effect                  | Strength   | Duration         | Description                       |
| ----------------------- | ---------- | ---------------- | --------------------------------- |
| Hunger relief           | 25–100     | Variable         | Reduces biological hunger         |
| Bloodlust reduction     | 5–25       | Immediate        | Restores control                  |
| Regeneration            | 1–5 HP/sec | 10–30 sec        | Temporary recovery                |
| Sensory enhancement     | 5–25%      | 30–120 sec       | Improved detection                |
| Strength enhancement    | 5–20%      | 30–90 sec        | Increased physical power          |
| Infection stabilization | 5–20%      | 1–5 min          | Slows fictional progression       |
| Mutation activation     | Variable   | Permanent unlock | Opens mutation progression        |
| Contamination           | 5–50       | Variable         | Introduces biological instability |


## 1.4 Blood risks


Every sample should have a risk profile.


| Risk                 | Trigger                        | Gameplay consequence            |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------- |
| Contamination        | Low-quality sample             | Increased instability           |
| Rejection            | Poor compatibility             | Reduced effect                  |
| Bloodlust spike      | High-intensity sample          | Increased aggression            |
| Mutation instability | Unstable catalyst              | Random mutation event           |
| Sensory overload     | Excessive sensory effect       | Temporary perception distortion |
| Cellular degradation | Expired sample                 | Reduced effectiveness           |
| Dependency           | Repeated use of specific serum | Increased resource demand       |
| Biological conflict  | Incompatible mutation          | Temporary ability penalty       |


## 1.5 Blood-to-mutation relationships


| Blood source     | Possible mutation              |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------ |
| Human survivor   | Memory preservation            |
| Fresh infected   | Improved undead senses         |
| Mutated infected | Increased resilience           |
| Alpha infected   | Advanced regeneration          |
| Hybrid blood     | Balanced hybrid traits         |
| Predatory animal | Enhanced tracking              |
| Infected animal  | Unstable sensory evolution     |
| Apex creature    | Advanced biological adaptation |
| Ancient strain   | Rare ancestral mutation        |
| Synthetic blood  | Improved stability             |


Mutation outcomes should be influenced by compatibility, player stage, existing mutations, and research progression. No sample should guarantee a specific result unless the player has unlocked the necessary research.


# 2. Cannabis System


The Cannabis System should be a complete fictional agricultural and product ecosystem inside the game.


It needs its own genetics database, strain identities, growth simulation, plant conditions, harvest quality, processing outputs, market demand, and crafting connections.


The system should not be limited to growing a plant and collecting an item. It should connect to the economy, settlement industry, research, culture, faction identity, and character progression.


## 2.1 Strain catalog


Original fictional game strains, not real-world cultivar specifications.


| ID      | Strain            | Genetic family  | Rarity    | Fictional trait                 |
| ------- | ----------------- | --------------- | --------- | ------------------------------- |
| CAN-001 | Phoenix OG        | Hybrid          | Common    | Balanced production             |
| CAN-002 | Toxic Sunrise     | Sativa-inspired | Uncommon  | Alertness theme                 |
| CAN-003 | Midnight Kush     | Indica-inspired | Common    | Relaxation theme                |
| CAN-004 | Green Reaper      | Hybrid          | Rare      | Resilience                      |
| CAN-005 | Zombie Breath     | Hybrid          | Rare      | Unusual aroma                   |
| CAN-006 | Ghost Train       | Sativa-inspired | Uncommon  | High energy theme               |
| CAN-007 | Purple Apocalypse | Indica-inspired | Rare      | Distinctive coloration          |
| CAN-008 | Nuclear Nectar    | Hybrid          | Epic      | High-value harvest              |
| CAN-009 | Widowmaker        | Hybrid          | Rare      | High-demand genetics            |
| CAN-010 | Emerald Wraith    | Hybrid          | Epic      | Unusual genetic stability       |
| CAN-011 | Blood Moon        | Indica-inspired | Epic      | Rare pigment expression         |
| CAN-012 | Graveyard Haze    | Sativa-inspired | Rare      | Distinctive flavor profile      |
| CAN-013 | Mutation Mist     | Hybrid          | Legendary | Experimental genetics           |
| CAN-014 | Paradise Lost     | Hybrid          | Epic      | Premium market appeal           |
| CAN-015 | Last Light        | Hybrid          | Legendary | Rare heritage genetics          |
| CAN-016 | Undead OG         | Hybrid          | Legendary | Unique fictional mutation trait |


## 2.2 Genetic attributes


Each strain needs a genetic record.


| Attribute           | Range | Purpose                 |
| ------------------- | ----- | ----------------------- |
| Genetic stability   | 0–100 | Predictability          |
| Growth vigor        | 0–100 | Growth performance      |
| Yield potential     | 0–100 | Harvest potential       |
| Resilience          | 0–100 | Environmental tolerance |
| Water efficiency    | 0–100 | Water demand modifier   |
| Disease resistance  | 0–100 | Disease tolerance       |
| Aroma intensity     | 0–100 | Product identity        |
| Color expression    | 0–100 | Appearance              |
| Processing quality  | 0–100 | Output quality          |
| Market desirability | 0–100 | Commercial demand       |
| Research value      | 0–100 | Scientific interest     |


## 2.3 Growth simulation


Every plant should have the following gameplay states:


1. Seed inventory.
2. Germination.
3. Seedling.
4. Vegetative growth.
5. Flowering.
6. Harvest readiness.
7. Harvest.
8. Processing.
9. Finished product.
10. Storage or sale.


Proposed fictional game durations:


| Growth stage      | Game duration |
| ----------------- | ------------- |
| Germination       | 1 day         |
| Seedling          | 3 days        |
| Vegetative        | 7 days        |
| Flowering         | 10 days       |
| Harvest readiness | 2 days        |
| Total base cycle  | 23 days       |


Strain modifiers can change the total game duration.


| Genetic type | Growth-time modifier |
| ------------ | -------------------- |
| Fast-cycle   | -20%                 |
| Standard     | 0%                   |
| Slow-cycle   | +20%                 |
| Experimental | Variable             |


Plant condition attributes:


- Health.
- Hydration.
- Nutrition.
- Temperature suitability.
- Light suitability.
- Environmental stress.
- Disease pressure.
- Genetic stability.
- Growth progress.
- Harvest quality.


## 2.4 Harvest quality


A proposed harvest score:


\\[ Q_h = 0.25G + 0.20E + 0.20H + 0.15R + 0.20P \\]


Where:


- \\(G\\) = genetic quality.
- \\(E\\) = environmental suitability.
- \\(H\\) = plant health.
- \\(R\\) = resilience.
- \\(P\\) = processing quality.


| Harvest score | Product grade |
| ------------- | ------------- |
| 0–19          | Failed        |
| 20–39         | Low           |
| 40–59         | Standard      |
| 60–74         | Good          |
| 75–89         | Premium       |
| 90–100        | Legendary     |


## 2.5 Processing outputs


| Processing category | Output           | Gameplay use                  |
| ------------------- | ---------------- | ----------------------------- |
| Raw harvest         | Plant material   | Base crafting resource        |
| Dried harvest       | Dried product    | Storage and trade             |
| Refined product     | Refined material | Premium economy               |
| Botanical extract   | Extract          | Fictional crafting ingredient |
| Seed recovery       | Seeds            | Genetic collection            |
| Fiber recovery      | Plant fiber      | Textile crafting              |
| Composting          | Organic material | Settlement agriculture        |
| Research sample     | Genetic specimen | Research progression          |
| Decorative product  | Display item     | Settlement morale and trade   |


## 2.6 Cannabis system effects


In the fictional game world, different products can influence:


- Character morale.
- Companion relationship events.
- Settlement culture.
- Trade demand.
- Reputation.
- Relaxation-themed buffs.
- Perception effects.
- Crafting and research.
- Festival and community events.


Each effect needs a defined duration, magnitude, stacking rule, and drawback.


## 2.7 Genetic breeding system


Breeding should use inherited traits rather than simply selecting a random new strain.


| Trait              | Inheritance method                      |
| ------------------ | --------------------------------------- |
| Growth vigor       | Weighted parental average               |
| Resilience         | Weighted parental average               |
| Yield potential    | Weighted parental average               |
| Color expression   | Dominant/recessive trait rules          |
| Aroma              | Weighted trait inheritance              |
| Genetic stability  | Parental average with mutation variance |
| Disease resistance | Weighted parental average               |


A breeding result should have:


- Unique genetic identifier.
- Parent strain identifiers.
- Inherited trait values.
- Mutation events.
- Stability score.
- Generation number.
- Breeding history.
- Discovery status.


The game should allow players to discover original strains and register them in a genetic archive.


# 3. Complete Crafting Catalog


Crafting needs a unified item database. Every object must have a unique ID, category, rarity, stack limit, weight, value, durability, unlock requirement, crafting station, and recipe.

## 3.7 Crafting rules


Every recipe should include:


- Recipe ID.
- Input item IDs.
- Input quantities.
- Output item IDs.
- Output quantities.
- Required skill level.
- Required station.
- Crafting duration.
- Failure probability.
- Quality modifier.
- Unlock condition.
- XP reward.


Crafting quality should be calculated from skill, station quality, material quality, and environmental conditions.


# 4. Enemies, Creatures & Wildlife


The creature database should include much more than health and damage.


Every creature needs a complete stat sheet, AI behavior, sensory abilities, movement characteristics, animation states, spawn conditions, loot, and relationships with other species.


## 4.1 Enemy stat sheets


| ID     | Enemy          | HP    | Damage | Speed | Detection | Threat     |
| ------ | -------------- | ----- | ------ | ----- | --------- | ---------- |
| EN-001 | Crawler        | 45    | 8      | 25    | 30        | Low        |
| EN-002 | Shambler       | 70    | 12     | 35    | 40        | Low        |
| EN-003 | Runner         | 90    | 18     | 90    | 65        | Medium     |
| EN-004 | Stalker        | 110   | 22     | 70    | 80        | Medium     |
| EN-005 | Screamer       | 160   | 15     | 45    | 90        | High       |
| EN-006 | Spitter        | 130   | 20     | 40    | 70        | High       |
| EN-007 | Brute          | 250   | 35     | 30    | 55        | High       |
| EN-008 | Armored undead | 350   | 30     | 35    | 60        | High       |
| EN-009 | Mutated hunter | 400   | 45     | 100   | 95        | Very high  |
| EN-010 | Alpha infected | 750   | 55     | 65    | 85        | Boss       |
| EN-011 | Horde champion | 1,200 | 65     | 55    | 90        | Boss       |
| EN-012 | Apex mutation  | 2,500 | 85     | 80    | 100       | World boss |


## 4.2 Enemy behavior definitions


| Enemy          | Behavior                 | Special ability         |
| -------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------------- |
| Crawler        | Ambush from ground level | Grabs                   |
| Shambler       | Slow group pursuit       | Horde attraction        |
| Runner         | Fast direct pursuit      | Sprint attack           |
| Stalker        | Hides and follows        | Ambush                  |
| Screamer       | Maintains distance       | Summons nearby infected |
| Spitter        | Ranged harassment        | Biological projectile   |
| Brute          | Charges obstacles        | Heavy impact            |
| Armored undead | Advances defensively     | Armor resistance        |
| Mutated hunter | Tracks player            | Leap attack             |
| Alpha infected | Controls nearby infected | Rally behavior          |
| Horde champion | Leads large groups       | Rally and charge        |
| Apex mutation  | Multi-phase boss AI      | Adaptive attacks        |


## 4.3 Enemy AI states


Each enemy should support:


1. Idle.
2. Patrol.
3. Wander.
4. Investigate sound.
5. Detect scent.
6. Detect player.
7. Alert.
8. Pursue.
9. Attack.
10. Defend.
11. Search.
12. Retreat.
13. Call allies.
14. Return to territory.
15. Feed.
16. Rest.
17. Mutate.
18. Die.


Enemies should remember recent player activity according to their intelligence and sensory capabilities.


## 4.4 Animation requirements


| Animation     | Required states                  |
| ------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Idle          | Standing, breathing, twitching   |
| Movement      | Walk, run, crawl                 |
| Combat        | Light attack, heavy attack, grab |
| Defense       | Block, stagger, recoil           |
| Detection     | Listen, sniff, investigate       |
| Damage        | Hit reactions, knockback         |
| Death         | Collapse, fall, disintegration   |
| Special       | Scream, spit, charge, mutation   |
| Environmental | Climb, climb over, break through |


Animation speed should match movement speed and attack timing. Hit detection should use defined animation frames or animation events, not visual guesswork.


## 4.5 Loot tables


| Enemy          | Common loot         | Uncommon loot         | Rare loot             |
| -------------- | ------------------- | --------------------- | --------------------- |
| Crawler        | Organic matter      | Cloth                 | Biological sample     |
| Shambler       | Organic matter      | Scrap                 | Preserved sample      |
| Runner         | Organic matter      | Chemicals             | Mutation sample       |
| Stalker        | Organic matter      | Electronic scrap      | Sensory catalyst      |
| Screamer       | Organic matter      | Bio-material          | Vocal mutation sample |
| Spitter        | Organic matter      | Chemicals             | Biological catalyst   |
| Brute          | Scrap, bio-material | Rare alloy            | Mutation core         |
| Armored undead | Metal plates        | Components            | Armor parts           |
| Mutated hunter | Bio-material        | Mutation catalyst     | Rare genetic sample   |
| Alpha infected | Rare bio-material   | Research specimen     | Alpha catalyst        |
| Horde champion | Rare components     | Mutation core         | Legendary sample      |
| Apex mutation  | Legendary materials | Advanced bio-material | Apex core             |


Loot should use weighted probability tables.


Example:


| Drop category | Chance |
| ------------- | ------ |
| Common        | 65%    |
| Uncommon      | 25%    |
| Rare          | 8%     |
| Epic          | 1.8%   |
| Legendary     | 0.2%   |


These are illustrative category probabilities. Individual enemy tables need separate item weights and quantity ranges.


## 4.6 Spawn tables


Spawn generation should consider:


- Region danger rating.
- Time of day.
- Weather.
- Player level.
- Infection stage.
- Noise level.
- Settlement activity.
- World simulation events.
- Faction territory.
- Recent player actions.


| Region danger | Common enemies      | Uncommon enemies | Rare enemies    |
| ------------- | ------------------- | ---------------- | --------------- |
| 1             | Crawlers, shamblers | None             | None            |
| 2             | Shamblers, runners  | Stalkers         | None            |
| 3             | Runners, stalkers   | Spitters         | Brutes          |
| 4             | Brutes, screamers   | Armored undead   | Mutated hunters |
| 5             | Mixed hordes        | Alpha infected   | Horde champions |
| 6             | Advanced mutations  | Apex hunters     | Apex mutation   |


# 5. Animal System


Animals should function as living wildlife rather than decorative scenery.


They need their own needs, habitats, territorial behavior, reproduction, food chains, taming, domestication, and relationships with the player and settlements.


## 5.1 Species catalog


| ID      | Species       | Type           | Temperament       | Primary role         |
| ------- | ------------- | -------------- | ----------------- | -------------------- |
| ANI-001 | Domestic dog  | Mammal         | Loyal             | Companion            |
| ANI-002 | Feral dog     | Mammal         | Aggressive        | Predator             |
| ANI-003 | Wolf          | Mammal         | Territorial       | Pack predator        |
| ANI-004 | Deer          | Mammal         | Skittish          | Prey                 |
| ANI-005 | Wild boar     | Mammal         | Aggressive        | Prey/predator threat |
| ANI-006 | Rabbit        | Mammal         | Skittish          | Small prey           |
| ANI-007 | Fox           | Mammal         | Cautious          | Scavenger            |
| ANI-008 | Bear          | Mammal         | Territorial       | Apex predator        |
| ANI-009 | Crow          | Bird           | Intelligent       | Scavenger            |
| ANI-010 | Raven         | Bird           | Intelligent       | Scavenger            |
| ANI-011 | Rat           | Mammal         | Cautious          | Scavenger            |
| ANI-012 | Infected dog  | Mutated mammal | Aggressive        | Infected predator    |
| ANI-013 | Infected wolf | Mutated mammal | Pack aggressive   | Infected predator    |
| ANI-014 | Mutated deer  | Mutated mammal | Unpredictable     | Mutated wildlife     |
| ANI-015 | Apex predator | Mutated mammal | Highly aggressive | Rare boss wildlife   |
| ANI-016 | Domestic goat | Mammal         | Calm              | Settlement livestock |


## 5.2 Animal attributes


Every species should have:


| Attribute       | Range   |
| --------------- | ------- |
| Health          | 1–1,000 |
| Stamina         | 1–500   |
| Speed           | 1–100   |
| Aggression      | 0–100   |
| Fear            | 0–100   |
| Intelligence    | 0–100   |
| Loyalty         | 0–100   |
| Hunger          | 0–100   |
| Thirst          | 0–100   |
| Trust           | 0–100   |
| Detection       | 0–100   |
| Social behavior | 0–100   |


Additional species fields:


- Habitat.
- Diet.
- Activity cycle.
- Territory radius.
- Pack size.
- Breeding season.
- Gestation or incubation duration.
- Lifespan.
- Infected variants.
- Predators.
- Prey.
- Environmental requirements.


## 5.3 Taming rules


Animal taming should be a progression system.


| Stage    | Trust | Behavior                  |
| -------- | ----- | ------------------------- |
| Wild     | 0–19  | Avoids or attacks player  |
| Aware    | 20–39 | Recognizes player         |
| Familiar | 40–59 | Approaches cautiously     |
| Friendly | 60–74 | Accepts interaction       |
| Bonded   | 75–89 | Responds to commands      |
| Loyal    | 90–99 | Strong companion behavior |
| Devoted  | 100   | Maximum relationship      |


Taming should depend on:


- Species temperament.
- Player behavior.
- Food preferences.
- Time spent together.
- Environmental safety.
- Existing bonds.
- Threat exposure.


Not every animal should be tamable. Large predators and certain mutated creatures may only be temporarily calmed, trained through rare story events, or remain permanently wild.


## 5.4 Loyalty system


Loyalty should increase through care, successful commands, protection, and companionship.


Loyalty should decrease through neglect, abuse, abandonment, and repeated dangerous situations.


| Event              | Loyalty change |
| ------------------ | -------------- |
| Feed               | +2             |
| Play               | +2             |
| Successful command | +1             |
| Protect animal     | +5             |
| Rescue animal      | +10            |
| Neglect            | -2/day         |
| Abandon            | -15            |
| Harm animal        | -25            |
| Repeated betrayal  | -30            |


## 5.5 Animal commands


| Command  | Function                       |
| -------- | ------------------------------ |
| Follow   | Follow player                  |
| Stay     | Remain in place                |
| Guard    | Protect assigned area          |
| Attack   | Engage designated target       |
| Defend   | Protect player or companion    |
| Search   | Investigate nearby area        |
| Track    | Follow a scent trail           |
| Retrieve | Collect a designated item      |
| Hunt     | Search for prey                |
| Return   | Go to settlement               |
| Rest     | Enter recovery state           |
| Dismiss  | Return to independent behavior |


Command success depends on species intelligence, loyalty, training, distance, environmental danger, and competing instincts.


## 5.6 Breeding rules


Animal breeding requires:


- Compatible species.
- Compatible genetics.
- Appropriate age.
- Health.
- Adequate habitat.
- Appropriate environmental conditions.
- A successful breeding event.


Offspring inherit weighted parental traits, including speed, resilience, temperament, intelligence, appearance, and infection resistance.


Mutation inheritance should be rare and governed by the game's fictional infection genetics.


# 6. World-Building Data


The world needs to become a fully defined playable environment, not simply a collection of locations on a map.


Every region needs geography, coordinates, entrances, exits, points of interest, structures, inhabitants, resources, threats, environmental conditions, and simulation rules.


I recommend an interconnected world made up of 12 major regions, with smaller subregions and discoverable locations inside each.


## 6.1 World geography

| REG-003 | Blackwood Forest         | 3      | Dense forest                   | Wood, wildlife, herbs             |
| REG-004 | Rustbelt Industrial Zone | 5      | Factories                      | Metal, machinery, components      |
| REG-005 | Deadwater District       | 4      | Flooded urban area             | Salvage, chemicals                |
| REG-006 | Redstone Quarry          | 3      | Mining region                  | Stone, minerals, metal            |
| REG-007 | Hollow Creek             | 2      | Rural settlement               | Food, livestock, supplies         |
| REG-008 | The Outskirts            | 3      | Suburban ruins                 | Household supplies, vehicles      |
| REG-009 | Iron Ridge               | 4      | Mountain terrain               | Minerals, military wreckage       |
| REG-010 | The Containment Zone     | 6      | Quarantined research territory | Research, rare samples            |
| REG-011 | Bonefield Wastes         | 6      | Mutated wasteland              | Rare biological materials         |
| REG-012 | New Eden Basin           | 2      | Reclaimed fertile territory    | Agriculture, settlement expansion |


These are original proposed locations. Their geography and contents should be fixed in the world database, while their population, ownership, resources, and conditions evolve during play.


## 6.2 Regional data sheets


Each region needs its own configuration.


| Region                   | Climate    | Population | Faction presence         | Main threat        |
| ------------------------ | ---------- | ---------- | ------------------------ | ------------------ |
| Ashfall City             | Temperate  | 1,200      | Multiple                 | Urban hordes       |
| Green Valley             | Temperate  | 280        | Agricultural communities | Raiders            |
| Blackwood Forest         | Forest     | 80         | Independent survivors    | Predators          |
| Rustbelt Industrial Zone | Industrial | 150        | Iron Dominion            | Armored infected   |
| Deadwater District       | Wetland    | 60         | Smugglers                | Mutated wildlife   |
| Redstone Quarry          | Dry        | 110        | Mining faction           | Brutes             |
| Hollow Creek             | Temperate  | 220        | Human settlements        | Infected raids     |
| The Outskirts            | Temperate  | 400        | Mixed                    | Runners            |
| Iron Ridge               | Mountain   | 90         | Military remnants        | Hunters            |
| The Containment Zone     | Variable   | 40         | Research remnants        | Apex mutations     |
| Bonefield Wastes         | Extreme    | 20         | Undead factions          | Advanced mutations |
| New Eden Basin           | Temperate  | 500        | Hybrid communities       | Political conflict |


Population figures are illustrative starting world-state values. They should be adjustable and generated from the actual simulation.


## 6.3 Region coordinate system


Use a consistent coordinate system for the entire game.


Recommended:


- World coordinates: X, Y.
- Region identifiers: REG-001 through REG-012.
- Local coordinates: region-specific X and Y.
- Elevation: Z.
- Location identifier: unique POI ID.
- Navigation connections: explicit exits and entrances.


Example world map grid:
