[Heavy Bio Mutant - Unreal Engine Asset](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/KmpDW3__6lQNe-cwpMQOXYKhz-GaaD38v_QEpSDGllQ59f8IF1Cv5HaFa6NMsPjUcpkLq5WPIYUqML7Q736e9PF8t_8i2zZg1XTC8Cwo3vhnOkZvmDRjKMDfoiNbok6XmusRXJMZ7R8JbOAgti7nf20Wy8b0-rcvJY-A4vAGFpI?purpose=inline)


[《消逝的光芒》 - 官方网站](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/a83H5SNQA5LYP0jZf4gHDjkqFxsK91iS6W2yF5e1h35uc3KiBNQe4_fGdZmfYwu37mkFnSXzfcqqPNwouwV4RB5LeH7SfGjF4bQ0W5HkuFX8m1Jf3a9qHVjLz7tLkjzTrwsg6J_TqnK7FfwKCFVyo_8K68BzhzAWy1KpuMndcV8?purpose=inline)


12


## 3.1 Core concept


Bloodlust is the conflict between conscious thought and the player's evolving undead biology.


The player remains a conscious person throughout the transformation. The undead instincts become increasingly powerful, but the player can learn to control, redirect, or embrace them.


Bloodlust must influence the player's entire experience:


- Combat.
- Hunger.
- Sensory perception.
- Social behavior.
- Dialogue.
- Movement.
- Decision-making.
- Mutations.
- Companion relationships.
- Human settlement access.
- Undead faction interactions.


## 3.2 Separate biological meters


Do not combine every biological mechanic into one bar.


Use separate meters:


| Meter       | Range  | Meaning                            |
| ----------- | ------ | ---------------------------------- |
| Bloodlust   | 0–100  | Predatory urges                    |
| Hunger      | 0–100  | Biological feeding need            |
| Infection   | 0–100% | Transformation progression         |
| Humanity    | 0–100  | Personal identity and self-control |
| Instability | 0–100  | Biological unpredictability        |
| Stress      | 0–100  | Psychological pressure             |
| Fatigue     | 0–100  | Physical exhaustion                |


These values interact but are not interchangeable.


## 3.3 Bloodlust stages


| Value | State         | Gameplay                     |
| ----- | ------------- | ---------------------------- |
| 0–9   | Dormant       | Normal control               |
| 10–24 | Stirring      | Enhanced scent awareness     |
| 25–39 | Agitated      | Increased sensory fixation   |
| 40–49 | Hungry        | Blood detection increases    |
| 50–64 | Predatory     | Combat benefits              |
| 65–74 | Frenzied      | Social penalties             |
| 75–84 | Unstable      | Control checks               |
| 85–94 | Near override | Strong impulse effects       |
| 95–99 | Critical      | Severe control risk          |
| 100   | Override      | Temporary instinct dominance |


## 3.4 Bloodlust increase


Bloodlust increases through environmental, biological, and behavioral triggers.


| Trigger                            | Proposed increase |
| ---------------------------------- | ----------------- |
| Prolonged hunger                   | +1 per 10 seconds |
| Fresh blood nearby                 | +3                |
| Injured human nearby               | +2                |
| Severe injury                      | +4                |
| Human kill                         | +12               |
| Infected kill                      | +3                |
| High-stress event                  | +2 to +5          |
| Entering a blood-contaminated area | +3                |
| Certain mutation activation        | +5 to +15         |
| Special narrative event            | Variable          |


Use cooldowns on repeated triggers so that standing near one source of blood cannot generate unlimited increases every frame.


## 3.5 Bloodlust decay


| Condition                   | Reduction         |
| --------------------------- | ----------------- |
| Safe resting                | -2/min            |
| Sleeping                    | -4/min            |
| Time away from stimuli      | -1/min            |
| Self-control perk           | Additional -1/min |
| Fictional stabilizer        | -15 immediately   |
| Successful resistance event | -10               |
| Certain mutations           | Variable          |


## 3.6 Control-loss model


Control loss should not happen randomly without warning.


A possible model:


\\[ R=\max(0,B-70) \\]


\\[ P=\min(95,\ 2R+S+T-W) \\]


Where:


- \\(B\\) = Bloodlust.
- \\(S\\) = Stress contribution.
- \\(T\\) = Environmental trigger contribution.
- \\(W\\) = Willpower and control bonuses.
- \\(P\\) = probability of a control event.


Perform checks every 10 seconds while bloodlust is 75 or higher.


## 3.7 What happens during loss of control?


The game should use graduated behavior rather than immediately taking away the player's character.


Possible effects:


- Character becomes fixated on nearby blood sources.
- Dialogue choices become limited.
- The character may ignore certain commands.
- Companion proximity causes tension.
- Movement may automatically approach a stimulus.
- Attacks may become more aggressive.
- The player receives a brief opportunity to resist.
- Severe episodes can trigger a temporary override.


The player should always understand why control is being threatened.


## 3.8 Bloodlust benefits


Bloodlust should be dangerous but useful.


| Threshold | Benefit                            |
| --------- | ---------------------------------- |
| 25        | Improved scent detection           |
| 40        | Enhanced enemy tracking            |
| 50        | +5% melee damage                   |
| 65        | +10% melee damage                  |
| 75        | +15% threat detection              |
| 85        | +15% pain resistance               |
| 95        | +20% temporary physical resistance |


## 3.9 Bloodlust control skills


| Skill               | Effect                                           |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| Restraint           | Reduces bloodlust growth                         |
| Focus               | Improves resistance checks                       |
| Meditation          | Accelerates decay                                |
| Sensory Discipline  | Reduces stimulus overload                        |
| Biological Control  | Improves mutation stability                      |
| Predator Awareness  | Allows controlled use of predatory senses        |
| Instinct Channeling | Converts bloodlust into temporary combat bonuses |


## 3.10 Bloodlust and character identity


The player should have three broad development philosophies:


Retain Humanity


- Prioritize control.
- Maintain human relationships.
- Resist biological urges.
- Develop social acceptance.


Embrace the Undead


- Develop predatory abilities.
- Accept biological instincts.
- Build relationships with undead factions.
- Sacrifice some human social access.


Forge a Hybrid Identity


- Balance human and undead traits.
- Develop specialized abilities.
- Establish new social relationships.
- Become a bridge between civilizations.


These should be paths rather than permanent class selections. The player can change direction through meaningful progression and consequences.


# 4. GATHERING SYSTEM


[RUST RU — Русскоязычное Сообщество Rust | Discord Сервер](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/0-Y2JDfJfvwXi5Rtj7vQrkwLVvDCXYlxmSy7LxJ6XWyJ5d8F6rXnMhmCB6ScQpGfycZwyiW6DQ58S7QiLcYNUYg3_JUh3NzbAhu6iQfufjB8HIng5Y5yXtTnisUyYVYKEcPzJ20vdvPv4F6BwyX27cMfxrZuvxqJcKo6BfEaROM?purpose=inline)


[Save 89% on Survival: Lost Way on Steam](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/dOUwyQ-LR6MTgGpjQqnQCeF_llQd1vzlWX3c2O-SfjUltsVT84Keu-yp2OTT39mPbdG3BErib039VCp3yaV-7zSsx7C917qeRmkO5SLfwn7kNgRrkuzs49cSnC0NMTog36b6eKu6hE6-pRSN1l2RETGSFQ-6mE1GJdhropSp3Bc?purpose=inline)


[CrisisX on Steam](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/0BnJgZEdOpDvf4GPO4B_Y-rQ5WrcwbkPxXbN9xVM1jDS7q5hhCQbMrJ2sLTusWyCHr1x3-nheVt2COaLYk2DEO9ZYkfVLDqPk49ro7l4bLvH5ECGoMsibEWk961FM6o9EFoGL-zJ2W6kDbOJnSticUiovxRL7CVM-_-yh13shuA?purpose=inline)


12


## 4.1 Core purpose


Gathering supplies the entire game economy.


Every crafting recipe, settlement, weapon, structure, vehicle, and production system depends on gathered resources.


The player should be able to gather materials from:


- Wilderness.
- Buildings.
- Vehicles.
- Abandoned settlements.
- Industrial sites.
- Farms.
- Mines.
- Water sources.
- Enemies.
- Animals.
- Containers.
- Environmental salvage.


## 4.2 Gathering categories


| Category    | Resources                               |
| ----------- | --------------------------------------- |
| Wood        | Hardwood, softwood, branches            |
| Stone       | Rock, gravel, mineral deposits          |
| Metal       | Scrap, steel, copper, aluminum          |
| Organic     | Fiber, plant matter, compost            |
| Wildlife    | Animal products and biological samples  |
| Food        | Wild plants, crops, preserved food      |
| Water       | Natural water, collected water          |
| Chemicals   | Industrial and laboratory salvage       |
| Electronics | Circuit boards, wires, components       |
| Textiles    | Cloth, leather, synthetic fiber         |
| Biological  | Fictional infected samples              |
| Botanical   | Seeds, harvests, fictional strains      |
| Fuel        | Fuel containers, combustible materials  |
| Knowledge   | Documents, schematics, research records |


## 4.3 Gathering interaction types


Different resources need different interactions.


| Interaction | Example                            |
| ----------- | ---------------------------------- |
| Pick up     | Loose items                        |
| Search      | Containers and abandoned locations |
| Harvest     | Plants and crops                   |
| Chop        | Trees and wooden objects           |
| Mine        | Mineral deposits                   |
| Salvage     | Vehicles and machinery             |
| Dismantle   | Furniture and structures           |
| Hunt        | Wildlife                           |
| Track       | Locate animals                     |
| Fish        | Water resources                    |
| Collect     | Water and environmental resources  |
| Extract     | Fictional research materials       |
| Forage      | Wild food and plants               |


## 4.4 Resource node data


Every gatherable node should have:


- Node ID.
- Resource type.
- Resource quantity.
- Maximum capacity.
- Current remaining quantity.
- Gathering difficulty.
- Required tool.
- Required skill.
- Gathering duration.
- Quality range.
- Respawn rule.
- Respawn duration.
- Regional abundance.
- Depletion state.
- Environmental condition.
- Interaction animation.


Example:


| Node     | Resource          | Quantity | Tool         | Time   |
| -------- | ----------------- | -------- | ------------ | ------ |
| NODE-001 | Hardwood tree     | 12       | Axe          | 8 sec  |
| NODE-002 | Scrap pile        | 8        | Crowbar      | 6 sec  |
| NODE-003 | Stone deposit     | 20       | Pickaxe      | 12 sec |
| NODE-004 | Wild herb patch   | 5        | None         | 4 sec  |
| NODE-005 | Abandoned vehicle | 10       | Wrench       | 15 sec |
| NODE-006 | Supply container  | Variable | None         | 5 sec  |
| NODE-007 | Metal wreckage    | 15       | Cutting tool | 18 sec |
| NODE-008 | Water source      | 10       | Container    | 5 sec  |


## 4.5 Gathering yield formula


\\[ Y=B\times S\times T\times Q\times E \\]


Where:


- \\(B\\) = base yield.
- \\(S\\) = skill modifier.
- \\(T\\) = tool modifier.
- \\(Q\\) = resource quality.
- \\(E\\) = environmental modifier.


Example modifiers:


| Factor                       | Multiplier |
| ---------------------------- | ---------- |
| Novice skill                 | 0.80x      |
| Standard skill               | 1.00x      |
| Expert skill                 | 1.20x      |
| Master skill                 | 1.40x      |
| Poor tool                    | 0.75x      |
| Standard tool                | 1.00x      |
| Advanced tool                | 1.25x      |
| Poor environmental condition | 0.70x      |
| Ideal condition              | 1.00x      |


## 4.6 Scavenging


Scavenging deserves its own subsystem.


Every searchable location should contain loot containers with:


- Container type.
- Search duration.
- Loot table.
- Discovery probability.
- Condition.
- Lock status.
- Trap status.
- Noise generated.
- Ownership.
- Previous search status.


Example container types:


- Cabinets.
- Drawers.
- Lockers.
- Desks.
- Toolboxes.
- Medical cabinets.
- Refrigerators.
- Vehicle trunks.
- Weapon lockers.
- Supply crates.
- Safes.
- Storage bins.


Searching a location should consume time and potentially attract enemies.


## 4.7 Gathering progression


| Skill level | Capability                     |
| ----------- | ------------------------------ |
| 1–5         | Basic resource collection      |
| 6–10        | Improved yield                 |
| 11–15       | Better resource identification |
| 16–20       | Rare material detection        |
| 21–25       | Advanced harvesting            |
| 26–30       | Master resource extraction     |


Perks should include:


- Efficient harvesting.
- Salvage expert.
- Keen eyes.
- Resource tracking.
- Careful extraction.
- Lucky scavenger.
- Deep search.
- Field knowledge.


## 4.8 Resource regeneration


Not all resources should respawn in the same way.


| Resource           | Regeneration behavior         |
| ------------------ | ----------------------------- |
| Wild plants        | Seasonal growth               |
| Trees              | Slow regrowth                 |
| Stone deposits     | Limited or nonrenewable       |
| Scrap              | No natural regeneration       |
| Containers         | World-event dependent         |
| Wildlife           | Reproduction and migration    |
| Water              | Environmental replenishment   |
| Crops              | Farming cycle                 |
| Biological samples | Creature population dependent |


The world simulation should prevent infinite resource farming.


## 4.9 Gathering risks


- Noise attracts enemies.
- Dangerous locations contain traps.
- Environmental hazards cause injury.
- Resource nodes may be contaminated.
- Wildlife may defend territory.
- Other survivors may claim resources.
- Carrying too much reduces movement.
- Time spent gathering increases exposure.


# 5. BUILDING AND CONSTRUCTION SYSTEM


[Abandoned House base in Mist Survival - Album on Imgur](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/PCMYhB3Z7VfWp_YJbqQRRRn7kgOyZBu_3kZ3TfPDvkhQr_AAg41JqDzjOawdzE1jlDLPaxbvqX7i5ZWfFI23C9pSes9TA837J9yavAF-sS6yuwEk5m1NYjeFHAKehTmuy2K7ICsIHArzXOO-I7O38h2nktelNYK83PsvoH_JAY8?purpose=inline)


[Force of Nature 2: Ghost Keeper Press Kit - Force of Nature](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/aWakE6waftZe0aP646Hu89tU1fhTaHi3mHaXQ_m5XcarQrCG8NLPB_Q-QFwpJmlamB12C34PEXjmv9nfHJBNKeJbnkOxzZQS-G7YJFZjPG2htOxqkivqQ1ITHsGtthXt5dEx1-7wqbxdfujzhY0l9IVCNZoFaZ5nbIJrvYm2MAw?purpose=inline)


[Shelter Building Guide | Dead Land Survival Wiki | Dead Land Survival](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/Vzo0sSEUWgFCwEuDTWMcts_w1sVfIZQmXaG9C5wcEw-qbqeS0oPiAXkA3JDa4e3ifs9lrzcEe16J1Fon7Ae9gFhmSZ6vUCAXZ52YYsg8vox8sWazXzzVPYfaY9xzyUepAbYsmVpmClXk0KxQ6iPygClbr-BAKQQ3LlYAjVWMc1w?purpose=inline)


14


## 5.1 Core purpose


Building allows players to create safehouses, workshops, farms, fortified settlements, laboratories, trading posts, and eventually entire communities.


It should support two distinct construction experiences:


Personal construction: The player places and upgrades structures directly.


Civilization construction: The player plans settlements, assigns workers, manages resources, establishes infrastructure, and expands territory.


A small cabin and a regional city should use the same underlying construction logic at different scales.


## 5.2 Construction categories


| Category          | Structures                                |
| ----------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| Foundations       | Dirt, wood, stone, concrete, metal        |
| Floors            | Wooden, reinforced, concrete, metal       |
| Walls             | Wooden, scrap, stone, reinforced          |
| Doors             | Basic, reinforced, secure, automated      |
| Windows           | Basic, reinforced, barricaded             |
| Roofs             | Wood, metal, concrete                     |
| Defenses          | Barricades, spikes, traps, turrets        |
| Storage           | Crates, lockers, warehouses               |
| Production        | Workbenches, workshops, factories         |
| Medical           | Clinics, infirmaries, laboratories        |
| Agriculture       | Farms, greenhouses, irrigation            |
| Utilities         | Generators, batteries, water systems      |
| Communications    | Radios, antennas, towers                  |
| Residential       | Beds, shelters, housing                   |
| Community         | Kitchens, halls, recreation               |
| Research          | Laboratories, archives, containment rooms |
| Animal facilities | Stables, pens, kennels                    |
| Transportation    | Garages, vehicle shelters, landing areas  |


## 5.3 Building placement


Every building piece should have:


- Position X, Y, Z.
- Rotation.
- Dimensions.
- Building ID.
- Owner.
- Construction state.
- Health.
- Material type.
- Structural strength.
- Upgrade level.
- Connection points.
- Collision geometry.
- Interaction points.
- Access permissions.


### Placement rules


A structure can only be placed when:


- The terrain supports it.
- The location is available.
- Required materials are present.
- Structural connections are valid.
- The player has construction access.
- Placement does not violate collision rules.
- Any required foundation is present.


The building preview should show:


- Valid placement.
- Invalid placement.
- Required materials.
- Structural support.
- Collision.
- Height.
- Rotation.
- Estimated construction cost.


## 5.4 Structural integrity


Buildings should have health and structural integrity.


| Material         | Health | Durability | Fire resistance |
| ---------------- | ------ | ---------- | --------------- |
| Cloth            | 50     | 20         | 5               |
| Softwood         | 150    | 50         | 20              |
| Hardwood         | 250    | 75         | 30              |
| Scrap metal      | 350    | 100        | 60              |
| Reinforced steel | 650    | 150        | 85              |
| Stone            | 700    | 200        | 90              |
| Concrete         | 900    | 250        | 95              |
| Advanced alloy   | 1,200  | 300        | 100             |


These are proposed relative game values, not real engineering ratings.


## 5.5 Construction stages


Every structure progresses through:


1. Blueprint placement.
2. Material reservation.
3. Foundation preparation.
4. Construction.
5. Completion.
6. Inspection.
7. Activation.
8. Maintenance.
9. Upgrade.
10. Repair or demolition.


Large structures should be constructed in phases.


## 5.6 Construction labor


NPC workers should have construction roles.


| Worker skill | Efficiency |
| ------------ | ---------- |
| Untrained    | 0.60x      |
| Basic        | 0.80x      |
| Skilled      | 1.00x      |
| Expert       | 1.25x      |
| Master       | 1.50x      |


Workers need:


- Assigned job.
- Required materials.
- Tools.
- Rest periods.
- Safety.
- Skill level.
- Morale.
- Work priority.


A settlement can construct buildings while the player explores elsewhere.


## 5.7 Base defenses


Defensive systems should include:


- Barricades.
- Reinforced doors.
- Watchtowers.
- Traps.
- Alarm systems.
- Perimeter lighting.
- Observation posts.
- Automated detection.
- Defensive firing positions.
- Reinforced walls.
- Emergency shelters.
- Escape tunnels.


Each defense should have detection radius, coverage, durability, resource requirements, maintenance, and activation state.


## 5.8 Power and utilities


Electricity should be a real settlement resource.


Power sources:


- Portable generators.
- Solar arrays.
- Wind turbines.
- Fuel generators.
- Battery banks.
- Experimental energy systems.


Power consumers:


- Lighting.
- Refrigeration.
- Medical equipment.
- Research laboratories.
- Security systems.
- Water pumps.
- Communications.
- Manufacturing.


Example:


| Facility                   | Power demand |
| -------------------------- | ------------ |
| Small light                | 1            |
| Radio                      | 2            |
| Refrigerator               | 3            |
| Water pump                 | 5            |
| Workshop                   | 5            |
| Medical station            | 6            |
| Research laboratory        | 12           |
| Industrial production line | 20           |


Each settlement should calculate generation, storage, consumption, losses, and outages.


## 5.9 Water and sanitation


A settlement needs:


- Water collection.
- Purification.
- Storage.
- Distribution.
- Waste management.
- Contamination monitoring.
- Maintenance.


A shortage should affect population health, morale, and migration.


## 5.10 Settlement management


The player should be able to:


- Assign NPCs to jobs.
- Manage construction queues.
- Set building priorities.
- Establish storage rules.
- Manage resource reserves.
- Assign guards.
- Establish farming production.
- Set access permissions.
- Manage housing.
- Establish laws.
- Set taxes or trade policies.
- Create emergency plans.


## 5.11 Building upgrades


Every building can have upgrade tiers.


| Tier | Name        | Capability          |
| ---- | ----------- | ------------------- |
| 1    | Makeshift   | Basic function      |
| 2    | Reinforced  | Better durability   |
| 3    | Developed   | Improved production |
| 4    | Advanced    | Special features    |
| 5    | Industrial  | High capacity       |
| 6    | Specialized | Unique technology   |


## 5.12 Building destruction and repair


Structures can be damaged by:


- Enemy attacks.
- Fire.
- Explosions.
- Weather.
- Neglect.
- Structural failure.
- Faction attacks.
- Accidents.


Repair requires resources and time.


If a critical support structure is destroyed, connected pieces may become unstable or collapse.


The system must distinguish between decorative damage and actual structural failure.


# 6. INVENTORY SYSTEM


[Mizz Survival on Steam](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/_Mez2owjD9UE9UW1nQ5u4ukhj6V0P-OkqDBfmOi6qxfZU9wVFvOl3vtYByfkIysCcZHXaDjdAkKvLpF2r6mg7dhtjKfy_OM3HP7PHpGHig9ERzsuDSLQFKO3OkiVd9gNddgFOzlMlFzUPdvJr9TjzS69Uzyw8u3PpJPomHiL0d8?purpose=inline)


[DeadPoly on Steam](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/C4AeDKkvSPdoZLtBEy8PkVsuQzRIW4P7eo6j-KmGHegAXTIuSIoWGlBO6UwahrMChauTYApDr8nI03cw2RK_Yqe7vKgeLUkaafiBHSBHIEa4YpBP7dNhLxUd9fezHZTwrubjZlsR85y9ydtoQqnMelrC06HTJe1phvpeGqHOYaM?purpose=inline)


[Windrose Guide: How to Get Plague Halberd | GAMES.GG](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/7yvEW-LNs7FJ5YFJd_d4iM4ZWgQf7dvLOsXQt1YZhaz3GHxxqk-EmxTLV8xn3PLDs9RuaSVKlgHSpfqoOx-ZVQLWeC8lpV5KnS_uChYynixaE6YuoriCPqGjiXDTizTgGSktDfb3r5ukGC6u2tyoYZ0mHBXdv7Oli8xurL3fhkY?purpose=inline)


12


## 6.1 Core purpose


Inventory is the central resource-management interface.


It must manage:


- Items.
- Equipment.
- Weapons.
- Ammunition.
- Materials.
- Components.
- Consumables.
- Quest items.
- Biological samples.
- Crafted products.
- Containers.
- Storage.
- Weight.
- Volume.
- Durability.
- Item condition.


Inventory should feel intuitive even when the game has hundreds or thousands of item types.


## 6.2 Inventory containers


| Container            | Purpose                    |
| -------------------- | -------------------------- |
| Personal inventory   | Main carried items         |
| Backpack             | Expanded carrying capacity |
| Weapon slots         | Equipped weapons           |
| Clothing slots       | Worn clothing              |
| Quick slots          | Rapid access               |
| Belt slots           | Tools and utility items    |
| Medical pouch        | Medical items              |
| Biological container | Blood and research samples |
| Vehicle storage      | Transported equipment      |
| Safehouse storage    | Persistent storage         |
| Settlement warehouse | Bulk resources             |
| Vendor inventory     | Commercial stock           |
| Companion inventory  | Companion equipment        |


## 6.3 Equipment slots


Suggested player equipment slots:


- Head.
- Face.
- Torso.
- Hands.
- Legs.
- Feet.
- Back.
- Belt.
- Primary weapon.
- Secondary weapon.
- Sidearm.
- Utility.
- Accessories.


Equipment can have compatibility requirements and conflicts.


For example, heavy armor may prevent certain stealth clothing bonuses from functioning.


## 6.4 Carrying capacity


Use weight and volume together.


Base values:


| Character state      | Carry weight |
| -------------------- | ------------ |
| Base character       | 35 kg        |
| Improved strength    | 45 kg        |
| Heavy backpack       | +15 kg       |
| Advanced backpack    | +25 kg       |
| Specialized mutation | +10 kg       |


Volume represents physical space, while weight represents carrying burden.


A large lightweight object may occupy substantial volume without making the player significantly slower.


## 6.5 Encumbrance levels


| Carry capacity used | Condition  | Effect                                  |
| ------------------- | ---------- | --------------------------------------- |
| 0–49%               | Light      | Normal movement                         |
| 50–74%              | Loaded     | Normal movement, slight stamina penalty |
| 75–89%              | Heavy      | Movement -10%                           |
| 90–99%              | Very heavy | Movement -25%, stamina cost +20%        |
| 100%+               | Overloaded | Sprint disabled, movement -50%          |


Some objects may be too large to carry regardless of remaining weight.


## 6.6 Item data


Every item should contain:


| Field             | Purpose                  |
| ----------------- | ------------------------ |
| ID                | Unique identifier        |
| Name              | Display name             |
| Description       | Item information         |
| Icon              | UI image                 |
| Category          | Item type                |
| Rarity            | Classification           |
| Weight            | Carry burden             |
| Volume            | Storage space            |
| Stack size        | Maximum stack            |
| Value             | Economic worth           |
| Durability        | Remaining condition      |
| Condition         | Item quality             |
| Quantity          | Current amount           |
| Use action        | Available interaction    |
| Equip slot        | Equipment location       |
| Recipe references | Crafting relationships   |
| Tags              | Search and filtering     |
| Restrictions      | Special use requirements |


## 6.7 Item rarity


| Rarity    | Classification       |
| --------- | -------------------- |
| Common    | Frequently available |
| Uncommon  | Less frequent        |
| Rare      | Specialized          |
| Epic      | High-value           |
| Legendary | Extremely scarce     |
| Unique    | One-of-a-kind        |


## 6.8 Item stacking


Suggested stack limits:


| Item type          | Maximum stack        |
| ------------------ | -------------------- |
| Small materials    | 100                  |
| Components         | 50                   |
| Ammunition         | 100                  |
| Food               | 20                   |
| Medical items      | 10                   |
| Weapons            | 1                    |
| Armor              | 1                    |
| Biological samples | 5                    |
| Quest items        | Defined individually |
| Large objects      | 1                    |


Stacks should automatically combine when the item ID and relevant item properties match.


Items with different condition, quality, or unique metadata may require separate stacks.


## 6.9 Inventory actions


Every item should support appropriate actions:


- Pick up.
- Drop.
- Equip.
- Unequip.
- Use.
- Consume.
- Inspect.
- Split.
- Combine.
- Transfer.
- Store.
- Sell.
- Trade.
- Repair.
- Dismantle.
- Favorite.
- Mark as junk.
- Lock against accidental disposal.


## 6.10 Inventory sorting and filtering


Filters should include:


- All items.
- Weapons.
- Armor.
- Clothing.
- Tools.
- Materials.
- Components.
- Food.
- Medicine.
- Biological.
- Botanical.
- Quest items.
- Valuables.
- Junk.


Sort by:


- Name.
- Quantity.
- Weight.
- Value.
- Rarity.
- Condition.
- Recently acquired.
- Category.


## 6.11 Item condition


Condition should affect item performance.


| Condition | Durability remaining |
| --------- | -------------------- |
| Broken    | 0%                   |
| Critical  | 1–10%                |
| Poor      | 11–30%               |
| Worn      | 31–60%               |
| Good      | 61–85%               |
| Excellent | 86–100%              |


## 6.12 Looting interface


When searching a container, show two inventory panels:


- Player inventory.
- Container inventory.


Support:


- Single-item transfer.
- Stack transfer.
- Transfer all.
- Quick loot.
- Sorting.
- Search.
- Weight warnings.
- Container capacity.


## 6.13 Storage persistence


Every storage container should have:


- Unique ID.
- Location.
- Ownership.
- Capacity.
- Contents.
- Access permissions.
- Lock state.
- Condition.
- Inventory history.


Items stored in the world must persist across saving, loading, and region transitions.


# 7. TRAVEL AND TRANSPORTATION SYSTEM


[How The Last Of Us raised the bar for video game narratives | TechRadar](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/rlCH-eKqHtZ2FQW0pTbcPI2CCSEKUsju2_1JNdaZ4pfylHH-IFvDJ0gdc4s7m8inbHAL6PqVtJDI-Jw207hSVKKb9zDVcZ_BQckyvtQOCom2QQv1AaLXdmceqwtcP2rfJaFez2O5cNJMxIqY27-ioL8-Fxk1oWISXCYQ-s7ftTo?purpose=inline)


[The Day Before has been delayed until 2023 but it’s for a good reason | GamesRadar+](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/WYhM9OgvSX2c4PLG2Ip0XpTCJh4iHyt-_zwx6vYbFY7UXUvSq-RZl2MxewVKTea3n4gi8QNfJgdUC-_4n8lES9YPs416D7yKXSW40TJmQZ8pss_gP68muEJRbwruW0kYkIp5eGoRmDfYDINqpY3Hi9liYVJ8y6Y7IDjpJmW-SKQ?purpose=inline)


[Metro Awakening has been revealed for PC and other VR platforms - OC3D](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/B2gxYGgVls3VL6pr96TAc1kjaaOa58ltHO2Ccb3dmYN_DTkOVfdVf8Ztv0EYpurH-ulcu2GF_Xbbn54Wb2ICFaTOd1ccpnblhjk01dY2lQYVfDcG_yhGd1eEw_9B7vI-PDqNYZyWZLlXMibuSE1xXkGYjLMdLaAi4sejtafMuCE?purpose=inline)


14


## 7.1 Core purpose


Travel connects the player's immediate survival experience to the larger world.


It should make distance, geography, weather, resources, danger, and transportation meaningful.


The player should not simply click a map location and instantly appear there.


Travel should involve:


- Route planning.
- Navigation.
- Movement.
- Vehicle management.
- Fuel.
- Time.
- Weather.
- Terrain.
- Encounters.
- Cargo.
- Companions.
- Road conditions.
- Regional ownership.
- Discovery.


## 7.2 Travel modes


| Mode              | Advantages           | Disadvantages            |
| ----------------- | -------------------- | ------------------------ |
| Walking           | Quiet, flexible      | Slow                     |
| Sprinting         | Fast escape          | High stamina consumption |
| Crouch movement   | Lower visibility     | Slow                     |
| Crawling          | Very low profile     | Extremely slow           |
| Bicycle           | Quiet, efficient     | Limited cargo            |
| Motorcycle        | Fast                 | Noise and fuel           |
| Car               | Balanced             | Road dependence          |
| Pickup truck      | High cargo capacity  | Fuel consumption         |
| Off-road vehicle  | Rough terrain access | Maintenance              |
| Armored vehicle   | High protection      | Expensive and noisy      |
| Boat              | Water travel         | Waterway restrictions    |
| Underground route | Concealed movement   | Restricted access        |


## 7.3 Movement statistics


Suggested base movement speeds:


| Movement     | Speed   |
| ------------ | ------- |
| Walking      | 1.4 m/s |
| Fast walking | 2.0 m/s |
| Jogging      | 3.0 m/s |
| Sprinting    | 5.5 m/s |
| Crouching    | 0.8 m/s |
| Crawling     | 0.3 m/s |


These values should be adjusted for terrain, injuries, encumbrance, fatigue, equipment, and mutations.


## 7.4 Travel calculation


\\[ T=\frac{D}{V\times M} \\]


Where:


- \\(T\\) = travel time.
- \\(D\\) = distance.
- \\(V\\) = base movement speed.
- \\(M\\) = combined movement modifier.


Movement modifier includes:


- Terrain.
- Weather.
- Character condition.
- Equipment.
- Vehicle condition.
- Road condition.


## 7.5 World navigation


The map should have several layers.


| Map layer          | Information                            |
| ------------------ | -------------------------------------- |
| World map          | Major regions                          |
| Regional map       | Roads, settlements, terrain            |
| Local map          | Nearby points of interest              |
| Tactical map       | Immediate threats and tactical details |
| Interior map       | Building layouts                       |
| Underground map    | Tunnels, bunkers, sewers               |
| Vehicle navigation | Routes and vehicle condition           |
| Faction map        | Territory ownership                    |
| Resource map       | Known resource locations               |
| Wildlife map       | Known animal habitats                  |
| Discovery map      | Explored and unexplored areas          |


Map information should be revealed through exploration, maps, radio intelligence, NPCs, and research.


## 7.6 Route planning


Every route should calculate:


- Distance.
- Travel time.
- Terrain difficulty.
- Road quality.
- Enemy density.
- Faction checkpoints.
- Resource opportunities.
- Weather exposure.
- Water availability.
- Safe resting locations.
- Vehicle compatibility.
- Alternate routes.


The player should be able to choose a route based on speed, safety, stealth, or resource opportunities.


## 7.7 Vehicles


Each vehicle requires a full stat sheet.


| Attribute           | Purpose                      |
| ------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| Vehicle ID          | Unique identity              |
| Vehicle type        | Car, truck, motorcycle, etc. |
| Health              | Structural condition         |
| Engine condition    | Mechanical state             |
| Fuel capacity       | Maximum fuel                 |
| Current fuel        | Remaining fuel               |
| Speed               | Travel speed                 |
| Acceleration        | Speed response               |
| Handling            | Steering                     |
| Cargo capacity      | Storage                      |
| Passenger capacity  | Occupants                    |
| Noise               | Detection risk               |
| Off-road capability | Terrain compatibility        |
| Armor               | Protection                   |
| Durability          | Component lifespan           |


Vehicle conditions:


- Engine.
- Tires.
- Battery.
- Fuel system.
- Suspension.
- Brakes.
- Bodywork.
- Electrical system.
- Cargo storage.


## 7.8 Vehicle maintenance


Vehicles require:


- Fuel.
- Repair materials.
- Spare parts.
- Tires.
- Batteries.
- Tools.
- Maintenance time.


Damage should affect performance.


For example:


- Damaged tires reduce handling.
- Damaged engines reduce speed.
- Low fuel can strand the player.
- Damaged suspension increases terrain penalties.
- Poor body condition reduces protection.


## 7.9 Travel encounters


Travel should generate meaningful events.


| Encounter                | Possible outcome          |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------- |
| Infected patrol          | Combat or evasion         |
| Horde crossing           | Detour or stealth         |
| Survivor group           | Trade or dialogue         |
| Merchant convoy          | Commerce                  |
| Distress signal          | Rescue opportunity        |
| Vehicle breakdown        | Repair or abandonment     |
| Roadblock                | Alternate route           |
| Faction checkpoint       | Inspection or negotiation |
| Wildlife encounter       | Hunting or danger         |
| Abandoned supply vehicle | Scavenging                |
| Refugee convoy           | Social event              |
| Territorial conflict     | Combat or diplomacy       |


Encounters should be based on location, time, route danger, and world-state conditions rather than completely random spawning.


## 7.10 Travel survival


Long journeys should require planning.


Track:


- Food.
- Water.
- Medical supplies.
- Fuel.
- Ammunition.
- Vehicle condition.
- Weather protection.
- Sleep.
- Fatigue.
- Blood hunger.
- Biological stability.
- Companion needs.


## 7.11 Fast travel


Fast travel should be earned rather than freely available everywhere.


Possible methods:


- Discovered safehouses.
- Secure roads.
- Established settlements.
- Reliable transport networks.
- Faction-controlled routes.
- Radio-guided transport.
- Special transportation services.


Fast travel should still advance the world simulation and consume appropriate resources.


## 7.12 Travel progression


Skills:


- Navigation.
- Survival travel.
- Driving.
- Vehicle maintenance.
- Tracking.
- Route scouting.
- Stealth movement.
- Wilderness endurance.


Perks can reduce fatigue, improve route discovery, lower fuel use, increase travel speed, or reduce encounter risk.


# 8. ADULT / NSFW SYSTEM


[In the Shadows of Steel | Story.com](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/80oV2Jt0DoTz9SxkpYIbnMEczUnWmekhtYhAKLr15Fynz3jR33g79FyuLKI2G1fPr3dX5yoxwIRvdsaKt8YiC6VNj1Plsa2HIonhEx_7vPxDkbx-kgjUISV6_z4E3cKJ3NiAZpADQKn8BQyii7IyBRXTsgIbntdqX-TwnkyggMs?purpose=inline)


[The Shelter - ElectroWolff Games](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/CYY9i21XuexopT-Lo8_IVltGKDYerMi6peEwvYTZ3mRvM1_hNB2cnbQFitji8xN2XOq0zsEmNhUf_ptQj4hKI5mLRuZNTYxPT4LFa_-f4owizi1hwAh7X0ZJcBwraQau1wDyBHRCfCrHCpo4CXH5ZMLDA8K5xBf6IcmJ0BLdtU8?purpose=inline)


[Broken Roads has the potential to become the next Disco Elysium | PC Gamer](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/psubWLawTqzGDDTb0v8iTgSsHtjnuT0jve_IJgwgzoRtSmeWrh-xbi3yTVuTqLV19Msxb_9SwMWAgtjrOqcsRcXBsL6_uPgJoHV075dgRV6zK2dWHVjJznlcf4IC9fgqVSqg_jKSL8mKt-gx1XNWj4gf8H4SH6vI-LIEX8L4JuE?purpose=inline)


14


## 8.1 Core purpose


The adult content system should make High and Undead a mature survival RPG with adult themes, relationships, romance, intimacy, complicated human behavior, and the realities of life after civilization collapses.


It should not be reduced to explicit scenes or decorative character interactions.


The system should explore:


- Romance.
- Attraction.
- Emotional intimacy.
- Adult relationships.
- Sexual health and safety themes.
- Consent.
- Trust.
- Jealousy.
- Commitment.
- Breakups.
- Family.
- Pregnancy and parenthood, if included.
- Relationship conflict.
- Social boundaries.
- Adult nightlife.
- Mature dialogue.
- Psychological consequences of intimacy in a dangerous world.


All sexual or romantic content must involve clearly adult characters. The game should never sexualize minors, and sexual violence or coercion should never be presented as an erotic reward or player-controlled sexual activity.


## 8.2 Adult content settings


The game should have a dedicated content settings menu.


| Setting               | Options                                                 |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| Mature dialogue       | On / Off                                                |
| Romance               | On / Off                                                |
| Non-explicit intimacy | On / Off                                                |
| Sexual references     | On / Off                                                |
| Nudity                | Off / Restricted to permitted non-explicit presentation |
| Violence              | Adjustable                                              |
| Gore                  | Off / Reduced / Full                                    |
| Strong language       | On / Off                                                |
| Drug references       | On / Off                                                |
| Horror intensity      | Reduced / Standard / High                               |
| Relationship drama    | On / Off                                                |
| Pregnancy themes      | On / Off, if implemented                                |


The exact presentation and distribution settings must follow the target platform's content policies and applicable age restrictions.


## 8.3 Character relationship system


Each eligible adult NPC should have a relationship profile.


| Attribute           | Range | Meaning                       |
| ------------------- | ----- | ----------------------------- |
| Trust               | 0–100 | Confidence in player          |
| Affection           | 0–100 | Emotional attachment          |
| Attraction          | 0–100 | Romantic interest             |
| Respect             | 0–100 | Opinion of player             |
| Commitment          | 0–100 | Relationship dedication       |
| Compatibility       | 0–100 | Shared values and personality |
| Jealousy            | 0–100 | Relationship insecurity       |
| Relationship stress | 0–100 | Current conflict              |


These are separate values. High attraction does not automatically mean high trust or consent.


## 8.4 Relationship stages


| Stage             | Description                       |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------- |
| Stranger          | No relationship                   |
| Acquaintance      | Basic familiarity                 |
| Friend            | Positive personal connection      |
| Close friend      | Strong emotional trust            |
| Romantic interest | Mutual attraction may develop     |
| Dating            | Established romantic relationship |
| Committed         | Long-term partnership             |
| Complicated       | Conflict or uncertainty           |
| Separated         | Relationship ended                |
| Reconciled        | Relationship restored             |


Relationships must be based on character-specific preferences and mutually chosen interactions, not a single generic affection meter.


## 8.5 Consent and boundaries


Consent must be an explicit part of the relationship system.


Rules:


- Intimacy requires mutual consent.
- Consent can be withdrawn.
- A refusal must be respected.
- High affection does not imply consent.
- Alcohol or fictional substances must not be used to bypass consent.
- Threats, coercion, captivity, or manipulation must not unlock sexual rewards.
- Characters can establish personal boundaries.
- The player can set boundaries too.
- Relationship consequences should be meaningful and persistent.


## 8.6 Relationship interactions


Examples of non-explicit adult interactions:


- Conversation.
- Compliments.
- Shared meals.
- Dancing.
- Spending time together.
- Gifts.
- Personal conversations.
- Sharing memories.
- Mutual affection.
- Kissing, where permitted by the game's content rules.
- Choosing to share accommodation.
- Fade-to-black intimacy.
- Long-term commitment.


Each interaction should have requirements, outcomes, and relationship effects.


## 8.7 Adult social locations


| Location            | Activities                              |
| ------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| Community bar       | Conversation and social events          |
| Settlement lounge   | Socializing                             |
| Private quarters    | Personal relationships                  |
| Dance hall          | Community entertainment                 |
| Festival grounds    | Celebrations                            |
| Private residence   | Domestic relationships                  |
| Community gathering | Social bonds                            |
| Medical clinic      | Sexual health education and care themes |


## 8.8 Relationship consequences


Relationships should affect the larger game.


Examples:


- A companion may defend the player.
- A partner may object to reckless behavior.
- A breakup can affect morale.
- A trusted relationship may unlock personal quests.
- A character may leave a dangerous settlement.
- Family responsibilities may influence settlement decisions.
- Romantic partners can have independent goals and relationships.


NPCs should have their own agency. They should not exist solely as romance options.


## 8.9 Mature themes and transformation


The player's undead transformation creates additional relationship complications.


Possible themes:


- Fear of physical changes.
- Acceptance.
- Trust.
- Physical vulnerability.
- Social stigma.
- Fear of bloodlust.
- Concerns about safety.
- Relationship adaptation.
- Human-undead cultural conflict.


A partner's response should depend on their personality, knowledge, beliefs, experiences, and relationship history.


## 8.10 Content presentation


Use a dedicated presentation layer:


- Age-gated entry where appropriate.
- Content warnings.
- Player-controlled content filters.
- Fade-to-black transitions.
- Relationship summaries.
- Dialogue boundaries.
- Scene availability rules.
- Persistent relationship state.


The goal is a mature, emotionally grounded game, not a collection of disconnected adult scenes.


# 9. STEALTH SYSTEM


[The Last of Us Part II dwingt je te doden](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/7jNTTI4BLVfOu7ZkfittH0JAN9U3IzPQBl-Rne2cgAxegjx2_Ay5QvTuW6wY5p3XkbLt1wTxQa1h_CLfkiOzDS09sv3okbXMAk8RzoehtP5yZRIpe0ZlqoJdcBy2r122b_pf_jZlLFEG2P6YC1BGlDRtJ8AqHQ_neeRyDoHyrYY?purpose=inline)


[The Last Of Us Part II Remastered - Revving The ViolenCycle! (Review)](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/fbyGyOHQzbUdB3cWodhwXJJK_DsEVjSp9bzII-UGCZVbqOGnTSCVc-ZOvd6mhuHoltN_-BAlEn_C3fHW0Zyz_GLqsoDPLDbMbfoFBjAJ-vaTkdMG2FAaJXCgilupl9GqXVKBUcq53-aa1gz50wgdWneVPiI_YYQQBUiKrRFfMiU?purpose=inline)


[DAYZ - PS4 PHYSICAL (EN)  – BOHEMIA INTERACTIVE](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/knwd0BmTB7js4BRPf-uYB7yfngoayOPHle79xnkPuFdyGFvG88bdbDJZMRT9uJacpz0Vvd9BlUYno3SSb_7KUWX-XQxKirZwC_WqjpApt5_ZFm2EQCdv-ofoTT79OTjOUav1-NSWNLIe2a0jt8Xk7gGCR_dZb9QH5EAjhcaGDYM?purpose=inline)


12


## 9.1 Core purpose


Stealth allows the player to avoid detection, bypass enemies, infiltrate locations, gather information, escape dangerous situations, and complete objectives without unnecessary combat.


Stealth should be a complete alternative playstyle, not merely crouching.


It should account for:


- Visibility.
- Noise.
- Scent.
- Light.
- Movement.
- Cover.
- Disguise.
- Environmental conditions.
- Enemy senses.
- Enemy intelligence.
- Distraction.
- Concealment.
- Social infiltration.
- Evidence and suspicion.


## 9.2 Detection channels


Enemies should have several detection systems.


| Detection type       | What it detects             |
| -------------------- | --------------------------- |
| Vision               | Visible characters          |
| Hearing              | Footsteps, impacts, weapons |
| Scent                | Biological presence         |
| Movement             | Motion and sudden activity  |
| Light                | Silhouette and illumination |
| Proximity            | Nearby presence             |
| Social observation   | Suspicious human behavior   |
| Biological detection | Undead or hybrid traits     |


Different enemy species should use different combinations.


A normal human may rely on sight and sound. A mutated hunter may rely heavily on scent and movement.


## 9.3 Visibility


Visibility should be affected by:


- Light level.
- Distance.
- Movement speed.
- Character silhouette.
- Clothing color.
- Cover.
- Weather.
- Vegetation.
- Environmental contrast.
- Enemy vision.


Suggested visibility multipliers:


| Condition        | Visibility modifier |
| ---------------- | ------------------- |
| Full daylight    | 1.00x               |
| Cloudy daylight  | 0.85x               |
| Dusk             | 0.65x               |
| Night            | 0.40x               |
| Darkness         | 0.20x               |
| Dense vegetation | 0.50x               |
| Heavy rain       | 0.70x               |
| Fog              | 0.45x               |
| Full sprint      | 1.50x               |
| Crouching        | 0.65x               |


These are initial gameplay modifiers. Darkness should not make the player completely invisible.


## 9.4 Noise system


Every action should generate a sound value.


| Action             | Noise value |
| ------------------ | ----------- |
| Slow crouch        | 2           |
| Normal walking     | 8           |
| Fast walking       | 12          |
| Sprinting          | 25          |
| Jumping            | 20          |
| Landing heavily    | 30          |
| Opening quiet door | 5           |
| Breaking door      | 65          |
| Melee impact       | 20–45       |
| Gunshot            | 80–100      |
| Vehicle engine     | 70–100      |


Noise should propagate through the environment.


Factors:


- Distance.
- Walls.
- Doors.
- Terrain.
- Wind.
- Weather.
- Indoor or outdoor location.
- Soundproofing.
- Enemy hearing.


## 9.5 Detection formula


A simplified detection score:


\\[ D_s=V+N+S+L+M-C \\]


Where:


- \\(V\\) = visual detection.
- \\(N\\) = noise detection.
- \\(S\\) = scent detection.
- \\(L\\) = light exposure.
- \\(M\\) = movement exposure.
- \\(C\\) = concealment.


Each component should be weighted by enemy sensory capability.


Detection states:


| Detection score | State             |
| --------------- | ----------------- |
| 0–19            | Unaware           |
| 20–39           | Suspicious        |
| 40–59           | Investigating     |
| 60–79           | Alert             |
| 80–99           | Target identified |
| 100+            | Full detection    |


## 9.6 Stealth movement


Movement states:


- Standing.
- Walking.
- Crouching.
- Crawling.
- Leaning.
- Peeking.
- Hiding.
- Moving between cover.
- Climbing.
- Quiet interaction.


Each state should have:


- Speed.
- Noise.
- Visibility.
- Stamina cost.
- Animation.
- Detection modifier.


## 9.7 Cover and concealment


Cover protects against vision and potentially incoming attacks.


Types:


- Vegetation.
- Vehicles.
- Walls.
- Furniture.
- Debris.
- Buildings.
- Shadows.
- Containers.
- Structural openings.


Cover must have a concealment rating and physical geometry.


The player should not become invisible simply because they are standing near a wall.


## 9.8 Distraction system


Players should be able to redirect enemy attention.


Distraction objects:


- Thrown objects.
- Noise makers.
- Alarm systems.
- Radios.
- Environmental machinery.
- Decoy devices.
- Triggered environmental events.


Enemies should investigate the source, communicate with nearby allies, and eventually return to patrol if they find nothing.


## 9.9 Hiding system


Hiding places:


- Closets.
- Under beds.
- Vehicles.
- Storage containers.
- Tall vegetation.
- Ruined structures.
- Underground areas.
- Concealed rooms.


Hiding places should have:


- Entry animation.
- Exit animation.
- Visibility rating.
- Noise rating.
- Escape options.
- Capacity.
- Detection rules.
- Environmental limitations.


Hiding should not guarantee safety if an enemy has a strong scent or investigation ability.


## 9.10 Social stealth


Humans should react to the player's appearance and behavior.


Social stealth should include:


- Clothing and uniforms.
- Faction identification.
- Reputation.
- Suspicion.
- Restricted access.
- False credentials as fictional gameplay items.
- Dialogue checks.
- Disguises.
- Behavioral consistency.
- Witnesses.
- Security inspections.


The player's undead condition should make social infiltration more complicated.


For example, a human settlement may permit entry to a trusted hybrid character but deny entry to a visibly mutated stranger.


## 9.11 Stealth takedowns


Takedowns should have:


- Target eligibility.
- Position requirements.
- Noise.
- Duration.
- Stamina cost.
- Detection risk.
- Target resistance.
- Nonlethal and lethal options where appropriate.
- Animation states.
- Consequences.


Not every enemy should be eligible. A massive brute or heavily armored enemy should resist ordinary stealth takedowns.


## 9.12 Stealth progression


| Skill           | Benefit                            |
| --------------- | ---------------------------------- |
| Silent Movement | Reduced footstep noise             |
| Concealment     | Improved hiding                    |
| Observation     | Improved enemy awareness           |
| Infiltration    | Improved restricted-area access    |
| Distraction     | Improved distraction effectiveness |
| Scent Control   | Reduced scent detection            |
| Night Movement  | Improved low-light movement        |
| Ambush          | Improved opening attacks           |
| Escape Artist   | Improved escape success            |


## 9.13 Stealth and bloodlust


This connection is especially important.


High bloodlust can make stealth harder:


- Increased fixation on blood sources.
- Less predictable movement.
- Greater risk of involuntary noise.
- Increased difficulty resisting nearby targets.
- Stronger scent awareness.


But it can also offer stealth-related benefits:


- Better tracking.
- Enhanced hearing.
- Improved detection of living targets.
- Ability to sense enemies through certain environmental conditions.


The player must decide whether to control the predator or use it.


# 10. How all nine systems connect


This is the most important part of the architecture.


The systems should not operate independently. They should exchange data through shared gameplay services.


## The gameplay ecosystem


GATHERING


Resources, salvage, food, biological samples


INVENTORY


Storage, weight, equipment, item condition


CRAFTING


Weapons, armor, tools, medicine, components


BUILDING


Safehouses, workshops, farms, settlements


COMBAT


Defense and conflict


STEALTH


Concealment and infiltration


BLOODLUST


Biology and instinct


TRAVEL


Exploration and discovery


WORLD AND CIVILIZATION SIMULATION


Population, factions, economy, relationships, territory and persistent consequences


## 10.1 Example gameplay chain


Imagine the player discovers an abandoned industrial building.


1. Travel to the building using a back road.
2. Use stealth to avoid a group of infected.
3. Enter through a damaged service entrance.
4. Search storage containers.
5. Gather metal, electronics, and mechanical components.
6. Store materials in the backpack.
7. Discover a rare biological sample.
8. Bloodlust increases after exposure to infected remains.
9. The player must control their instincts to avoid attracting attention.
10. Escape with the collected materials.
11. Return to the settlement.
12. Craft weapon upgrades and electrical components.
13. Use those components to construct a powered workshop.
14. Assign an NPC technician.
15. The workshop begins producing equipment.
16. The settlement becomes more defensible.
17. A neighboring faction notices the settlement's growing industrial capacity.
18. A trade opportunity or territorial conflict develops.


One expedition has affected nine systems and potentially changed the world.


# 11. Shared technical architecture


These systems need shared foundations to prevent duplicated code and inconsistent rules.


Suggested architecture:

```
    ├── SaveManager.js
    ├── DataRegistry.js
    ├── ValidationManager.js
    └── BalanceConfig.js
```


## 11.1 Shared event system


Every system should communicate through standardized events.


Examples:


| Event                          | Trigger                      |
| ------------------------------ | ---------------------------- |
| `resource:gathered`            | Resource collected           |
| `item:created`                 | Crafting completed           |
| `item:equipped`                | Equipment changed            |
| `item:broken`                  | Item durability reaches zero |
| `combat:damage`                | Combat damage applied        |
| `combat:enemy_killed`          | Enemy defeated               |
| `bloodlust:changed`            | Bloodlust changes            |
| `bloodlust:override`           | Instinct override begins     |
| `building:completed`           | Structure completed          |
| `building:destroyed`           | Structure destroyed          |
| `travel:region_changed`        | Region transition            |
| `travel:encounter`             | Travel encounter begins      |
| `stealth:detected`             | Player discovered            |
| `relationship:changed`         | Relationship changes         |
| `settlement:resource_shortage` | Settlement supply shortage   |


This lets systems react without directly depending on one another's internal implementation.


# 12. Definition of done


A system should not be considered implemented simply because it has a menu or a button.


| System    | Minimum implementation requirement                               |
| --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Crafting  | Working recipes, resource consumption, outputs, stations, queues |
| Combat    | Damage, hit detection, weapons, armor, enemies, injuries         |
| Bloodlust | Separate meters, triggers, decay, effects, control checks        |
| Gathering | Resource nodes, interactions, yields, depletion, regeneration    |
| Building  | Placement, construction, collision, structural health, upgrades  |
| Inventory | Items, stacking, weight, equipment, transfers, persistence       |
| Travel    | Navigation, movement, routes, encounters, vehicles               |
| NSFW      | Adult character rules, consent, relationships, content controls  |
| Stealth   | Detection, noise, visibility, scent, hiding, enemy investigation |


Every system also needs:


- User interface.
- Audio and animation feedback.
- Save/load support.
- Error handling.
- Data validation.
- Balancing configuration.
- Accessibility.
- Responsive controls.
- Automated tests.


Important distinction: This is the detailed design specification, not a claim that these features are already implemented in the source code.


My recommended implementation order is to build the shared item and resource foundation first, then inventory, gathering, crafting, combat, bloodlust, building, stealth, travel, and finally the mature relationship layer. This creates a working foundation instead of nine disconnected feature demos.


The north star: Every action should consume something, produce something, change something, or create a consequence. That is how Phoenix Blaze becomes a genuinely interconnected survival RPG.


Core Premise: You play as a protagonist who has survived the transformation into a zombie but somehow retained their human consciousness, memories, and emotions—creating a constant tension between survival instincts and humanity.


Key Mechanics: Features unique survival loops such as managing bloodlust, fictional cannabis-based crafting for suppression and enhancement, stealth, social disguise, and branching moral choices.


Project Architecture: Details directory structures (src/game/core, systems, entities, etc.), a technology stack (Phaser 3, JavaScript ES6+, Webpack/Parcel, Jest), and setup/installation instructions for local development.


Development Roadmap & Expansion: Outlines a 4-phase development roadmap (Foundation, Core Mechanics, Content, and Polish) followed by a massive append-only expansion compendium covering advanced visual styling, world regions, combat design, NPC intelligence, and production readiness.




That inversion of the classic survival formula—turning the Last Day on Earth or Dawn of Zombies top-down isometric survival loop completely on its head—creates a brilliant mechanical and narrative playground.


In standard isometric survival RPGs, your enemy is the mindless horde, and human settlements represent safety and trade. In Phoenix Blaze, that dynamic is shattered: you are the monster trying to pass as human, and humanity is the most dangerous, unpredictable faction in the wasteland.


🕹️ Translating Isometric Survival Mechanics to a Conscious Undead Protagonist
Adapting the proven mechanics of games like Last Day on Earth, Prey Day, and Lost in Blue to a half-human/half-zombie protagonist redefines every core loop:


Traditional Survival Mechanic        Phoenix Blaze Inverted Mechanic        Strategic Implication
Hunger & Thirst Meters        Bloodlust & Humanity Meters        Eating raw meat or scavenging rations doesn't just stave off starvation—it feeds your internal monster. Suppressing bloodlust requires specialized crafting (cannabis-derived terpenes and strains) to keep your human consciousness anchored.
Stealth & Sound Cones        Disguise & Decay Management        You have to actively mask your physical deterioration. If your skin sloughs off or your gait falters near a human patrol outpost, your disguise fails and they treat you as a hostile threat. Conversely, walking too upright among a zombie horde triggers their predatory aggression.
Base Building & Fortification        The Sanctuary / Hideout        Instead of just building walls to keep zombies out, you need specialized workstations: a botanical grow-room to refine suppressants, a concealment workshop to craft human disguises, and a medical bench to treat tissue necrosis.
Zone Grinding & Resource Runs        Bifurcated Territory Access        Exploring zones requires navigating two entirely different threat tables: Human Sectors (where you risk detection, traps, and scavengers) and Necrotic Sectors (where mindless hordes ignore you, but apex undead mutations and environmental decay hazards threaten you).
Co-op & Multiplayer Raids        Faction Infiltration & Betrayal        Posing as a survivor to infiltrate human settlements allows you to trade or sabotage from the inside, but a single slip in social interaction or scent masking turns the entire settlement hostile.

🧠 DESIGN INTENT
By utilizing the familiar, addictive progression loops of mobile and browser survival games (resource grinding, workbench upgrades, map exploration), the player instantly understands how to play. The innovation lies entirely in why they are doing it: managing an internal civil war between human reason and undead hunger.

1. **Character Creation**: Begin with a basic character model. Focus on implementing simple character customization—skin color, clothing, and body features. Unity’s **ProBuilder** and **Character Creator** tools can help you get this up and running.


2. **Movement & Controls**: Get basic player movement down. Use Unity's built-in **CharacterController** for smooth movement and simple animations for walking, sneaking, and idle poses. 


3. **Basic Survival System**: Implement a simple hunger, thirst, or health system. Add a basic marijuana mechanic where consuming it can regenerate health or manage bloodlust. You can build the system with Unity’s **UI** tools to track and display these stats.


4. **Environment Setup**: Create a small test area—a basic urban or forest environment. Use Unity’s **Terrain Tools** to quickly shape the landscape, and **Asset Store** assets for buildings or props (if you need them). You could start with just a small patch of world to explore and test mechanics.


5. **Simple Crafting System**: Start by allowing the player to gather basic materials (like sticks and hemp) and craft simple tools (like a sharpened stick). Use **Inventory Systems** in Unity to track the materials and items created.


6. **AI Behavior (Zombies/Animals)**: Set up basic AI for zombies or animals. Zombies can roam aimlessly or be triggered by noise, and animals can be interacted with (hunting, taming). Unity’s **NavMesh** and **Animator** can help you create simple AI and animations for them.


7. **Basic Stealth/Combat**: Implement a simple stealth system where the player can hide or crouch to avoid detection. Zombies could be given a simple “line of sight” AI behavior, and you could experiment with rudimentary combat—like a basic attack with a stick or other tool.


8. **Testing and Iteration**: As you build, constantly test the mechanics and make sure things feel smooth. Iterate based on what works and refine the features over time.


Unity’s flexibility makes it great for taking gradual steps. With each milestone, you can refine and expand the game. Would you like some tips on setting up any of these systems in Unity?

2. **Initial Gameplay Loop:**
* ## Tutorial Level Design: "Waking Nightmare"


**Objective:** Introduce players to core mechanics in a safe environment that reinforces the narrative context.


**Environment:**


* **Starting Point:** The bathroom where the character wakes up. Dimly lit, disheveled, with the mirror reflecting their zombified form.
* **Adjacent Offices:** Mostly empty, with overturned furniture and scattered debris providing opportunities for stealth and exploration. Some interactive elements like a flickering light or dripping faucet add atmosphere.
* **Hallway:** A linear path leading to an exit, with a few blind corners and obstacles. This introduces basic movement and the concept of navigating the environment.
* **Small Courtyard:** An enclosed outdoor area with overgrown plants, offering hiding spots and a chance to experiment with stealth mechanics. A couple of low-level zombies wander aimlessly.


**Core Mechanic Introductions:**


* **Movement:**
    * Upon waking up, the character feels sluggish and disoriented. Movement is initially slow and clunky, reflecting their newfound state.
    * A prompt guides the player to use basic movement controls (WASD or arrow keys). 
    * As they move, they gain more control, eventually reaching a normal walking speed. This subtly reinforces the idea of adapting to their new body.


* **Stealth:**
    * Entering the offices, the player encounters their first zombie. It's slow and easily avoidable, but a prompt suggests trying to sneak past it.
    * The game introduces a simple stealth meter, indicating how visible the character is. Crouching and staying in shadows reduce visibility.
    * The player is encouraged to use environmental objects like overturned desks and cabinets for cover.


* **Combat:**
    * Further down the hallway, a zombie blocks the path and cannot be avoided. A prompt suggests finding a weapon.
    * A nearby broken chair leg serves as the first weapon. Picking it up triggers a basic combat tutorial.
    * Combat focuses on simple melee attacks and timing. The goal is to defeat the zombie without taking too much damage.


* **Crafting:**
    * Reaching the courtyard, the player encounters a locked gate. A prompt suggests finding a way to open it.
    * Scattered nearby are some loose nails and a plank of wood. The game introduces a basic crafting menu.
    * Combining these items creates a makeshift crowbar, which can then be used to pry open the gate.


**Narrative Integration:**


* Throughout the level, internal monologues and flashbacks continue the story.
* The character struggles with their newfound identity, questioning their sanity and humanity.
* Finding a note or audio log from another survivor could introduce the concept of factions and the broader world.


**Additional Considerations:**


* **Visual Cues:** Use subtle visual effects to indicate interactive elements, stealth zones, and potential crafting materials.
* **Audio Feedback:** Sound effects play a vital role in immersion and gameplay. Footsteps, zombie groans, and environmental sounds help players navigate and anticipate threats.
* **Difficulty Curve:** Keep the initial challenges manageable to build confidence and encourage experimentation.
* **Replayability:** Include optional objectives or hidden areas to reward exploration and offer a reason to revisit the tutorial later.


**Example Tutorial Flow:**


1. Wake up in the bathroom, struggle with movement controls.
2. Explore the offices, learn to sneak past a zombie.
3. Encounter a blocking zombie, find a weapon, and engage in combat.
4. Reach the courtyard, craft a crowbar to open the gate.
5. Exit the courtyard, ending the tutorial and transitioning to the open world.


**AI Integration:**


* Procedural Generation: Could be used later to create variations of the office layout for replayability.
* Dynamic Difficulty: The AI could adjust zombie behavior or item placement based on player performance, ensuring a smooth learning curve.
* Narrative Adaptation: The character's internal monologue could be subtly tailored based on player choices or actions, adding a personal touch to the experience.


This tutorial level design aims to create a seamless onboarding experience that not only teaches the game's core mechanics but also immerses players in the narrative and prepares them for the challenges of the open world.

* ## Deeper into the Apocalypse: The World of High and Undead


**ChroniX: The Biotech Behemoth**


ChroniX wasn't simply a corporation; it was an empire built on innovation and ambition, its tendrils reaching into every facet of modern life. Founded by the enigmatic Dr. Elias Thorne, a visionary geneticist with a penchant for pushing ethical boundaries, ChroniX quickly rose to prominence, its groundbreaking advancements in biotechnology and pharmaceuticals earning it both accolades and controversy.


Their sprawling headquarters, a gleaming monument to their success, was a fortress of laboratories, research facilities, and corporate offices. The company's influence extended far beyond its walls, shaping government policies, funding academic institutions, and even sponsoring popular culture events. But beneath the glossy exterior, a darkness festered. Rumors of clandestine experiments, ruthless corporate espionage, and a disregard for human life painted a chilling portrait of a company willing to do anything to achieve its goals.


The legalization of cannabis provided ChroniX with a new frontier to conquer. Their "Green Initiative" promised to revolutionize the industry, harnessing the plant's potential for both profit and progress. The "Euphoria" project, spearheaded by Dr. Thorne himself, was the crown jewel of this initiative. A genetically modified strain, engineered for unparalleled potency and effects, it was poised to become the next blockbuster drug, a cash cow that would solidify ChroniX's dominance for years to come.


**The Outbreak: A Night of Revelry Turned Nightmare**


The annual ChroniX employee party was a spectacle of excess, a celebration of the company's achievements and a testament to its power. Champagne flowed freely, gourmet food was plentiful, and the air crackled with the energy of ambition and success.  


But amidst the revelry, a silent killer lurked.  A single vial of the "Euphoria" strain, contaminated with an experimental viral agent, found its way into the hands of an unsuspecting partygoer. Within hours, the virus had spread throughout the venue, transforming the joyous celebration into a scene of unimaginable horror.


The infected turned on their colleagues, their minds consumed by an insatiable hunger for flesh. The once-familiar faces of friends and coworkers became grotesque parodies of themselves, their eyes glazed over, their bodies contorted in a macabre dance of death.  The security forces, caught completely off guard, were quickly overwhelmed.  Within a matter of hours, the ChroniX campus was transformed into a nightmarish hellscape, a breeding ground for the undead.


**Factions: A Fractured Landscape**


* **The ChroniX Inner Circle:** Dr. Thorne and a select group of loyalists retreated to a fortified underground bunker, their sanctuary hidden beneath the ruins of the corporate headquarters. Determined to salvage their legacy and regain control, they cling to the hope of developing a cure or a weapon to turn the tide of the outbreak. However, their isolation and desperation have bred paranoia and infighting, threatening to tear them apart from within.


* **The Resistance: Flames of Defiance:**  Born from the ashes of the outbreak, the Resistance is a ragtag alliance of survivors, united by their shared hatred of ChroniX and their refusal to surrender to the darkness. Led by a charismatic former ChroniX scientist, Dr. Anya Petrova, they operate from hidden bases, launching guerrilla attacks on corporate strongholds and scavenging for supplies. Their ranks are diverse, comprising scientists, engineers, soldiers, and everyday citizens who have found a common purpose in their fight for freedom.


* **The Scavengers: Survival at Any Cost:**  In the harsh reality of the apocalypse, morality is a luxury few can afford. The Scavengers are a testament to this grim truth, their loyalty lying solely with themselves. They roam the wasteland in packs, preying on the weak and vulnerable, their survival dependent on their cunning and ruthlessness. They are a constant threat to other factions, their presence a reminder of the brutal nature of this new world.


* **The Cultists: Embracing the Darkness:**  Drawn to the power and mystique of the undead, the Cultists see the outbreak as a divine revelation, a chance to transcend their mortal limitations and achieve a twisted form of immortality. Led by a enigmatic figure known only as "The Harbinger," they conduct dark rituals and embrace the infection, their bodies and minds becoming warped reflections of the undead they worship. Their ultimate goals remain shrouded in mystery, but their presence casts a sinister shadow over the wasteland.


* **The Lone Wolves: Solitary Wanderers:** The apocalypse has forced many to confront their own mortality, their trust in others shattered by the horrors they've witnessed. The Lone Wolves are survivors who have chosen to navigate this treacherous world alone, their independence their greatest strength and their isolation their heaviest burden. They are often skilled hunters, trackers, or scavengers, their knowledge of the wasteland invaluable to those who can earn their trust.


**Unraveling the Mystery: The Player's Role**


As a newly turned zombie with a unique consciousness, the player character stands at the crossroads of this fractured world. Their journey will lead them to encounter these various factions, each with their own agendas and motivations. Will they join forces with the Resistance to overthrow ChroniX? Will they succumb to the allure of the Cultists and embrace their newfound power? Or will they forge their own path, navigating the wasteland as a lone wolf, seeking answers and a way to reclaim their humanity?


The choices they make will shape the course of their journey and determine the fate of this shattered world. The truth behind the outbreak, the secrets of the "Euphoria" strain, and the possibility of a cure all lie waiting to be discovered. The player's unique abilities, their resilience, and their unwavering spirit will be their greatest weapons in this fight for survival and redemption.

# High and Undead / Phoenix Blaze


## The Complete Content & World-Building Data Bible


This is the next major layer of the game: turning the concepts into actual game-ready content.


The difference is important. Saying the game has blood crafting, cannabis genetics, animals, enemies, regions, and civilization simulation describes the systems. Building the data bible means defining what exists, what it does, where it appears, how it behaves, what it produces, and how it affects everything else.


I would organize this into five connected content databases:


1. Blood & Biological Systems
2. Cannabis Genetics, Cultivation & Processing
3. Complete Crafting and Item Catalog
4. Creatures, Enemies & Wildlife
5. World Geography, Population & Civilization Simulation


The values below are proposed original game-design data. Fictional recipes and biological effects are designed as gameplay mechanics, not real-world medical or cultivation instructions.


# 1. Blood System


The Blood System should be a complete survival, crafting, research, and transformation mechanic.


Blood is not just a consumable. It is a biological resource with different characteristics, qualities, compatibility ratings, contamination risks, and mutation interactions.


## 1.1 Blood source catalog


Create at least 10 blood-source classifications, each with unique gameplay properties.


| ID      | Blood source             | Rarity    | Quality | Primary gameplay property      |
| ------- | ------------------------ | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------ |
