| BLO-002 | Infected human           | Common    | 45      | Infection progression          |
| BLO-003 | Fresh infected           | Uncommon  | 65      | Strong undead compatibility    |
| BLO-004 | Mutated infected         | Rare      | 75      | Mutation research              |
| BLO-005 | Alpha infected           | Very rare | 90      | Advanced mutation catalyst     |
| BLO-006 | Hybrid blood             | Rare      | 85      | Hybrid compatibility           |
| BLO-007 | Animal blood             | Common    | 40      | Basic biological resource      |
| BLO-008 | Predatory animal         | Uncommon  | 60      | Sensory mutation research      |
| BLO-009 | Infected animal          | Rare      | 70      | Unstable biological properties |
| BLO-010 | Apex creature            | Legendary | 100     | Rare mutation research         |
| BLO-011 | Preserved blood sample   | Uncommon  | 80      | Research stability             |
| BLO-012 | Synthetic blood          | Rare      | 70      | Controlled compatibility       |

### Blood quality attributes


Each blood sample should store:


- Source species.
- Infection classification.
- Sample quality.

- Compatibility.
- Mutation signature.
- Storage condition.
- Collection date.
- Expiration time.
- Research value.
- Traceability information.

## 1.4 Blood risks


Every sample should have a risk profile.


| Risk                 | Trigger                        | Gameplay consequence            |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------- |
| Contamination        | Low-quality sample             | Increased instability           |
| Rejection            | Poor compatibility             | Reduced effect                  |
| Bloodlust spike      | High-intensity sample          | Increased aggression            |
| Mutation instability | Unstable catalyst              | Random mutation event           |
| Sensory overload     | Excessive sensory effect       | Temporary perception distortion |
| Cellular degradation | Expired sample                 | Reduced effectiveness           |
| Dependency           | Repeated use of specific serum | Increased resource demand       |
| Biological conflict  | Incompatible mutation          | Temporary ability penalty       |


## 1.5 Blood-to-mutation relationships


| Blood source     | Possible mutation              |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------ |
| Human survivor   | Memory preservation            |

| Mutated infected | Increased resilience           |
| Alpha infected   | Advanced regeneration          |
| Hybrid blood     | Balanced hybrid traits         |
| Predatory animal | Enhanced tracking              |
| Infected animal  | Unstable sensory evolution     |
| Apex creature    | Advanced biological adaptation |
| Ancient strain   | Rare ancestral mutation        |
| Synthetic blood  | Improved stability             |


Mutation outcomes should be influenced by compatibility, player stage, existing mutations, and research progression. No sample should guarantee a specific result unless the player has unlocked the necessary research.


# 2. Cannabis System


The Cannabis System should be a complete fictional agricultural and product ecosystem inside the game.


It needs its own genetics database, strain identities, growth simulation, plant conditions, harvest quality, processing outputs, market demand, and crafting connections.


The system should not be limited to growing a plant and collecting an item. It should connect to the economy, settlement industry, research, culture, faction identity, and character progression.


## 2.1 Strain catalog


Original fictional game strains, not real-world cultivar specifications.

| CAN-009 | Widowmaker        | Hybrid          | Rare      | High-demand genetics            |
| CAN-010 | Emerald Wraith    | Hybrid          | Epic      | Unusual genetic stability       |
| CAN-011 | Blood Moon        | Indica-inspired | Epic      | Rare pigment expression         |
| CAN-012 | Graveyard Haze    | Sativa-inspired | Rare      | Distinctive flavor profile      |
| CAN-013 | Mutation Mist     | Hybrid          | Legendary | Experimental genetics           |
| CAN-014 | Paradise Lost     | Hybrid          | Epic      | Premium market appeal           |
| CAN-015 | Last Light        | Hybrid          | Legendary | Rare heritage genetics          |
| CAN-016 | Undead OG         | Hybrid          | Legendary | Unique fictional mutation trait |


## 2.2 Genetic attributes


Each strain needs a genetic record.


| Attribute           | Range | Purpose                 |
| ------------------- | ----- | ----------------------- |
| Genetic stability   | 0–100 | Predictability          |
| Growth vigor        | 0–100 | Growth performance      |
| Yield potential     | 0–100 | Harvest potential       |
| Resilience          | 0–100 | Environmental tolerance |
| Water efficiency    | 0–100 | Water demand modifier   |
| Disease resistance  | 0–100 | Disease tolerance       |
| Aroma intensity     | 0–100 | Product identity        |
| Color expression    | 0–100 | Appearance              |
| Processing quality  | 0–100 | Output quality          |
| Market desirability | 0–100 | Commercial demand       |
| Research value      | 0–100 | Scientific interest     |


## 2.3 Growth simulation


Every plant should have the following gameplay states:


1. Seed inventory.
2. Germination.
3. Seedling.
4. Vegetative growth.
5. Flowering.
6. Harvest readiness.
7. Harvest.
8. Processing.
9. Finished product.
10. Storage or sale.


Proposed fictional game durations:


| Growth stage      | Game duration |
| ----------------- | ------------- |
| Germination       | 1 day         |
| Seedling          | 3 days        |
| Vegetative        | 7 days        |
| Flowering         | 10 days       |
| Harvest readiness | 2 days        |
| Total base cycle  | 23 days       |


Strain modifiers can change the total game duration.


| Genetic type | Growth-time modifier |
| ------------ | -------------------- |
| Fast-cycle   | -20%                 |
| Standard     | 0%                   |
| Slow-cycle   | +20%                 |
| Experimental | Variable             |


Plant condition attributes:


- Health.
- Hydration.
- Nutrition.
- Temperature suitability.
- Light suitability.
- Environmental stress.
- Disease pressure.
- Genetic stability.
- Growth progress.
- Harvest quality.


## 2.4 Harvest quality


A proposed harvest score:


\\[ Q_h = 0.25G + 0.20E + 0.20H + 0.15R + 0.20P \\]


Where:


- \\(G\\) = genetic quality.
- \\(E\\) = environmental suitability.
- \\(H\\) = plant health.
- \\(R\\) = resilience.
- \\(P\\) = processing quality.


| Harvest score | Product grade |
| ------------- | ------------- |
| 0–19          | Failed        |
| 20–39         | Low           |
| 40–59         | Standard      |
| 60–74         | Good          |
| 75–89         | Premium       |
| 90–100        | Legendary     |


## 2.5 Processing outputs


| Processing category | Output           | Gameplay use                  |
| ------------------- | ---------------- | ----------------------------- |
| Raw harvest         | Plant material   | Base crafting resource        |
| Dried harvest       | Dried product    | Storage and trade             |
| Refined product     | Refined material | Premium economy               |
| Botanical extract   | Extract          | Fictional crafting ingredient |
| Seed recovery       | Seeds            | Genetic collection            |
| Fiber recovery      | Plant fiber      | Textile crafting              |
| Composting          | Organic material | Settlement agriculture        |
| Research sample     | Genetic specimen | Research progression          |
| Decorative product  | Display item     | Settlement morale and trade   |


## 2.6 Cannabis system effects


In the fictional game world, different products can influence:


- Character morale.
- Companion relationship events.
- Settlement culture.
- Trade demand.
- Reputation.
- Relaxation-themed buffs.
- Perception effects.
- Crafting and research.
- Festival and community events.


Each effect needs a defined duration, magnitude, stacking rule, and drawback.


## 2.7 Genetic breeding system


Breeding should use inherited traits rather than simply selecting a random new strain.


| Trait              | Inheritance method                      |
| ------------------ | --------------------------------------- |
| Growth vigor       | Weighted parental average               |
| Resilience         | Weighted parental average               |
| Yield potential    | Weighted parental average               |
| Color expression   | Dominant/recessive trait rules          |
| Aroma              | Weighted trait inheritance              |
| Genetic stability  | Parental average with mutation variance |
| Disease resistance | Weighted parental average               |


A breeding result should have:


- Unique genetic identifier.
- Parent strain identifiers.
- Inherited trait values.
- Mutation events.
- Stability score.
- Generation number.
- Breeding history.
- Discovery status.


The game should allow players to discover original strains and register them in a genetic archive.


# 3. Complete Crafting Catalog


Crafting needs a unified item database. Every object must have a unique ID, category, rarity, stack limit, weight, value, durability, unlock requirement, crafting station, and recipe.

## 3.7 Crafting rules


Every recipe should include:


- Recipe ID.
- Input item IDs.
- Input quantities.
- Output item IDs.
- Output quantities.
- Required skill level.
- Required station.
- Crafting duration.
- Failure probability.
- Quality modifier.
- Unlock condition.
- XP reward.


Crafting quality should be calculated from skill, station quality, material quality, and environmental conditions.


# 4. Enemies, Creatures & Wildlife


The creature database should include much more than health and damage.


Every creature needs a complete stat sheet, AI behavior, sensory abilities, movement characteristics, animation states, spawn conditions, loot, and relationships with other species.


## 4.1 Enemy stat sheets


| ID     | Enemy          | HP    | Damage | Speed | Detection | Threat     |
| ------ | -------------- | ----- | ------ | ----- | --------- | ---------- |
| EN-001 | Crawler        | 45    | 8      | 25    | 30        | Low        |
| EN-002 | Shambler       | 70    | 12     | 35    | 40        | Low        |
| EN-003 | Runner         | 90    | 18     | 90    | 65        | Medium     |
| EN-004 | Stalker        | 110   | 22     | 70    | 80        | Medium     |
| EN-005 | Screamer       | 160   | 15     | 45    | 90        | High       |
| EN-006 | Spitter        | 130   | 20     | 40    | 70        | High       |
| EN-007 | Brute          | 250   | 35     | 30    | 55        | High       |
| EN-008 | Armored undead | 350   | 30     | 35    | 60        | High       |
| EN-009 | Mutated hunter | 400   | 45     | 100   | 95        | Very high  |
| EN-010 | Alpha infected | 750   | 55     | 65    | 85        | Boss       |
| EN-011 | Horde champion | 1,200 | 65     | 55    | 90        | Boss       |
| EN-012 | Apex mutation  | 2,500 | 85     | 80    | 100       | World boss |


## 4.2 Enemy behavior definitions


| Enemy          | Behavior                 | Special ability         |
| -------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------------- |
| Crawler        | Ambush from ground level | Grabs                   |
| Shambler       | Slow group pursuit       | Horde attraction        |
| Runner         | Fast direct pursuit      | Sprint attack           |
| Stalker        | Hides and follows        | Ambush                  |
| Screamer       | Maintains distance       | Summons nearby infected |
| Spitter        | Ranged harassment        | Biological projectile   |
| Brute          | Charges obstacles        | Heavy impact            |
| Armored undead | Advances defensively     | Armor resistance        |
| Mutated hunter | Tracks player            | Leap attack             |
| Alpha infected | Controls nearby infected | Rally behavior          |
| Horde champion | Leads large groups       | Rally and charge        |
| Apex mutation  | Multi-phase boss AI      | Adaptive attacks        |


## 4.3 Enemy AI states


Each enemy should support:


1. Idle.
2. Patrol.
3. Wander.
4. Investigate sound.
5. Detect scent.
6. Detect player.
7. Alert.
8. Pursue.
9. Attack.
10. Defend.
11. Search.
12. Retreat.
13. Call allies.
14. Return to territory.
15. Feed.
16. Rest.
17. Mutate.
18. Die.


Enemies should remember recent player activity according to their intelligence and sensory capabilities.


## 4.4 Animation requirements


| Animation     | Required states                  |
| ------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Idle          | Standing, breathing, twitching   |
| Movement      | Walk, run, crawl                 |
| Combat        | Light attack, heavy attack, grab |
| Defense       | Block, stagger, recoil           |
| Detection     | Listen, sniff, investigate       |
| Damage        | Hit reactions, knockback         |
| Death         | Collapse, fall, disintegration   |
| Special       | Scream, spit, charge, mutation   |
| Environmental | Climb, climb over, break through |


Animation speed should match movement speed and attack timing. Hit detection should use defined animation frames or animation events, not visual guesswork.


## 4.5 Loot tables


| Enemy          | Common loot         | Uncommon loot         | Rare loot             |
| -------------- | ------------------- | --------------------- | --------------------- |
| Crawler        | Organic matter      | Cloth                 | Biological sample     |
| Shambler       | Organic matter      | Scrap                 | Preserved sample      |
| Runner         | Organic matter      | Chemicals             | Mutation sample       |
| Stalker        | Organic matter      | Electronic scrap      | Sensory catalyst      |
| Screamer       | Organic matter      | Bio-material          | Vocal mutation sample |
| Spitter        | Organic matter      | Chemicals             | Biological catalyst   |
| Brute          | Scrap, bio-material | Rare alloy            | Mutation core         |
| Armored undead | Metal plates        | Components            | Armor parts           |
| Mutated hunter | Bio-material        | Mutation catalyst     | Rare genetic sample   |
| Alpha infected | Rare bio-material   | Research specimen     | Alpha catalyst        |
| Horde champion | Rare components     | Mutation core         | Legendary sample      |
| Apex mutation  | Legendary materials | Advanced bio-material | Apex core             |


Loot should use weighted probability tables.


Example:


| Drop category | Chance |
| ------------- | ------ |
| Common        | 65%    |
| Uncommon      | 25%    |
| Rare          | 8%     |
| Epic          | 1.8%   |
| Legendary     | 0.2%   |


These are illustrative category probabilities. Individual enemy tables need separate item weights and quantity ranges.


## 4.6 Spawn tables


Spawn generation should consider:


- Region danger rating.
- Time of day.
- Weather.
- Player level.
- Infection stage.
- Noise level.
- Settlement activity.
- World simulation events.
- Faction territory.
- Recent player actions.


| Region danger | Common enemies      | Uncommon enemies | Rare enemies    |
| ------------- | ------------------- | ---------------- | --------------- |
| 1             | Crawlers, shamblers | None             | None            |
| 2             | Shamblers, runners  | Stalkers         | None            |
| 3             | Runners, stalkers   | Spitters         | Brutes          |
| 4             | Brutes, screamers   | Armored undead   | Mutated hunters |
| 5             | Mixed hordes        | Alpha infected   | Horde champions |
| 6             | Advanced mutations  | Apex hunters     | Apex mutation   |


# 5. Animal System


Animals should function as living wildlife rather than decorative scenery.


They need their own needs, habitats, territorial behavior, reproduction, food chains, taming, domestication, and relationships with the player and settlements.


## 5.1 Species catalog


| ID      | Species       | Type           | Temperament       | Primary role         |
| ------- | ------------- | -------------- | ----------------- | -------------------- |
| ANI-001 | Domestic dog  | Mammal         | Loyal             | Companion            |
| ANI-002 | Feral dog     | Mammal         | Aggressive        | Predator             |
| ANI-003 | Wolf          | Mammal         | Territorial       | Pack predator        |
| ANI-004 | Deer          | Mammal         | Skittish          | Prey                 |
| ANI-005 | Wild boar     | Mammal         | Aggressive        | Prey/predator threat |
| ANI-006 | Rabbit        | Mammal         | Skittish          | Small prey           |
| ANI-007 | Fox           | Mammal         | Cautious          | Scavenger            |
| ANI-008 | Bear          | Mammal         | Territorial       | Apex predator        |
| ANI-009 | Crow          | Bird           | Intelligent       | Scavenger            |
| ANI-010 | Raven         | Bird           | Intelligent       | Scavenger            |
| ANI-011 | Rat           | Mammal         | Cautious          | Scavenger            |
| ANI-012 | Infected dog  | Mutated mammal | Aggressive        | Infected predator    |
| ANI-013 | Infected wolf | Mutated mammal | Pack aggressive   | Infected predator    |
| ANI-014 | Mutated deer  | Mutated mammal | Unpredictable     | Mutated wildlife     |
| ANI-015 | Apex predator | Mutated mammal | Highly aggressive | Rare boss wildlife   |
| ANI-016 | Domestic goat | Mammal         | Calm              | Settlement livestock |


## 5.2 Animal attributes


Every species should have:


| Attribute       | Range   |
| --------------- | ------- |
| Health          | 1–1,000 |
| Stamina         | 1–500   |
| Speed           | 1–100   |
| Aggression      | 0–100   |
| Fear            | 0–100   |
| Intelligence    | 0–100   |
| Loyalty         | 0–100   |
| Hunger          | 0–100   |
| Thirst          | 0–100   |
| Trust           | 0–100   |
| Detection       | 0–100   |
| Social behavior | 0–100   |


Additional species fields:


- Habitat.
- Diet.
- Activity cycle.
- Territory radius.
- Pack size.
- Breeding season.
- Gestation or incubation duration.
- Lifespan.
- Infected variants.
- Predators.
- Prey.
- Environmental requirements.


## 5.3 Taming rules


Animal taming should be a progression system.


| Stage    | Trust | Behavior                  |
| -------- | ----- | ------------------------- |
| Wild     | 0–19  | Avoids or attacks player  |
| Aware    | 20–39 | Recognizes player         |
| Familiar | 40–59 | Approaches cautiously     |
| Friendly | 60–74 | Accepts interaction       |
| Bonded   | 75–89 | Responds to commands      |
| Loyal    | 90–99 | Strong companion behavior |
| Devoted  | 100   | Maximum relationship      |


Taming should depend on:


- Species temperament.
- Player behavior.
- Food preferences.
- Time spent together.
- Environmental safety.
- Existing bonds.
- Threat exposure.


Not every animal should be tamable. Large predators and certain mutated creatures may only be temporarily calmed, trained through rare story events, or remain permanently wild.


## 5.4 Loyalty system


Loyalty should increase through care, successful commands, protection, and companionship.


Loyalty should decrease through neglect, abuse, abandonment, and repeated dangerous situations.


| Event              | Loyalty change |
| ------------------ | -------------- |
| Feed               | +2             |
| Play               | +2             |
| Successful command | +1             |
| Protect animal     | +5             |
| Rescue animal      | +10            |
| Neglect            | -2/day         |
| Abandon            | -15            |
| Harm animal        | -25            |
| Repeated betrayal  | -30            |


## 5.5 Animal commands


| Command  | Function                       |
| -------- | ------------------------------ |
| Follow   | Follow player                  |
| Stay     | Remain in place                |
| Guard    | Protect assigned area          |
| Attack   | Engage designated target       |
| Defend   | Protect player or companion    |
| Search   | Investigate nearby area        |
| Track    | Follow a scent trail           |
| Retrieve | Collect a designated item      |
| Hunt     | Search for prey                |
| Return   | Go to settlement               |
| Rest     | Enter recovery state           |
| Dismiss  | Return to independent behavior |


Command success depends on species intelligence, loyalty, training, distance, environmental danger, and competing instincts.


## 5.6 Breeding rules


Animal breeding requires:


- Compatible species.
- Compatible genetics.
- Appropriate age.
- Health.
- Adequate habitat.
- Appropriate environmental conditions.
- A successful breeding event.


Offspring inherit weighted parental traits, including speed, resilience, temperament, intelligence, appearance, and infection resistance.


Mutation inheritance should be rare and governed by the game's fictional infection genetics.


# 6. World-Building Data


The world needs to become a fully defined playable environment, not simply a collection of locations on a map.


Every region needs geography, coordinates, entrances, exits, points of interest, structures, inhabitants, resources, threats, environmental conditions, and simulation rules.


I recommend an interconnected world made up of 12 major regions, with smaller subregions and discoverable locations inside each.


## 6.1 World geography

| ------- | ------------------------ | ------ | ------------------------------ | --------------------------------- |

| REG-002 | Green Valley             | 2      | Farmland                       | Food, seeds, water                |
| REG-003 | Blackwood Forest         | 3      | Dense forest                   | Wood, wildlife, herbs             |

| REG-005 | Deadwater District       | 4      | Flooded urban area             | Salvage, chemicals                |

These are original proposed locations. Their geography and contents should be fixed in the world database, while their population, ownership, resources, and conditions evolve during play.


## 6.2 Regional data sheets


Each region needs its own configuration.


| Region                   | Climate    | Population | Faction presence         | Main threat        |
| ------------------------ | ---------- | ---------- | ------------------------ | ------------------ |
| Ashfall City             | Temperate  | 1,200      | Multiple                 | Urban hordes       |
| Green Valley             | Temperate  | 280        | Agricultural communities | Raiders            |
| Blackwood Forest         | Forest     | 80         | Independent survivors    | Predators          |
| Rustbelt Industrial Zone | Industrial | 150        | Iron Dominion            | Armored infected   |
| Deadwater District       | Wetland    | 60         | Smugglers                | Mutated wildlife   |
| Redstone Quarry          | Dry        | 110        | Mining faction           | Brutes             |
| Hollow Creek             | Temperate  | 220        | Human settlements        | Infected raids     |
| The Outskirts            | Temperate  | 400        | Mixed                    | Runners            |
| Iron Ridge               | Mountain   | 90         | Military remnants        | Hunters            |
| The Containment Zone     | Variable   | 40         | Research remnants        | Apex mutations     |
| Bonefield Wastes         | Extreme    | 20         | Undead factions          | Advanced mutations |
| New Eden Basin           | Temperate  | 500        | Hybrid communities       | Political conflict |


Population figures are illustrative starting world-state values. They should be adjustable and generated from the actual simulation.


## 6.3 Region coordinate system


Use a consistent coordinate system for the entire game.


Recommended:


- World coordinates: X, Y.
- Region identifiers: REG-001 through REG-012.
- Local coordinates: region-specific X and Y.
- Elevation: Z.
- Location identifier: unique POI ID.
- Navigation connections: explicit exits and entrances.


Example world map grid:

These coordinates establish a fictional navigation grid. They are not real-world coordinates.


## 6.4 Region connections


The world should not be a collection of isolated maps.


Each region needs connection records that define travel routes, requirements, dangers, and traversal methods.


| Connection | From             | To               | Travel type     | Danger |
| ---------- | ---------------- | ---------------- | --------------- | ------ |
| CON-001    | Outskirts        | Ashfall City     | Road            | 4      |
| CON-002    | Outskirts        | Blackwood Forest | Trail           | 2      |
| CON-003    | Outskirts        | Hollow Creek     | Road            | 2      |
| CON-004    | Ashfall City     | Rustbelt         | Highway         | 5      |
| CON-005    | Ashfall City     | Deadwater        | Bridge          | 4      |
| CON-006    | Blackwood Forest | Iron Ridge       | Mountain trail  | 3      |
| CON-007    | Hollow Creek     | Green Valley     | Rural road      | 2      |
| CON-008    | Green Valley     | New Eden         | Farm road       | 1      |
| CON-009    | Rustbelt         | Redstone Quarry  | Industrial road | 4      |
| CON-010    | Iron Ridge       | Containment Zone | Restricted road | 6      |
| CON-011    | Redstone Quarry  | Bonefield Wastes | Wasteland route | 6      |
| CON-012    | Deadwater        | Bonefield Wastes | Floodplain      | 6      |


Connections should have:


- Travel distance.
- Estimated travel time.
- Terrain.
- Route condition.
- Blockage status.
- Enemy activity.
- Vehicle accessibility.
- Faction control.
- Weather exposure.
- Discovery status.
- Alternate routes.


Routes should change over time through bridge destruction, road clearing, faction checkpoints, flooding, repairs, and new construction.


# 7. Points of Interest and Buildings


Every region needs meaningful locations that support gameplay, exploration, and simulation.


## 7.1 Points of interest catalog


| ID      | Location                   | Region           | Purpose                | Primary contents    |
| ------- | -------------------------- | ---------------- | ---------------------- | ------------------- |
| POI-001 | Central Hospital           | Ashfall City     | Medical exploration    | Medicine, infected  |
| POI-002 | Police Department          | Ashfall City     | Weapon discovery       | Weapons, ammunition |
| POI-003 | City Hall                  | Ashfall City     | Political history      | Records, documents  |
| POI-004 | Underground Station        | Ashfall City     | Travel and exploration | Tunnels, hordes     |
| POI-005 | Survivor Market            | Ashfall City     | Commerce               | Vendors             |
| POI-006 | Abandoned School           | Ashfall City     | Community exploration  | Supplies, survivors |
| POI-007 | Green Valley Farm          | Green Valley     | Agriculture            | Crops, tools        |
| POI-008 | Seed Research Center       | Green Valley     | Genetics               | Strain archive      |
| POI-009 | Timber Camp                | Blackwood        | Resource production    | Wood, tools         |
| POI-010 | Ranger Station             | Blackwood        | Wildlife research      | Animal records      |
| POI-011 | Steelworks                 | Rustbelt         | Industrial crafting    | Machinery           |
| POI-012 | Power Station              | Rustbelt         | Energy                 | Generators          |
| POI-013 | Flooded Mall               | Deadwater        | Salvage                | Consumer goods      |
| POI-014 | Water Treatment Plant      | Deadwater        | Water production       | Components          |
| POI-015 | Quarry Operations Center   | Redstone         | Mining                 | Equipment           |
| POI-016 | Military Bunker            | Iron Ridge       | Military exploration   | Advanced gear       |
| POI-017 | Containment Laboratory     | Containment Zone | Story and research     | Biological research |
| POI-018 | Underground Research Vault | Containment Zone | Endgame discovery      | Rare specimens      |
| POI-019 | Bonefield Nest             | Bonefield Wastes | Boss encounter         | Apex materials      |
| POI-020 | New Eden Settlement        | New Eden Basin   | Civilization hub       | Trade, governance   |


Each POI needs a complete record with:


- Unique ID.
- Name.
- Region ID.
- Coordinates.
- Building type.
- Floor count.
- Interior layout.
- Entrance coordinates.
- Exit coordinates.
- Loot tables.
- Enemy spawn tables.
- Animal presence.
- Environmental conditions.
- Ownership.
- Discovery status.
- Quest links.
- Persistent world-state changes.


## 7.2 Building categories


| Category       | Building types                                  |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| Residential    | Houses, apartments, shelters, cabins            |
| Commercial     | Shops, markets, warehouses, restaurants         |
| Medical        | Clinics, hospitals, pharmacies                  |
| Industrial     | Factories, workshops, power stations            |
| Agricultural   | Farms, greenhouses, barns, silos                |
| Military       | Bunkers, bases, checkpoints                     |
| Research       | Laboratories, containment facilities            |
| Infrastructure | Water plants, substations, radio towers         |
| Transportation | Garages, stations, tunnels, bridges             |
| Government     | City halls, courthouses, administrative centers |
| Recreational   | Parks, theaters, community centers              |
| Underground    | Bunkers, vaults, sewer networks, laboratories   |


## 7.3 Interior layouts


Buildings need actual room-level data.


A hospital, for example, could include:


| Floor    | Room             | Contents                | Threat     |
| -------- | ---------------- | ----------------------- | ---------- |
| Ground   | Reception        | Documents, supplies     | Low        |
| Ground   | Emergency room   | Medical loot            | High       |
| Ground   | Pharmacy         | Medicine                | Medium     |
| First    | Patient rooms    | Personal loot           | Medium     |
| First    | Intensive care   | Medical equipment       | High       |
| Second   | Research ward    | Records, samples        | Very high  |
| Basement | Storage          | Components              | Medium     |
| Basement | Containment room | Rare biological samples | Boss-level |


The same concept applies to every major building. The game should support procedurally varied interiors while maintaining authored landmarks and recognizable layouts.


## 7.4 Safehouses and settlements


Safehouses should be persistent locations with:


- Ownership.
- Security.
- Storage.
- Sleeping facilities.
- Medical facilities.
- Power.
- Water.
- Food.
- Defensive structures.
- Population.
- Visitor activity.
- Threat level.
- Maintenance requirements.


Safehouses can be abandoned, discovered, attacked, captured, upgraded, or converted into settlement hubs.


# 8. World Spawn, Loot, and Event Data


The world must generate content based on location, time, population, danger, and simulation state.


## 8.1 Spawn categories


| Spawn category    | Examples                    | Conditions                      |
| ----------------- | --------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| Ambient enemies   | Shamblers, crawlers         | Region danger                   |
| Predators         | Wolves, bears               | Habitat and time                |
| Infected wildlife | Infected dogs, mutated deer | Infection pressure              |
| Survivors         | Scavengers, traders         | Population and faction activity |
| Patrols           | Guards, raiders             | Territory control               |
| Hordes            | Mixed infected              | Noise and migration             |
| Boss encounters   | Alpha, apex                 | Story and world progression     |
| Traveling groups  | Merchants, refugees         | Road safety and migration       |


## 8.2 Loot placement rules


Loot should be contextual.


| Location         | Expected loot                     |
| ---------------- | --------------------------------- |
| Hospital         | Medicine, medical tools           |
| Police station   | Weapons, ammunition               |
| Grocery store    | Food, water                       |
| Hardware store   | Tools, materials                  |
| Factory          | Components, machinery             |
| Farm             | Food, seeds, agricultural tools   |
| Laboratory       | Research materials                |
| Military bunker  | Advanced equipment                |
| Residential home | Household goods                   |
| Garage           | Vehicle components                |
| Research vault   | Rare biological specimens         |
| Animal den       | Organic materials, animal remains |


Loot should have several persistence rules:


- Items can be depleted.
- Containers can be emptied by NPCs.
- Settlements can collect resources.
- Loot can respawn only where the world simulation permits it.
- Valuable discoveries should remain permanently recorded.
- Story-critical items should not disappear through ordinary loot refreshes.


## 8.3 Dynamic world events


| ID      | Event                     | Trigger                        | Consequence                 |
| ------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------ | --------------------------- |
| EVT-001 | Horde migration           | Noise concentration            | Regional danger rises       |
| EVT-002 | Settlement raid           | Hostile faction activity       | Infrastructure damage       |
| EVT-003 | Supply convoy             | Trade agreement                | Resource movement           |
| EVT-004 | Refugee movement          | Settlement collapse            | Population migration        |
| EVT-005 | Disease outbreak          | Contamination                  | Population losses           |
| EVT-006 | Power failure             | Infrastructure damage          | Production decreases        |
| EVT-007 | Water shortage            | Low water reserves             | Unrest increases            |
| EVT-008 | Harvest festival          | Successful agricultural season | Morale increases            |
| EVT-009 | Faction uprising          | Low political stability        | Government changes          |
| EVT-010 | Mutation outbreak         | Infection pressure             | New infected populations    |
| EVT-011 | Research discovery        | Scientific progress            | Technology unlocked         |
| EVT-012 | Territory takeover        | Faction conflict               | Ownership changes           |
| EVT-013 | Wildlife migration        | Ecological pressure            | Animal distribution changes |
| EVT-014 | Trade embargo             | Diplomatic conflict            | Market shortages            |
| EVT-015 | New settlement founded    | Population expansion           | New civilization node       |
| EVT-016 | Underground discovery     | Exploration event              | New route unlocked          |
| EVT-017 | Environmental disaster    | Weather or infrastructure      | Regional damage             |
| EVT-018 | Leadership succession     | Leader death or retirement     | Political transition        |
| EVT-019 | Undead diplomatic contact | Faction relationship           | New political possibilities |
| EVT-020 | Civilization breakthrough | Technology and social progress | Major historical event      |


Each event needs a probability, prerequisites, duration, affected entities, consequences, resolution conditions, and history record.


# 9. World Simulation and Population Systems


[Endzone: A World Apart Review - It is Chaos Out There! - PC Reviews](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/iEBl8nrWbBGL7Bpg1YyPbHlM3nzn0IrjEM4rHoOOMMOkOa5uyziu5Rtz_NStSDhygng-oARD37gE10VKcL_l0VGmZkI-D1r-j-pW3znOzwDiFjhmHTO5pdWYHijBnTugEF_hPETTgz_l3drwv4tKq9g90G12ZaIGgq9dTVtosUs?purpose=inline)


[Endzone 2 : le jeu de gestion survie post-apocalyptique en early access](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/4GTBOZt3cSDf9xrl6zMb6-Sfn15ooHd_f4ZRr2UNF7V4JcwfPWkTEqyVqRWoK0mmxXBALP3TrNTYr94nqWLQ4vmO1TIltEudVeguiJ2bb1XGJuU5mu_p1Zq2x-vDWbb4OZnoqbyfwjNojay074-2mX6hbTn41AViGQkdDRTxO6Q?purpose=inline)


12


This is where the game grows beyond an ordinary survival experience.


The world must continue developing even when the player is somewhere else.


A settlement should not freeze because the player left. A faction should not wait for the player to return before expanding. Animals should migrate, supply chains should change, and populations should grow or decline.


## 9.1 Population classifications


| Population group    | Description                               |
| ------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| Original survivors  | People who experienced the collapse       |
| Rebuilders          | People restoring society                  |
| Born After          | People born after the outbreak            |
| Changed             | Infected or transformed individuals       |
| Conscious undead    | Undead retaining independent intelligence |
| Hybrid population   | Human-undead communities                  |
| Refugees            | Displaced populations                     |
| Nomads              | Mobile communities                        |
| Raiders             | Hostile organized groups                  |
| Independent traders | Commercial traveling population           |
| Specialists         | Doctors, engineers, scientists, farmers   |
| Wildlife            | Natural and mutated animal populations    |


Each individual NPC should have:


- Unique ID.
- Name.
- Age.
- Species or biological state.
- Skills.
- Occupation.
- Faction.
- Settlement.
- Relationships.
- Health.
- Infection.
- Morale.
- Loyalty.
- Inventory.
- Needs.
- Current activity.
- Historical events.


Not every background NPC needs full simulation detail. Use aggregated population groups for distant regions and detailed individual simulation near the player.


## 9.2 Population growth


Population changes through:


- Births.
- Deaths.
- Recruitment.
- Immigration.
- Emigration.
- Infection.
- Transformation.
- Rescue.
- Captivity.
- Conflict.
- Disease.
- Resource availability.


A simplified population equation:


\\[ P\_{next}=P+B+I+R-D-E-M \\]


Where:


- \\(P\\) = current population.
- \\(B\\) = births.
- \\(I\\) = immigration.
- \\(R\\) = recruitment or rescued arrivals.
- \\(D\\) = deaths.
- \\(E\\) = emigration.
- \\(M\\) = people removed through other modeled population transitions.


The simulation should record transitions between human, infected, hybrid, and undead populations separately, rather than treating every transformation as a death.


## 9.3 Population needs


Each settlement should track:


| Need            | Importance | Failure consequence           |
| --------------- | ---------- | ----------------------------- |
| Food            | Critical   | Hunger and mortality          |
| Water           | Critical   | Dehydration and mortality     |
| Shelter         | High       | Exposure and illness          |
| Medicine        | High       | Reduced health recovery       |
| Security        | High       | Raids and unrest              |
| Employment      | Medium     | Reduced morale                |
| Social activity | Medium     | Lower cohesion                |
| Education       | Medium     | Reduced skill development     |
| Power           | Variable   | Infrastructure disruption     |
| Culture         | Medium     | Identity and cohesion decline |


## 9.4 Migration rules


Migration should be driven by push and pull factors.


Push factors:


- Famine.
- Water shortage.
- Disease.
- War.
- Unemployment.
- Political oppression.
- Infected population pressure.
- Infrastructure collapse.


Pull factors:


- Available food.
- Clean water.
- Security.
- Employment.
- Family connections.
- Political freedom.
- Medical facilities.
- Trade opportunities.


Example migration score:


\\[ M = 0.25S + 0.20E + 0.20F + 0.15W + 0.10T + 0.10R \\]


Where:


- \\(S\\) = security attractiveness.
- \\(E\\) = employment availability.
- \\(F\\) = food security.
- \\(W\\) = water security.
- \\(T\\) = trade access.
- \\(R\\) = relationship and family attraction.


Migration should be calculated alongside the destination's capacity, route danger, and faction policies.


## 9.5 Faction growth


Factions need their own simulation statistics.


| Statistic            | Range |
| -------------------- | ----- |
| Population           | 1+    |
| Territory count      | 0+    |
| Military strength    | 0–100 |
| Economic strength    | 0–100 |
| Political stability  | 0–100 |
| Technology           | 0–100 |
| Public support       | 0–100 |
| Diplomatic influence | 0–100 |
| Infrastructure       | 0–100 |
| Cultural cohesion    | 0–100 |


Faction growth should depend on:


- Population growth.
- Recruitment.
- Economic production.
- Territory resources.
- Military strength.
- Political stability.
- Diplomatic alliances.
- Technology.
- Leadership quality.
- Public support.


## 9.6 Faction expansion and territory takeover


Territory takeover should be a multi-stage process.


1. Reconnaissance


   Faction scouts identify weak territory, resources, population, and defenses.
2. Influence


   The faction establishes trade, political relationships, propaganda, or local alliances.
3. Pressure


   The faction applies economic, diplomatic, or military pressure.
4. Conflict


   Armed conflict, negotiations, internal uprising, or voluntary surrender may occur.
5. Control transfer


   Ownership changes when the takeover conditions are satisfied.
6. Occupation and rebuilding


   New laws, taxes, patrols, supply systems, and infrastructure policies take effect.
7. Integration or resistance


   The population accepts, resists, revolts, migrates, or forms an underground movement.


### Territory control calculation


A proposed control score:


\\[ T = 0.25M + 0.20E + 0.15P + 0.15D + 0.15L + 0.10S \\]


Where:


- \\(M\\) = military power.
- \\(E\\) = economic influence.
- \\(P\\) = population support.
- \\(D\\) = diplomatic influence.
- \\(L\\) = local infrastructure.
- \\(S\\) = strategic advantage.


The control score should be compared with competing faction influence, not treated as an automatic conquest button.


## 9.7 Civilization simulation ticks


Use multiple simulation speeds.


| Simulation layer     | Update frequency   | Detail                            |
| -------------------- | ------------------ | --------------------------------- |
| Active player area   | 10–60 times/sec    | Full gameplay                     |
| Nearby regional area | Every 1–10 seconds | Detailed NPC and enemy activity   |
| Regional world       | Every 1–5 minutes  | Settlements and factions          |
| Distant world        | Every 1–6 hours    | Aggregated population and economy |
| Historical world     | Event-driven       | Permanent records                 |


The simulation must use elapsed world time and deterministic rules so that saving, loading, and offline progression do not produce inconsistent results.


## 9.8 World history and persistent consequences


The world should maintain a historical record of significant events.


Example:


## World history archive


Illustrative simulation history


1. Year 0: The Outbreak


   The first infection wave devastates major population centers.
2. Year 2: Fall of Ashfall


   The city's surviving government loses control of its central district.
3. Year 5: Green Valley Compact


   Three agricultural settlements establish a trade agreement.
4. Year 8: First Hybrid Settlement


   Conscious undead and humans establish a shared community.
5. Year 12: Iron Dominion Expansion


   Industrial factions seize several surrounding territories.
6. Year 16: The New Eden Accord


   Several settlements establish a regional political federation.
7. Year 20: Age of Evolution


   The world enters a new era of human, undead, and hybrid civilization.


These events should be possible outcomes, not mandatory scripted history. Player decisions and simulation conditions should create alternative histories.


# 10. The underlying content database


The content above should not be stored as unrelated hardcoded arrays across the game.


It needs a consistent data architecture.


## 10.1 Recommended database structure


```
game-data/
│
├── blood/
│   ├── sources.json
│   ├── samples.json
│   ├── recipes.json
│   ├── effects.json
│   ├── risks.json
│   └── mutation-links.json
│
├── cannabis/
│   ├── strains.json
│   ├── genetics.json
│   ├── growth-stages.json
│   ├── plant-conditions.json
│   ├── processing.json
│   └── products.json
│
├── crafting/
│   ├── materials.json
│   ├── components.json
│   ├── weapons.json
│   ├── clothing.json
│   ├── tools.json
│   ├── structures.json
│   └── recipes.json
│
├── creatures/
│   ├── enemies.json
│   ├── enemy-behaviors.json
│   ├── animations.json
│   ├── loot-tables.json
│   ├── spawn-tables.json
│   └── bosses.json
│
├── wildlife/
│   ├── species.json
│   ├── habitats.json
│   ├── taming.json
│   ├── commands.json
│   ├── loyalty.json
│   └── breeding.json
│
├── world/
│   ├── regions.json
│   ├── coordinates.json
│   ├── connections.json
│   ├── poi.json
│   ├── buildings.json
│   ├── interiors.json
│   ├── maps.json
│   ├── loot-zones.json
│   ├── spawn-zones.json
│   └── world-events.json
│
└── civilization/
    ├── populations.json
    ├── settlements.json
    ├── factions.json
    ├── migration.json
    ├── territory.json
    ├── diplomacy.json
    ├── economy.json
    ├── production.json
    └── history.json
```


## 10.2 Every content record needs a standard schema


For example, every weapon should have:


```
{
  "id": "WPN-001",
  "name": "Improvised Club",
  "category": "melee",
  "rarity": "common",
  "damage": 18,
  "attackSpeed": 1.0,
  "staminaCost": 8,
  "range": 1.5,
  "durability": 45,
  "weight": 2.5,
  "value": 8,
  "armorPenetration": 0,
  "criticalChanceBonus": 0,
  "noise": 15,
  "craftable": true,
  "recipeId": "REC-WPN-001",
  "unlockLevel": 1,
  "tags": ["improvised", "melee"]
}
```


The same principle should apply to blood samples, strains, recipes, enemies, animals, structures, locations, settlements, and factions.


## 10.3 Data validation


Every database should be checked for:


- Duplicate IDs.
- Missing references.
- Invalid numerical ranges.
- Missing required fields.
- Invalid recipe inputs.
- Missing item outputs.
- Invalid region connections.
- Unreachable points of interest.
- Missing spawn definitions.
- Missing loot tables.
- Invalid animal species references.
- Missing animation states.
- Impossible population values.
- Invalid faction ownership.
- Circular or broken world connections.


A content validator should run automatically during development and before every production build.

Here is the content completion checklist.


Content production tracker

Reset checklist


## 12. The final design objective


The real ambition here is to make every content category feed another category.


- Blood samples unlock biological research.
- Biological research unlocks mutations.
- Mutations change character capabilities and social identity.
- Cannabis genetics create agricultural products and new commercial opportunities.
- Crafting supports settlement development.
- Animals provide companionship, resources, and ecological activity.
- Enemies affect regional security and population movement.
- Regions create resource dependencies.
- Resource dependencies create trade relationships.
- Trade relationships influence political alliances.
- Factions compete for territory.
- Territory ownership changes the world.
- The world records its history and continues evolving.


Phoenix Blaze should have two simultaneous games running inside it:


1. The personal survival RPG, where the player fights, crafts, explores, transforms, builds relationships, and decides what kind of being to become.
2. The civilization simulator, where thousands of lives, settlements, economies, factions, ecosystems, and political systems continue developing around that player.


That is the difference between a zombie survival game with a big map and a persistent post-apocalyptic world with its own history, economy, ecology, and evolving civilizations.


The ultimate question is no longer simply whether the player survives. It is what kind of world survives with them.




:::writing{variant="document" id="63821"}

⚙️ 2. BLOODLUST + CANNABIS SYSTEM
Here’s the core gameplay effect logic:
🎮 HUD:
🩸 Bloodlust Meter (fills over time)
🪴 Weed Gauge (affects sanity, health, and bloodlust)
♻️ Decay Meter (long-term wear-down unless weed is used)


[Combat Buffs from Weed Use]


🧠 Pseudo Code Example (Godot-style GDScript):
var bloodlust = 0
var decay = 0
var highness = 0
var max_high = 100
var is_high = false


func _process(delta):
    # Bloodlust rises slowly
    bloodlust += delta * 0.25
    decay += delta * 0.05


    # Highness decays over time
    if highness > 0:
        highness -= delta * 1
        is_high = true
    else:
        is_high = false


    # Weed effect on bloodlust
    if is_high:
        bloodlust -= delta * 0.75  # slows down bloodlust


func smoke_weed(strain_strength):
    highness = clamp(highness + strain_strength, 0, max_high)
    decay -= strain_strength * 0.5  # slow the rot too


🎯 Weed Strain Effects (Common Ones):
Strain Name
Effect
Brain Buster
Short-term +Speed, +Vision
Rot-Stopper
Slows decay dramatically
Stealth Kush
Reduces enemy detection radius
Chronic Chill
Lowers bloodlust, raises sanity
Munchy Meltdown
Heals minor damage, boosts stamina

🕵️‍♀️ 6. STEALTH & DISGUISE MECHANIC
Stealth is 🔑. Blend in with both humans and zombies.
🧠 Core Mechanics:
Blood Camouflage: Rub human/zombie blood on self


Rotten Meat Cloak: Temporarily masks your scent


Clothing: Clean human clothes = less detection by survivors


Weed Use: “Stealth Kush” makes you harder to detect for 30 secs




🎮 Stealth System Flow:
var disguised = false
var stealth_timer = 0
var visibility_level = 100  # 0 = invisible, 100 = fully visible


func apply_blood_camouflage():
    disguised = true
    stealth_timer = 30
    visibility_level -= 50


func _process(delta):
    if disguised:
        stealth_timer -= delta
        visibility_level += delta * 2
        if stealth_timer <= 0:
            disguised = false
            visibility_level = 100


🔊 Alerts:
Zombies sniffing you out? → Use rotten blood disguise


Humans nearby with flashlights? → Hide in dumpsters, shadows, or pose like a corpse


Too close? → Slow-mo panic heartbeat, breath sounds, stoner inner voice goes:




 “Bro... stay still. You’re meat in a meat suit.”
